# Autodesk Fusion 360 `.f3d`: Format Specification

> **License:** This document is released under [CC-BY-4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). Attribute to the cadmpeg project.

---

Record offsets, field widths, and endianness are also maintained as a machine-checked table in [`docs/layouts/f3d.md`](../layouts/f3d.md), generated from `docs/layouts/f3d.toml`. That table is the canonical source for the numbers; the prose below carries the semantics. `cargo test -p cadmpeg --test layout_tables` proves the two agree.

## 1. Container layer

`.f3d` is a **ZIP archive**. Entries may be stored, DEFLATE-compressed, or compressed with **zstd (ZIP method 93)**.

### 1.1 Payload families

| Path pattern                                                                     | Role                                                                                                                                                                  |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `<folder>/Breps.BlobParts/*.smb`, `*.smbh`                                       | ASM/ACIS exact B-rep streams, binary encoding (§2)                                                                                                                    |
| `<folder>/Breps.BlobParts/*.sat`, `*.smt`                                        | ASM/ACIS exact B-rep streams, text encoding (§1.1.3)                                                                                                                  |
| `<folder>/ProteinAssets.BlobParts/*.protein`                                     | nested ZIP archives with appearance/material assets                                                                                                                   |
| `<folder>/Design1/BulkStream.dat` (or `FusionDesignSegmentType1/BulkStream.dat`) | design recipes, material assignments, body map                                                                                                                        |
| `<folder>/*/MetaStream.dat`                                                      | per-segment object tables (GUID → entity-type registry)                                                                                                               |
| `<folder>/FusionACTSegmentType1/BulkStream.dat`                                  | Active Component Tree entity/appearance tables                                                                                                                        |
| `<folder>/FusionBrowserSegmentType1/BulkStream.dat`                              | Fusion UI browser tree                                                                                                                                                |
| `<folder>/Previews/*`, `<folder>/Images.BlobParts/*`                             | thumbnails / appearance images; never geometry                                                                                                                        |
| `<folder>/ParaMeshGeometry.BlobParts/*.paramesh`                                 | mesh-body geometry containers (§1.1.2)                                                                                                                                |
| `<folder>/OGS.BlobFolder/OGS/<scene>/*`                                          | display scene graph (`world`) and its vertex and index buffer arenas (`stream_mesh_NNN`, `Fusion_mesh_NNN`); each drawable node carries the design entity ID it draws |
| `<folder>/TSplines.BlobParts/*.tsm`                                              | T-spline control-cage source of Form bodies; the exact face geometry is the B-rep stream's `t_spl_sur` carriers                                                       |
| `Manifest.dat` (top-level and per-asset)                                         | document, asset, and segment registry (see §1.3)                                                                                                                      |
| `RedirectionsStream.dat` (top-level)                                             | external-reference table (see §1.4)                                                                                                                                   |
| `ComponentReferenceData.json` (top-level)                                        | component-reference slot (see §1.4)                                                                                                                                   |

`<folder>` is an asset-folder path component.

In an OGS `world` scene graph, each drawable node has zero or more attributes. An attribute is a length-prefixed name, a five-byte prologue, and its payload. A `VisibilityAttribute` payload is one byte with value `0` or `1`. On sketch-curve nodes, this value is drawable render-cache state. It does not give the display visibility of the owning Sketch. Sketch display visibility is in the typed Design sketch-container member (§3.1).

### 1.1.1 Form control cages

A `TSplines.BlobParts/*.tsm` entry is a UTF-8, line-oriented `TS0200` control-cage program. The first line is `#TS0200`. Its fixed declarations are `degree 3`, `cap-type`, `star-smoothness`, `units`, `end-conditions`, and `star-knot-rule`. Geometry coordinates are centimetres irrespective of the declarative `units 1 meters` line.

Topology uses zero-based indices in record order. An `f root flags` record defines a face and one half-edge in its boundary. An `e root scalar` record defines an edge and one of its two half-edges; the scalar is a finite f64 and is nominally one. A `v root direction` record defines a control vertex. An `l next previous mate vertex face edge flags` record defines a half-edge: `next` and `previous` are reciprocal within a face ring, `mate` is reciprocal across an edge, `vertex` is the half-edge's terminal control vertex, `face` is the owning face index or `-1` on a boundary mate, and `edge` is the index of the owning edge. An `e` root and its mate carry the same `edge` value, and that value is the `e` record's own index. Starting at an `f` root and following `next` yields that face's directed edge ring. For an `e` root, the directed edge runs from the mate's terminal vertex to the root's terminal vertex. Every half-edge belongs to exactly one `e` root pair. An `ec edge flags` record marks the indexed edge as a crease. Vertex subdivision tags follow crease incidence: zero crease edges is smooth, one is dart, two is crease, and three or more is corner.

A bare `f`, `e`, `v`, `l`, or `0g` token with no operands is a deleted slot, not a malformed line. It consumes an index in its record order and defines no element, so every index in the program counts bare and populated records alike; half-edge reciprocity closes only under that rule, and no half-edge names a deleted `l` slot. A deleted slot has no counterpart in the Design segment's per-element records, whose counts equal the populated token counts alone.

Each `0g x y z weight` record defines a rational grip point. Without an explicit grip map, the grip records correspond positionally to the `v` records. An explicit map starts with `0m odd-grip-map`; each following `0m gvp vertex` or `0m gv vertex` marker corresponds to the next `0g` record. A `gvp` marker assigns that point to the indexed topology vertex, while `gv` marks a secondary grip. Every topology vertex has exactly one primary grip. The map has one marker per `0g` slot, counting deleted slots, and every marker precedes the first `0g` record. A deleted `0g` slot's marker is `0m gv -1`: the operand is signed, and `-1` is the only negative value and marks exactly the deleted slots.

A `0m cg vertex wedges S G` record describes derived grip connectivity for one vertex and does not replace the primary topology map. `wedges` is the number of wedges around `vertex`; `S` is that many spoke lengths; and `G` is a grip-index run whose length is not written. It is `sum(S[k] + S[k] * S[(k + 1) mod wedges])` over the wedges, which fixes the record's arity at `2 + wedges + len(G)`. A nonnegative entry of `G` indexes the `0g` grip array and names a secondary grip of the same vertex the record names; `-1` marks an absent grip. The `0m odd-grip-map` header precedes every `0m cg` record, and every grip-to-vertex marker precedes the first of them.

The terminal `tol` and `geom-tol` values are centimetres. `ver`, `behavior-version`, and `compat-version` identify the generating T-spline implementation. `100edges`, `100verts`, and `50000grip` records retain editor selections and do not change the committed cage topology.

A `105sym 0` record starts one cage-symmetry block. The following `105plane` record has twelve finite f64 coefficients. Six `105a` correspondence records use the selectors `fr`, `er`, `vr`, `f`, `e`, and `v`. The remaining operands are index pairs. The `fr`, `er`, and `vr` maps name the forward face, edge, and vertex correspondences; `f`, `e`, and `v` contain their reverse correspondences. Every non-fixed pair occurs in both directions, and a fixed element occurs only as a self-pair in the forward map. All indices address populated slots in the corresponding topology-record domain. Symmetry correspondence describes editor state over the complete cage and does not add or remove neutral control-cage elements.

A `105sym 1` record starts a radial-symmetry editor block. Its `105plane` record has twelve finite f64 coefficients. Its `105r segments` record has a positive integer, its `105r sweep` record has a finite f64, and its `105r ef`, `er`, `ff`, `fr`, `vf`, and `vr` records contain nonnegative index pairs. The radial-symmetry records describe editor state over the complete cage and do not add or remove neutral control-cage elements.

A Form scope owns an indexed cage-list record. After its indexed-record header, that record stores six zero bytes, `u8 1`, the owning scope's u64 record index, two zero bytes, a u32 cage count, and that many entries of `u8 1 + u64 cage-object reference + two zero bytes`. Its result-body bindings identify the B-rep bodies committed by the operation.

A class-325 Form cage table is 1850 bytes from its indexed header to its class-258 paired header and carries 32 entries. It stores nine zero bytes at offset 11, `u8 1` at offset 20, five zero bytes, a marked u64 result-record reference at offset 26, two zero bytes, and the u32 cage count at offset 37. The result-record reference names a class-407 record in the Form scope's reference members. The entries start at offset 41 and repeat every 30 bytes. Each class-325 Form cage entry is 30 bytes. Each entry stores a marked u64 class-289 cage-object reference, `u16 0`, a u64 type discriminator, a marked u64 class-273 companion reference, and `u16 0`. The 32 type discriminators are the values 307 through 338, each exactly once. The class-289 record is paired with class 258 and contains one marked u32 reference to a class-310 record. The frame's fixed tail follows the 32-entry run; the class-325 frame length is 890 plus 30 times its cage count.

A compact one-cage cage-list record is 100 bytes from its indexed header to its same-index paired header. Its dynamic class tags do not select the form. Ten zero bytes follow the indexed header, then `u8 1`, the owning Form scope's u64 record index, two zero bytes, and a u32 cage count of one. The sole member is `u8 1`, a u64 cage-object record index, `u16 0`, and `u16 0x00fc`. The compact form has no serializer-name record. When the document has one Form scope and one T-spline entry, those two counts establish the unique cage binding.

A legacy one-cage owner record is 81 bytes from its indexed header to its same-index paired header. The admitted owner/paired/nested class triples are `335/262/328`, `395/264/329`, `448/258/276`, and `295/258/274`. The owner frame stores fourteen zero bytes, a marked owning-scope reference, twenty-four zero bytes, a marked nested cage-object reference, three zero bytes, a repeated marked owning-scope reference, and two zero bytes. The nested class identifies one cage-object wrapper; the paired record is generation-specific companion data.

Each cage object reaches its surface through three indexed records. Its 200-byte class-301 record stores a marked u64 reference at offset 189 to a 33-byte class-373 wrapper. That wrapper stores ten zero bytes and a marked u64 reference at offset 21 to a 29-byte class-362 wrapper. The class-362 wrapper stores ten zero bytes and an unmarked u64 reference at offset 21 to a 665-byte class-457 record paired with class 264. The class-457 record stores the owning Form scope as a marked u64 reference at offset 317 and the cage surface as a marked u64 reference at offset 339.

A 132-byte Form serializer record stores ten zero bytes after its indexed header, the LP-UTF16 blob-part entry name `TSpline.<uuid>.tsm` at offset 21, and a marked u64 reference to the cage surface immediately after the name. Classes `315`, `349`, `360`, `431`, and `446` use the ordinary paired-header form. A class-335 serializer uses the same 132-byte primary frame followed by a class-331 header with a different record index; its fixed tail after the marked surface reference is zero, and its surface reference resolves to class `358`. The class-335 serializer is distinct from the 81-byte class-335 legacy one-cage owner. That entry name is the cage-to-archive join: every serializer name that has an active `TSplines.BlobParts` entry joins exactly one such entry. A serializer record can remain for a stale entry with no active blob part; that record does not add a neutral cage. The join is explicit, so it does not depend on archive order or on counting, and it divides the cages between two or more Form scopes. Each cage object's back reference agrees with the owning Form's cage list. A Form scope owns zero or more cages, and a document holding cages need not hold a Form scope; neither case changes the entry-name join. The `.tsm` program itself carries no identity and cannot be matched on content.

A class-328 Form scope is 940 bytes from its primary indexed header to its class-267 paired header. The class-328 primary indexed header starts a scope whose reference table has exactly two ordered members: one class-417/267 cage group and one class-341/267 cage metadata group. The scope payload and fixed history/kind tail use the Design feature-scope grammar. All offsets are relative to the class-328 primary indexed header.

A class-417 cage group owned by a class-328 Form scope is 136 bytes from its primary indexed header to its class-267 paired header. The class-417 primary indexed header is followed by fourteen zero bytes, a marked owning class-328 scope reference, and the owning class-328 scope record index. All offsets are relative to the class-417 primary indexed header. It has fourteen zero bytes before the member count. The member count is four class-350 member references and the group has four entries of `u8 1 + u64 record index + u16 0`, repeated every 11 bytes; each entry names a class-350 primary record paired with class 351. A fixed u32 value 1 follows the member run. The tail has a marked class-272 tail reference and its class-272 tail record index, four zero bytes, a marked class-404 tail reference and its class-404 tail record index, three zero bytes, and a marked repeated class-328 scope reference carrying the repeated owning class-328 scope record index. The two terminal bytes are zero.

A class-328 Form reference entry is 11 bytes. It contains a marked record reference, its referenced record index, and two zero bytes. The same entry form is repeated in the class-417 cage run and the class-341 metadata run.

A class-341 cage metadata group owned by a class-328 Form scope is 293 bytes from its primary indexed header to its class-267 paired header. The class-341 primary indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes, a marked owning class-328 scope reference, and the owning class-328 scope record index. All offsets are relative to the class-341 primary indexed header. Its fixed u32 member count is 19. The group has nineteen class-320 member references, encoded as nineteen entries of `u8 1 + u64 record index + u16 0`, repeated every 11 bytes. Its tail stores fixed u32 value 61, a finite little-endian f64, a marked class-259 tail reference with a class-259 tail record index, four zero bytes, a marked class-404 tail reference with a class-404 tail record index, three zero bytes, and a marked repeated class-328 scope reference carrying the repeated owning class-328 scope record index. The two terminal bytes are zero.

A class-350 member's class-351 paired header is preceded by a 13-byte owner backlink tail. The tail contains a marked class-417 group reference, its owning class-417 group record index, three zero bytes before the paired header, and a final paired marker `u8 1`. The class-320 metadata records are single indexed records and retain their class tag as the metadata-member discriminator.

### 1.1.2 Mesh geometry containers

A `<folder>/ParaMeshGeometry.BlobParts/ParaMeshGeometry.<uuid>.paramesh` entry holds one mesh body's geometry. A document whose bodies are all mesh bodies carries an empty `Breps.BlobParts/` directory entry and no BREP stream: the mesh containers hold the model geometry, and three Design-segment records join each container to its body (§3.1).

The container layout is:

```
0x00  89 55 44 50 4D 45 53 48 0D 0A 1A 0A   magic
0x0C  u32  2                                container version
0x10  32 zero bytes                         reserved
0x30  u64  protobuf byte count
0x38  u32  1
0x3C  protobuf message
then chunks to the end of the entry:
      u64  body byte count
      u32  chunk kind                       3 name table, 4 stream data
      body
```

The protobuf message registers the container's streams and resource GUIDs and carries a `fusion_uuid` key whose ASCII GUID equals the GUID stored by the container's Design-segment GUID record (§3.1). That GUID is independent of the `<uuid>` in the entry name.

A kind-3 body is a MessagePack map from stream name to integer stream id. A kind-4 body is a u16 descriptor byte count, a MessagePack descriptor map of that many bytes, a u32 uncompressed byte count, and a two-byte stream encoding. Encoding `5D 14` is followed by a raw LZMA1 stream with `lc` 3, `lp` 0, `pb` 2, and a 1 MiB dictionary; it decompresses to exactly the declared count. Encoding `5D FE` is followed directly by that many uncompressed bytes. The kind-4 chunks follow the name table in ascending stream-id order.

The version-2 stream-descriptor grammar contains exactly six maps: `{T: 0}`, `{T: 1, d: 1}`, `{D: 2, T: 3}`, `{D: 3, T: 3}`, `{D: 4, T: 3}`, and `{D: 3, T: 3, U: true}`. Map-entry order has no meaning. `D` is the logical component count. `T: 0` stores bytes, `T: 1` stores 32-bit terminal-delta words, and `T: 3` stores little-endian f32 components. Other `T` values and other key/value combinations are invalid in version 2. A stream whose descriptor omits `U` decompresses to the element count times the component count times the component width. Descriptor `{D: 3, T: 3, U: true}` stores each logical three-component direction as two f32 octahedral coordinates.

Stream names are container-local slots. Registry field 21 names the vertex-position stream, and field 22 names the triangle-corner stream. Their values are UTF-8 stream names. Fields 21 and 22 assign these roles; the literal names `v` and `t` do not. The registry binds every other stream name to its channel or property.

Registry field 9 is the number of face-group records in the role-0 code-7 channel. Registry field 12 is one lowercase ASCII hyphenated version-4 UUID. It identifies the mesh container locally and is independent of the entry-name UUID, `fusion_uuid`, channel-resource GUIDs, and face-group GUIDs. The `fusion_uuid` property remains the Design-body join identity.

The position stream holds one f32 coordinate triple per vertex. Those coordinates are not model centimetres: the mesh-body Design record (§3.1) stores the affine map from container coordinates to model space. Its linear part `A` maps an oriented normal with the cofactor matrix `det(A) A^-T`; the result is normalized. The determinant sign keeps the normal aligned with the stored triangle tuple under a reflected placement. In the corner stream, the first corner is implicit. The values before the last are two's-complement differences from one corner to the next. The implicit corner is the unique starting index that keeps the complete decoded sequence inside the vertex-index domain; the last stored value does not continue the sequence.

#### Attribute channels

A `Base Mesh Feature` scope is an indexed Design operation. Its owning entity reference identifies the feature object. The mesh-body record carries a marked reference to the scope's record index; that reference is the operation-to-tessellation join. The mesh-body record also references the Design GUID record whose ASCII identity equals the container's `fusion_uuid`, joins its `.paramesh` entry, and stores the affine map that projects its vertices into model millimetres. The neutral feature definition is `MeshImport` and stores the resulting tessellation identities in reference order. A mesh import owns tessellation geometry and has no BREP `Body`; the decoder does not fabricate one.

Registry field 4 declares one channel whose values address vertices or triangle corners, and field 5 declares one channel whose values address triangles. A channel entry holds an optional role in field 2, an optional resource GUID in field 3, one stream entry in field 5, and zero or more face-group records in field 6. The stream entry holds the element code in field 1, the value-stream name in field 2, and the optional index-stream name in field 3. A field-6 face-group record holds its u32 key in field 1 and its GUID in field 2. Channel resource GUIDs are unique within the registry. Face-group keys and GUIDs are unique within their channel.

The element code selects the stored element:

| Code | Element                                                                      |
| ---- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 2    | two f32 components                                                           |
| 4    | four f32 components: red, green, blue, and alpha, in the authored sRGB scale |
| 5    | a unit direction encoded as two f32 octahedral coordinates                   |
| 7    | one terminal-delta u32 value per triangle                                    |

A field-4 channel that declares no index stream stores exactly one value per vertex, so the value stream holds the vertex count times the element width. A field-4 channel that declares an index stream stores one default value for each vertex, followed by one override value for each indexed corner position. If the value table has `E` elements and the mesh has `V` vertices, the index stream has `E - V` signed i32 values. The first `E - V - 1` values are two's-complement differences; the final value is the absolute terminal corner position. The first decoded position is the terminal minus the sum of the differences. Each next position adds the next difference. Positions are strictly increasing and address the triangle-corner sequence. A corner at a decoded position uses the corresponding override value. Every other corner uses the default value for its referenced vertex. Role 0 with code 5 supplies the corner-normal table. Roles 3 and 4 mark the texture-coordinate and colour channels.

A code-7 value stream has one 32-bit word per triangle. Every word before the last is a signed two's-complement difference from the current value to the next value. The final word is the absolute u32 value of the last triangle. The first value is the final value minus the sum of the differences. Each subsequent value adds the corresponding difference.

The role-0 code-7 channel is the face-group partition. It has no field-3 resource GUID. Its repeated field-6 records define the group keys in serialized order, and registry field 9 equals the number of those records. Each decoded triangle value selects one field-6 key. Every group has at least one triangle, and the records partition all triangle ordinals.

The role-1 code-7 channel named `tid` by the attribute-name stream assigns textures. It has one field-3 resource GUID and no field-6 records. Value `0` leaves the triangle untextured. Value `n`, for `n > 0`, selects the `n`th resource in the owning Design texture table's flags-map order (§3.1). The index is one-based and must not exceed the texture-resource count.

For a code-5 element `(u,v)`, both values are finite and in `[-1,1]`. Set `x = u`, `y = v`, and `z = 1 - |u| - |v|`. If `z < 0`, replace `x` with `(1 - |y|) sign(x)` and replace `y` with `(1 - |x_old|) sign(y)`, where `sign(0) = 1`. Normalize `(x,y,z)` to obtain the unit normal. To encode a unit normal `(nx,ny,nz)`, first divide `(nx,ny)` by `|nx| + |ny| + |nz|`. If `nz < 0`, apply the same fold to those two quotients. The resulting pair is `(u,v)`.

Registry field 7 contains one nested field 4 that names a `{T: 1, d: 1}` stream. Every value before the final value is a two's-complement difference. The final u32 is the absolute final endpoint. The first endpoint is the final endpoint minus the sum of the differences, and each next endpoint adds the next difference. Pair the resulting endpoints in order. Each pair is `(low_vertex_index, high_vertex_index)`, with `low_vertex_index < high_vertex_index`. Pairs are unique and each pair is an edge of at least one stored triangle. They classify source feature edges; ordinary triangulation edges are absent unless the source classifies them as features. An empty endpoint stream classifies no feature edges.

A registry field-8 entry pairs a property key in field 1 with either a text value in field 3 or a stream name in field 4. Key `fusion_uuid` carries the ASCII GUID the container's Design-segment GUID record repeats. Key `attname.amt.autodesk` names a `{T: 0}` UTF-8 stream. That stream starts with `<?xml version="1.0"?>` and then concatenates one or more sibling `<Attrib>` fragments. Each fragment contains exactly one `TriName` followed by one `AmtName`. `AmtName` is the authored attribute name. `TriName` is a channel-kind prefix followed immediately by the channel resource GUID: `color_tt` identifies a role-4 code-4 colour channel, `tco_tt` identifies a role-3 code-2 texture-coordinate channel, and `grp_tt` identifies a role-1 code-7 triangle channel. A `grp_tt` fragment whose `AmtName` is `tid` has the texture-selector semantics above.

### 1.1.3 Text encoding of a B-rep stream

A `Breps.BlobParts` member with the `.sat` or `.smt` extension carries the same entity model as a binary `.smb` or `.smbh` stream (§2) in the line-oriented ASCII text encoding. The header lines, record grammar, indexing, terminator, and unit rule are in [`asm.md` §7](asm.md#7-text-encoding-satsmt). The two encodings are alternatives, not layers: a document's geometry is in one of them.

### 1.2 Stored property and configuration entries

The following small entries are STORED:

| Entry                                           | Bytes                         | Meaning                                                                              |
| ----------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `Properties.dat`                                | u32 byte count + JSON payload | empty document-properties slot (u32 `0`), or a `docstruct` document-type declaration |
| `.../DesignConfigurationTable.<uuid>.dsgcfg`    | JSON object                   | configuration table, including parameter and suppression overrides                   |
| `.../DesignConfigurationRule.<uuid>.dsgcfgrule` | JSON object                   | configuration activation rules                                                       |

`Properties.dat` is a u32 payload byte count followed by that many JSON bytes; count 0 (`00 00 00 00`) is the empty slot. A non-empty payload is a JSON object whose `docstruct` member declares the document type: `version` (string), `type` (`assembly-design` or `part-design`), `subtype` (`assembly-standard`, `part-standard`, `part-sheetmetal`), and an `attributes` object. An `assembly-design` document carries no B-rep streams; its model is the placement of its external-reference targets (§1.4).

Configuration tables and rules are complete JSON objects. A table's `configurations` member is an object keyed by variant name. The serialized member order of that object is the authored table-row order; variant names are unique within the object. Each variant value is an object; its `parameters` member is an object, `suppressed` is an array of strings, and `material` is a string. The table's `active` string equals one key in `configurations`. A rule's paired `when` and `activate` members are strings containing its activation condition and target variant name. A rule object can be empty; an empty object has no members and activates nothing. Unknown object members remain part of the configuration document. ZIP entry name and extension select table versus rule; duplicate entry names are invalid. One nonempty table supplies the document's configuration list. Independent nonempty tables do not encode a shared row order.

Each table variant has a stable neutral identity formed from the complete table-entry name and the variant name as two byte-length-prefixed UTF-8 strings. Table order and variants in other tables do not affect that identity. Its zero-based neutral ordinal is its serialized position in the `configurations` object. The table's `active` member selects the active neutral variant. A parameter override names the unique parameter with the same source name and retains its JSON scalar text as the configuration-specific source expression. A suppressed-feature name identifies the unique feature with the same source name. A parameter or feature name with zero or multiple matching entities remains a configuration-local property. Material names transfer as material overrides. A paired rule transfers its `when` condition to the named variant when exactly one variant across all tables has its `activate` name; the property identity includes the complete rule-entry name. Native tables and rules remain the full-fidelity source for unrecognized members.

### 1.3 `Manifest.dat` grammar

Both manifests use `u32`-length-prefixed strings. An ASCII field stores a byte count followed by that many bytes. A UTF-16LE field stores a code-unit count followed by twice that many bytes.

The **top-level manifest** starts with LP-ASCII `3-2-0-0`, LP-ASCII `FusionDocType`, LP-UTF16 `.f3d`, an LP-UTF16 display name, an LP-UTF16 description, a document GUID, and a document-asset GUID. Each GUID is a 36-code-unit hyphenated hexadecimal LP-UTF16 string.

Two generation-header forms follow the document-asset GUID. The current form is `u32 1234`, three generation u32 values, and a u32 capability-registry count. The legacy form is a first u32 other than `1234`, one further generation u32, and a u32 capability-registry count. The count can be zero. Each registry entry is a nonempty unique LP-ASCII name and a u32 value. A generation extension follows the registry and ends immediately before the asset-folder tail.

The asset-folder tail is the unique suffix that consumes the end of the manifest with this grammar:

```
LP-UTF16  asset_folder_guid       36-code-unit hyphenated hexadecimal GUID
u32       asset_folder_count      nonzero
repeat asset_folder_count:
  LP-UTF16 asset_folder_base      one archive-path component
u32       terminal_word           0
terminal:
  end of file
  or u8 0, nonempty LP-UTF16 document_display_name, end of file
  or u8 1, end of file
  or u8 1, LP-UTF16 `NA_EXPORT`, end of file
```

Each listed base resolves to exactly one archive folder: either `<base>` or `<base>[Active]`. That folder contains `Manifest.dat`. A **per-asset manifest** starts with its LP-UTF16 base name, a first asset GUID, a second asset GUID, and an LP-ASCII asset type. The unique listed folder whose first asset GUID equals `asset_folder_guid` and whose asset type is `FusionAssetType` is the Design asset folder. Folder-run order, archive-entry order, and B-rep presence do not select the Design asset. A sibling folder can also declare `FusionAssetType`; its independent first asset GUID prevents selection.

After this prefix, a Design per-asset manifest carries the `Neutron3DAssetType` token, a `physicalChangeGuid`, and the segment-type registry (`FusionDesignSegmentType`, `FusionACTSegmentType`, `FusionBrowserSegmentType`).

### 1.4 External references

A document that places other documents records each placement as an **XRef** keyed by an occurrence-role GUID (`neutronRole`). The same role GUID joins four carriers: the container reference table, the Design-segment XRef record, the ACT GUID pool, and — in a multi-document archive — the design description (§1.5).

**`RedirectionsStream.dat`** is a top-level JSON object: `{"name":"RedirectionsStream","schema-version":0,"designs":[...],"references":...}`. `designs` is an array; the first entry describes the document itself and each further entry one referenced document. A design entry carries `file-version` (integer), `targetFileName` (the document's `.f3d` file name), `displayName`, `lineageUrn` (`urn:adsk.wipprod:dm.lineage:<key>`), and `versionUrn` (`urn:adsk.wipprod:fs.file:vf.<key>?version=N`, same `<key>` as the lineage URN). In a document with no outgoing references, `references` is the empty object `{}`. Otherwise `references` is an array of objects `{"from","relativePath","type":"XREF","properties":[...]}`: `from` is the document's own file name, `relativePath` is the target design entry's `targetFileName`, and `properties` is an array of single-member objects `neutronRole` and `neutronData`, each `{"value":"<string>","dataType":"STRING"}`. `neutronRole` is the occurrence-role GUID. `neutronData` is an independent property and need not equal it.

**`ComponentReferenceData.json`** is a top-level STORED entry containing a JSON object. The object is an open document: member names and values are retained without a closed schema.

**Design-segment XRef records.** The Design BulkStream registers the record class name `DcXRefPCIFeatureMetaType`. Its dynamic three-digit ASCII class tag and record index form the indexed-record header. A standalone occurrence record carries, in order: a `u32`-count UTF-16LE record GUID; marker-tagged integer fields; a `0x01`-tagged eight-byte value, which is the presence flag and target entity ID of a cross-document reference; a `u32`-count ASCII GUID, which is that target record's type GUID and appears in the target segment's MetaStream type table; a `0x01`-tagged `u32` zero; and a `u32`-count UTF-16LE occurrence-role string equal to the reference's `neutronRole`. A grouped component-insert carrier can contain several occurrences and repeat the same occurrence-role string in metadata, placement, and construction-reference fields. Several placements may therefore carry the same role and place the same target document more than once. Each occurrence-role string is also present in the ACT-segment GUID pool.

**Placement.** An occurrence placement is an indexed record whose type-table entry has GUID `CE2913AA-CFE0-4F04-9102-24424ED3BCFA` and add-in name `Component`. Its payload is a target path, an optional matrix, and three reference runs:

```
u8   1
u32  count                       path elements
count × (reference, u32 instance discriminator)
u8   0
u8   identity_flag               absent in generation 2, which always stores the matrix
16 × f64                         row-major 4×4, stored only when identity_flag is 0
u32  count, count × reference
u8   tag, u32 count, count × u32, reference     modern containers only
reference
reference
```

The path names the occurrence by its chain of component references. The integer after each element is an instance discriminator, not the element's ordinal along the path: it separates two placements that reach the same element, and `(reference, discriminator)` identifies one placement. The whole tuple of discriminators is a function of the target path. The element carrying the cross-document link name is not necessarily the first.

The matrix is a proper rigid motion. Its upper-left 3×3 block is orthonormal, column 3 is the translation in centimetres, the bottom row is `(0, 0, 0, 1)`, and the determinant is `1`. An `identity_flag` of `1` states that the placement is the identity transform and stores no matrix. The 128-byte matrix is the only length difference between the two forms.

The counted reference run after the matrix reaches both local and cross-document targets. The parser selects the tagged u32 run and its reference exactly when the paired Design `MetaStream.dat` header's serializer magic is `1234`; every other serializer magic selects the two-reference form without that run. The two references that close the record name the same pair of entities for every placement of one document.

A legacy typed occurrence placement uses the same type-table identity but a separate envelope. Its indexed record header is followed by:

```
u8   1
u32  1
legacy target reference
u32  instance discriminator
u32  1
u8   0
u32  1
LP-UTF16 36       opaque GUID field
LP-UTF16 36       opaque GUID field
u8   0
legacy target reference
u8   identity_flag
16 × f64          row-major 4×4, stored only when identity_flag is 0
u32  0
LP-UTF16 36       occurrence-role GUID
u8   0
u8   1
u8   4
u8[9] zero
```

Each legacy target reference is `u8 1`, `u64 target entity ID`, `u8 1`, `u64 opaque target-reference value`, `u8 0`, `LP-ASCII 36 target type GUID`, and `u8 0`. The identity form is 403 bytes from its indexed header through its fixed closing tail; the matrix form is 531 bytes. `identity_flag` `1` stores no matrix, and `0` stores the rigid matrix. The dynamic class tag does not select this envelope: the occurrence-placement type-table identity and exact record closure do.

The transform belongs to the occurrence record rather than the referenced target. Each reference placement projects as one root external occurrence whose target path is `relativePath`; translation converts from centimetres to millimetres. An external occurrence without a serialized transform places the target document's model in the referencing document's frame unchanged.

A type-admitted occurrence placement whose target path parses but whose remaining payload does not close under the selected generation grammar is an undecoded placement, not an identity placement. When no scope-bound construction carrier or other valid placement names the same occurrence role, the decoder retains the container reference at identity and records an `xref.placement-undecoded` loss. An identity marker that closes the placement record is not a loss, and a role with no placement record is not a placement decode failure.

### 1.5 Multi-document archives (`.f3z`)

A `.f3z` is a ZIP archive holding `Manifest.json`, `DesignDescription.json`, and one `.f3d` member per document.

`Manifest.json` is the single-member object `{"root":"<file>.f3d"}` naming the root document's member.

`DesignDescription.json` is `{"name":"Autodesk Design Description","version":"0.1","designDescription":{...}}`. The `designDescription` object carries `id`, `name`, `currentVersion`, and `designGraphs`, an array of graphs. A graph carries `creationDate`, `creatingService`, `rootIds` (the root design-object ids), `designObjectRefs`, and `designObjects`. A design object carries: `id` (integer), `version`, `about` (version URN), `lineage` (lineage URN), `from` (an `urn:adsk.objects:os.object:` URN), `relativePath` and `downloadAs` (both equal to the document's archive member name), `friendlyName`, `displayName`, `zipped`, `rootFile`, `contentType` (`f3d`), `shareInfo`, `references`, `metadata`, and `createdAt`. The `metadata.docstruct` string holds the JSON docstruct mirrored in the member's `Properties.dat` (§1.2), and `metadata.rootFileName` the document's display file name. A referencing design object's `references` is `[{"type":"XREF","ids":[...],"relationships":[...]}]`, where `ids` lists the referenced design-object ids and each relationship is `{"id":<design-object id>,"metadata":{"neutronRole":"<guid>"}}` with the same occurrence-role GUID as the member's `RedirectionsStream.dat` reference (§1.4). A leaf design object's `references` is the empty array.

Reference resolution is member-local and recursive. Each member's `RedirectionsStream.dat` maps its occurrence-role strings to `relativePath` values naming other `.f3d` members, whose lineage and version URNs match those members' design objects. A child occurrence transform maps the child model into its containing member; ancestor occurrence transforms then map that complete result toward the root frame. A member path cannot recur in one active reference chain.

---

## 2. B-rep streams

Both `.smb` and `.smbh` entries carry an ASM `BinaryFile` token stream. The stream encoding — binary header, record framing, unit rules, topology records, geometry carriers, and the construction-history partition — is specified in [`asm.md`](asm.md).

The Design body map selects model BREP entries by complete BREP basename. Each referenced basename resolves to exactly one archive entry. With no body-map binding, exactly one history-bearing stream selects the model; otherwise a single BREP entry is the only fallback. Multiple unbound candidates are ambiguous. History streams are selected independently by the header flag ([`asm.md`](asm.md) §1) and every selected history graph is decoded; `.smb` and `.smbh` extensions do not select either role.

---

## 3. Materials and appearance (non-B-rep)

### 3.1 Design metadata

A class-`369`/`261` `Shell` scope has a 271-byte frame and a 261-byte payload after the ten UTF-16 kind bytes are excluded. Relative to the primary indexed header, offsets 11 through 19 are zero, offset 20 is `1`, offset 21 is the outward Boolean, and offsets 22 through 24 are zero. Offset 25 is `1`; the marked reference at offset 26 names the first ordered scalar and has six zero trailing bytes. Offsets 37 through 45 are zero, offset 46 is `1`, and the marked reference at offset 50 names the second ordered construction group. That group has role `0x0000000400000000` and its member is the third ordered reference, so the shell removes no faces. The group reference has six zero trailing bytes. A u32 `36` at offset 61 precedes a 36-code-unit null GUID at offset 65; offsets 137 through 139 are zero. The three-entry ordered reference table begins at offset 140. The current history-state identity is at offset 177, the kind count is u32 `5` at offset 181, `Shell` begins at offset 185, the feature ordinal follows at offset 195, and the preceding history-state identity is at offset 225. The paired class-261 header begins at offset 271. The first ordered reference is a positive finite wall-thickness scalar in centimetres; offset 21 is its outward convention.

Same-index scope envelopes with the same logical payload are equivalent generation forms. Scope byte offsets, scope IDs, primary and paired class tags, and the current and effective preceding history-state identities do not participate in that equivalence; all other logical fields, including the feature-family token, frame length, family ordinal, and ordered reference indices, must match. When exactly one envelope's current and effective preceding history-state identities resolve to one ASM transition, that envelope is authoritative. When none resolves to an ASM transition and all logical payloads are equivalent, the later equivalent envelope in stream order is authoritative. Every other duplicate group is malformed.

The last edge-recipe member of a counted construction-operand group ends at the immediately following indexed record. Its terminal record index need not continue the consecutive indices of the member's four-record envelope.

A body-map entity suffix between a history-bearing parameter scope and the next parameter scope is the result body of the first scope. Its BREP basename selects that scope's ASM history when the scope's state pair occurs in multiple histories. A whole-body recipe whose solved candidate faces name exactly one candidate BREP supplies the same history selection.

`MetaStream.dat` is a segment header, a type table, two record indexes, and an optional property block. It is not a sequence of independent object records.

The header is the LP-ASCII segment type name, the u32 segment ID, an LP-UTF16 asset GUID, a u32 serializer magic, three further u32 when that magic is `1234` and one otherwise, a second LP-ASCII segment type name, an LP-ASCII add-in name, and two u32. The two type names need not be equal: the first is the segment's short name and the second its full type name.

The type table is `u32 count` and that many entries. An entry is an LP-ASCII type GUID, an LP-ASCII base type GUID, a u32 type version, an LP-ASCII add-in name, a u32 id count, and that many little-endian u64 design-entity IDs. The base type GUID is empty for a root type. The ID count is a count rather than a flag; an entry carries any number of IDs, including none.

A type GUID is an opaque length-prefixed string, not a parsable GUID. The field holds 36 or 37 characters, and its hyphens do not always fall at the canonical positions; a reader that validates hyphen placement or fixes the width at 36 rejects real files. Compare and store the string whole. The inline type GUID a generation-3 reference carries is always 36 characters.

The add-in name names the owning module and is shared by every type that module registers, so it classifies a type's module and does not identify the type. The registered modules are `ACT`, `Animation`, `Body`, `BrowserAddInIdentifier`, `CommonData`, `Component`, `DataModel`, `Dimension`, `EntityTracking`, `Fusion`, `Geometry`, `MSketch`, `ParaMesh`, `Scene`, `SimBase`, `SimCommon`, `SimFEACSObjects`, `SimPostProcessing`, and `SimStructuralAttributes`. The name is also empty, on entries no module claims, so it does not partition the table: the type GUID is the only stable identity a type carries across files.

The type table is followed by `u32 count` and that many u64 named-entity IDs, then the primary record index as `u32 count` and that many `(u64 entity ID, u64 BulkStream offset)` pairs, then a secondary index in the same form. A u64 next-entity-ID counter follows. A segment can end at the secondary index or after this counter.

When bytes remain after the counter, a u32 version-context presence value follows. Value zero carries no context. Value one carries an opaque u64 token, LP-UTF16 asset GUID, LP-UTF16 revision GUID, LP-UTF16 version URN, LP-UTF16 asset-revision GUID, and u32 revision. The three GUIDs are canonical 36-character hyphenated GUIDs. The version URN is a nonempty ASCII URN. Other presence values are invalid. A property block follows both presence forms: `u32 count` and that many `(LP-ASCII key, u32 value)` pairs. The presence value and property block are always present or absent together. These fields close the stream exactly.

Offsets increase strictly within each record index, and entity IDs are unique within each index. The primary index covers every sibling record in the `BulkStream`. Its entry for an entity names that entity's sole live sibling record. An indexed-record-shaped byte run at another offset is contained payload and does not replace or add a sibling record. The named-entity ID list is exactly the set of entity IDs whose bulk record carries a nonempty name. Every secondary-index entity ID is also a primary-index entity ID, and its offset lies strictly inside that entity's primary record, locating a nested record header carried within that record's own payload. The secondary offset bounds one class-member level; it does not end the primary record.

The segment ID is unique within its asset folder, not within the archive, so a cross-segment reference resolves against the segments of its own asset. It is not a fixed enum and it is not positional: a Design segment is not always `0`. The two u32 after the add-in name are a segment _type_ code — `1` Design, `5` ACT, `6` Browser — and are the same in every archive; they are not the segment ID.

**References.** Every reference member of every record in every Fusion segment uses one encoding:

```
u8   present               0 ends the reference; nothing follows
u64  target entity ID
u8   cross_document
if cross_document == 0:
    u8   different_segment
    if different_segment == 1: u32 target segment ID
if cross_document == 1:
    u32  target segment ID          a segment of the TARGET document
    LP-UTF16 asset GUID
    u8   same_document
    if same_document == 0:
        LP-ASCII 36 target type GUID
        LP-UTF16 link name          equals a `RedirectionsStream.dat` `neutronRole`
        u8   versioned
        if versioned == 1:
            LP-UTF16 36 property-key GUID
            LP-UTF16 version URN    an `urn:adsk.wipprod:fs.file:vf.…?version=n`
```

`cross_document`, `different_segment`, `same_document`, and `versioned` are booleans. A cross-document reference does not always end after the link name: `versioned` of `1` appends the property-key GUID that names the version property and the target document's version URN, and the property-key GUID is one value per document. So a null reference is one byte, a same-segment reference is eleven, and a cross-segment reference is fifteen: any length arithmetic that assumes one reference width fails on a record that reaches another segment. The recurring idiom `0x01`, a u32 record index, and six zero bytes elsewhere in this document is this encoding read with a 32-bit id — the six bytes are the id's high half, which is zero while entity IDs stay small, followed by the two flag bytes.

One container generation writes a wider local form: where the meta stream's serializer magic is not `1234` and the first integer after it is `3`, a present reference carries an LP-ASCII 36 **target type GUID** between the target entity ID and `cross_document`, making the local form 51 bytes. The `0x24` length prefix cannot be confused with a `cross_document` flag of `0` or `1`, so a reader can detect the form from the bytes rather than from the header. An LP-ASCII GUID inside a reference always names the target record's type, in this form and in the cross-document form alike; it never names a document.

Within the payload, members are written one class level at a time, most-derived level first, and within a level in **case-sensitive lexicographic order of the member name**, so an uppercase initial sorts before a lowercase one. Declaration order does not reach the stream. A member added at a later record version takes its place in that ordering rather than at the end, so a version gate moves a member into the middle of a level, not onto its tail.

Every payload opens with a presence byte for a class-defined leading block. The byte is `00` or `01` and never anything else. When it is `00` the property-block presence byte follows immediately, which is the common two-byte prologue. When it is `01` the class writes its leading block first and the property-block presence byte follows that block; a sketch relation's pair list is such a block. So the second payload byte is the property-block presence byte only on records whose first byte is `00`, and a reader that takes it unconditionally misframes every record carrying a leading block.

When the property-block presence byte is `01`, a u32 property count and that many `(LP-ASCII key, LP-ASCII type name, value)` triples follow before the first member. `EntityGenesis`, `pt_tag`, `crv_primary_id`, `crv_secondary_id`, `crv_tag_id_idx`, `DcFeatureOperationIdFlag`, and the sketch-text tags are keys of this block, not members of any class; the type name `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64` selects a u64 value. Any reader that addresses members by fixed offset therefore misframes exactly those records that happen to carry a property.

A record's payload may also carry a nested record header for its own entity, located by the segment's secondary index. It bounds the member sequence: the class levels end where that header begins.

A `BulkStream` record begins with an LP-ASCII decimal class tag, the u64 entity ID, and an LP-ASCII record name, then the member payload. The entity ID repeats the record-index key. The class tag is segment-local: its value is `256` plus the index of the type in that segment's type table, so one number names different types in different segments and the type GUID is the only stable discriminator. The type selected by a record's class tag is the type whose ID list contains that record's entity ID.

The Fusion feature-timeline record has type GUID `2F4C1849-1A5A-4F6C-A086-8DD445CBF94B`, base-type GUID `98542EB9-A4F2-4137-A808-DBB5B3CD6159`, type version `2` or `3`, and add-in name `Fusion`. Both versions use the same record grammar. Its type-table entity-ID run is the ordered list of timeline-record identities. Each record has the common `BulkStream` prefix, the two-byte absent-leading-block and absent-property-block prologue, one non-null same-segment context reference, a u32 item count, and that many non-null same-segment references. A reference can carry its target type GUID inline; that GUID equals the target entity's type-table registration. The item references are distinct within the Design stream, and the record ends after the final reference. An empty item run is valid. More than one timeline record can belong to one Design stream, but the records carry separate context references and do not encode an order between two nonempty item runs.

The item run gives authored construction order. An item can name any typed Design record and need not name a parameter scope. A parameter scope named by the item run represents a top-level feature. An unlisted parameter scope is an internal operation operand or history carrier and remains native. Its current and preceding history-state identities can bridge two listed scopes. A history-state dependency follows a unique state chain in one Design stream and one bound ASM history through zero or more unlisted scopes to the preceding listed scope. An absent or non-unique state chain creates no neutral dependency. Equal current and preceding state identities on the same scope do not create a dependency. A listed scope's neutral feature ordinal is its item position; items without a projected scope retain their positions as gaps. An `Assemble` datum envelope bound to a `JointOrigin` scope contributes one `JointOrigin` feature and no `Assemble` feature. The `JointOrigin` uses its own item position when the run lists it and otherwise uses the datum envelope's item position. A timeline-less Design stream contains scopes from one feature family, and its complete one-based family-local ordinal sequence gives their authored order. Independent nonempty timelines and independent scope-bearing Design streams do not encode a shared total construction order.

The design `BulkStream` caches each body's axis-aligned bounding box in the three indexed records whose identities are the body entity suffix plus one, two, and three. Each record contains a `u8 1` marker followed by the same six contiguous f64 values in centimetres, ordered `(xmax, ymax, zmax, xmin, ymin, zmin)`. Every maximum is greater than or equal to its corresponding minimum, and a body has positive extent on at least one axis. The three marker-and-sextuple frames are byte-identical despite the records' different dynamic class tags and prefix lengths. Model-space bounds convert each coordinate to millimetres. The body entity suffix joins the cache to every BREP body-map pair carrying that suffix in the same Design stream.

The design BulkStream BREP body map belongs to the type family with GUID `74CA4562-59F8-4C97-8AD1-F8297C21F9AA`, base-type GUID `E8DCB040-6A0E-4AD1-BCF3-1E1AEDD35EE4`, type version `0`, and add-in name `Body`. The sibling MetaStream type entry lists the family's entity IDs. The primary record index gives each entity's BulkStream offset, and the next primary offset gives the record end. This type family also contains non-map record frames. A body-map frame has an eleven-byte indexed header followed by either ten or eleven reserved zero bytes and a `u32 count`. Exactly `count` ordered pairs of `u64 body_selector, u64 entity_suffix` follow, then `u64 trailing_record_ref`, `u32 pad`, `u32 char_count`, and UTF-16LE basename code units. The string ends at the primary record end. A positive-count record stores a `BREP.<uuid>.smb` or `BREP.<uuid>.smbh` basename. A zero-count record stores no pairs and a zero character count. The pair count has no format bound below `u32::MAX`; the record extent imposes the physical bound. `entity_suffix` is the numeric suffix of the design entity ID. The basename qualifies the selector namespace and selects the BREP blob contributing the mapped bodies to the document model. `body_selector` first matches an equal nonnegative ASM body key. If that key is absent from the blob, the selector matches the zero-based body-record ordinal. Every ordered pair resolves to exactly one solved body under its blob's selector namespace. Multiple referenced blobs contribute independent topology graphs to one document model. A retained nonnegative body-key edit updates Design selectors that matched the old key; ordinal selectors remain unchanged.

The direct join from a mesh geometry container (§1.1.2) to its Design body uses three record classes whose type-table add-in name is `ParaMesh`. The entry-name class has type GUID `A1BAA3F6-4B67-4A0D-BACC-75F38A2230F3`, base-type GUID `130A0711-4E92-4FCD-AADE-B9C82238BB27`, and type version `0`. Its indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes, a marked same-segment reference to the GUID record at offset 21, and the u32-count UTF-16LE blob-part entry name `ParaMeshGeometry.<uuid>.paramesh` at offset 32. The entry name ends at the primary-record boundary. The GUID class has type GUID `A8338A26-5436-433C-8BAC-C3CF024AD595`, base-type GUID `98542EB9-A4F2-4137-A808-DBB5B3CD6159`, and type version `1`. Its indexed header is followed by 21 zero bytes, the 36-byte LP-ASCII `fusion_uuid` at offset 32, and a marked same-segment backlink to the entry-name record at offset 72. The two records reference each other. The stored `fusion_uuid` equals the container field and is independent of the entry-name `<uuid>`.

The mesh-body class has type GUID `EA90DA22-556C-4C61-89BB-20C2681B7A9D`, base-type GUID `CB844AB6-240D-4FC9-9C9F-3679DC896D6F`, and type version `7`. Its indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes. It carries two equal row-major 4×4 f64 affine matrices at record-relative offsets 42 and 171. Unequal matrices make the body record invalid. Marked same-segment references at offsets 508, 519, 530, 541, and 553 name the owning `Base Mesh Feature` scope, the reciprocal body wrapper, a `Body` owner, the GUID record, and a Scene node. The final eleven bytes of the primary record are a marked same-segment backlink to the owning mesh collection. The `Body` owner class has type GUID `CD57BC48-50EC-47DC-975A-FB6DEA72F4DA`, base-type GUID `A7AEA631-985B-4DD1-8CE2-DE2C-14B54081`, type version `4`, and add-in name `Body`. Multiple mesh bodies in one feature can reference the same `Body` owner. Each container joins through one mutually referencing entry-name and GUID pair and one mesh-body record. A join is valid only when this complete chain is unique in one Design stream. The matrix maps container vertex coordinates to model centimetres: its upper-left 3×3 block is nonsingular and carries axis scale and orientation, column 3 carries translation, and its bottom row is `(0, 0, 0, 1)`. The determinant can have either sign. The matrix changes vertex coordinates and transforms oriented normals with its cofactor matrix; triangle tuples and attribute selectors remain in container order. A negative determinant reverses model-space geometric winding relative to container-space winding. An untransformed millimetre mesh stores the f64 nearest float `0.1` on the first three diagonal cells and zero translation. The matrices are followed by two presentation envelopes with the body display name between them. An envelope is a length-prefixed UTF-16LE GUID, the physical-material library id `C1EEA57C-3F56-45FC-B8CB-A9EC46A9994C`, and a length-prefixed UTF-16LE preset name; the first name is the physical material `PrismMaterial-###` and the second is that name's `_physmat_aspects:Prism-###` form. The display-name count is zero on a body that carries no name.

The mesh-collection class has type GUID `443807AD-8025-41A3-8A50-5157579C3D78`, base-type GUID `A7AEA631-985B-4DD1-8CE2-DE2C-14B54081`, type version `0`, and add-in name `ParaMesh`. Its indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes, a u32 body count at offset 21, bytes `01 01` at offset 25, a marked same-segment texture-table reference at offset 27, and a nested same-index record at offset 38. The nested record has type GUID `A7AEA631-985B-4DD1-8CE2-DE2C-14B54081`, base-type GUID `834C9DEF-4C39-4587-A08D-A5BD1267B7B4`, type version `0`, and add-in name `CommonData`. Its indexed header is followed by nine zero bytes, a second u32 body count at collection offset 58, that many ordered marked same-segment body references at offset 62, and one marked same-segment owner reference. The two counts are equal. The collection ends after the owner reference and has length `73 + 11 × body_count` bytes. The owner has type GUID `E03784ED-5E19-4E14-B9F2-3B07017018CD`, base-type GUID `42054630-20A0-40E1-B969-CFE9E742F5C9`, type version `23`, and add-in name `Fusion`. Its marked same-segment reference at offset 262 points back to the collection. Each mesh feature has one collection.

The texture-table class has type GUID `6FC173DC-C7E3-402C-A8C0-891A26DADF8D`, base-type GUID `98542EB9-A4F2-4137-A808-DBB5B3CD6159`, type version `0`, and add-in name `ParaMesh`. Its indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes and a u32 flags-map count at offset 21. Each flags-map entry is a 36-byte LP-ASCII resource GUID followed by an opaque u32 flags value. A u32 filename-map count follows the flags map. Each filename-map entry is a 36-byte LP-ASCII resource GUID followed by a marked same-segment filename-record reference. Keys are unique within each map, both maps have the same key set, and their serialized key orders are independent. Multiple resource keys can reference the same filename record. Both maps end at the primary-record boundary. An empty table is exactly 29 bytes. A filename record has type GUID `830A2A2B-0AA9-4D6A-ACA1-F7A2B2A06573`, base-type GUID `98542EB9-A4F2-4137-A808-DBB5B3CD6159`, type version `0`, and an empty add-in name. Its indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes and one nonempty u32-count UTF-16LE archive-entry basename. The basename ends at the primary-record boundary and resolves to exactly one entry in `Images.BlobParts`.

The mesh wrapper has type GUID `E5B3F49A-D8D0-4EEF-BC2B-FCDDAEF9745E`, base-type GUID `98542EB9-A4F2-4137-A808-DBB5B3CD6159`, type version `0`, and add-in name `ParaMesh`. It is exactly 40 bytes: its indexed header, ten zero bytes, a marked same-segment body reference at offset 21, and eight zero bytes. The body references the same wrapper at body offset 519. Each body has one wrapper.

The `Base Mesh Feature` scope has type GUID `99F6967E-ED35-4222-B906-5CCF0AC70B53`, base-type GUID `2FCB0587-233E-449B-9724-9AAE5AA23647`, type version `0`, and add-in name `Fusion`. Its indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes, a u32 body count at offset 21, and that many ordered marked same-segment body references at offset 25. The parameter-scope payload follows this list. The scope closes with a nested same-index record whose type GUID is `CB844AB6-240D-4FC9-9C9F-3679DC896D6F`, base-type GUID is `98542EB9-A4F2-4137-A808-DBB5B3CD6159`, type version is `1`, and add-in name is `DataModel`. This closing record is exactly 30 bytes: its indexed header, eight zero bytes, and a marked same-segment owner reference at offset 19. The scope, collection prefix, and collection base carry the same body count and the same ordered body-record list. Every listed body points back to both records.

The fixed mesh Scene-state record has type GUID `F85F2E62-7627-4922-A16D-53E1275D2AAC`, base-type GUID `D0EDEF7C-5879-45A4-9651-900659CC4FDD`, type version `0`, and add-in name `Scene`. It is exactly 95 bytes: its indexed header, 34 zero bytes, byte `01`, and a 49-byte mask. Mask bytes 6, 14, 22, 30, 38, and 46 are `EF`; bytes 31, 39, and 47 are `7F`; byte 48 is `01`; all other mask bytes are `FF`.

The Scene node has type GUID `702B9CD2-537C-429E-8CC4-22BEEEB98C37`, base-type GUID `EB7847AF-E60D-4AB0-A736-4AC00C1F1D21`, type version `1`, and add-in name `Scene`. It is exactly 133 bytes. Its indexed header is followed by 14 zero bytes, u32 values `2` at offsets 25 and 29, a marked same-segment Scene-state reference at offset 33, u32 value `3` at offset 44, a marked same-segment auxiliary-record reference at offset 48, 24 zero bytes at offset 59, and the same 50-byte Scene footer at offset 83. The auxiliary record has type GUID `2343B7AB-A2E0-4C66-8B74-99A05E4C670B`, base-type GUID `74D7FEF9-44E9-494D-A25D-81EC33D2841E`, type version `1`, and add-in name `Scene`. Each mesh body has one Scene node, state, and auxiliary record.

The design BulkStream carries the document's unit settings in a `UnitSystems` collection. The collection is the LP-ASCII name `UnitSystems`, two zero bytes, `u32 6`, and six `01 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes` references. The six referenced records are the unit systems `CmMKS`, `MmMKS`, `MMKS`, `InchImperial`, `Imperial`, and `Custom`, in that order. A unit-system record stores an LP-ASCII key, an LP-ASCII label, byte `01`, the LP-ASCII name `<key>UnitSystemName`, the LP-ASCII namespace `NaFusion`, four zero bytes, `u32 19`, and nineteen `01 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes` references to its unit entries. A unit entry stores an LP-ASCII key, an LP-ASCII label, byte `01`, an LP-ASCII property name, the LP-ASCII namespace `NsCommonData`, four zero bytes, a `u32` UTF-16 code-unit count, and that many code units holding the unit name. Seventeen entries are the quantity families `Length`, `Mass`, `Time`, `Temperature`, `Speed`, `Volume`, `Pressure`, `Force`, `Power`, `Energy`, `Current`, `Substance`, `Luminosity`, `Angle`, `Currency`, `Percentage`, and `Pieces`; the remaining two are `ModelingLength` and `ModelingMass`.

The five preset systems hold fixed `ModelingLength` names: `centimeter` for `CmMKS`, `millimeter` for `MmMKS`, `meter` for `MMKS`, `inch` for `InchImperial`, and `foot` for `Imperial`. The `Custom` system holds the document's active settings; its `ModelingLength` entry, whose property name is `modelingLengthName`, holds the document display length unit as one of `millimeter`, `centimeter`, `meter`, `inch`, or `foot`. The display unit is a presentation setting and is the sole carrier of that setting. No stored quantity depends on it: model-space coordinates remain centimetres under every value, the BREP streams are independent of it, and every material-asset value carries its own unit tag.

A construction-recipe record stores its Design selector in one of two fixed frames. The prefix frame places the positive u32 selector at 20 bytes before the recipe-family name, its i32 record index at `name−16`, eight zero bytes at `name−12`, and the u32 byte length of the ASCII family name at `name−4`. The body-recipe suffix frame places the length-prefixed ASCII Design entity id at `name+24` and the selector immediately after the id; that selector names the enclosing whole-body operand's `N+4` record. In both frames, the selector immediately follows the ASCII Design entity id. The family name selects `body_recipe_data`, `face_recipe_data`, `bounded_face_recipe_data`, `edge_recipe_data`, or `vertex_recipe_data`; the preceding record index is not a family discriminator. The common payload begins with an i64 `−1` null sentinel followed by the five i32 values `[2, 0, −1, 1, −1]`. An edge-recipe operand opens with four consecutively indexed records `N` through `N+3`; record `N+3` contains the recipe prefix, family name, and i32 program. The indexed record `N+4` terminates the ordinary edge-feature program. A `Sweep` path or guide-rail edge recipe terminates at indexed record `N+7`. Header-shaped scalar bytes inside the prefix do not terminate the operand.

An unresolved edge-selection record consists of its eleven-byte indexed header, fourteen zero bytes, `u32 19`, and ASCII `EDGE_REFERENCE_LOST`. The indexed header immediately following the marker terminates the record. Consecutive unresolved selections share that boundary: the following header of one record is the owning header of the next. An unresolved run belongs to the counted edge group whose first identity-wrapper header is the run's final following header. The run cardinality equals the group's operand count. A complete independent recipe/history proof resolves the group's historical edges despite the unresolved run. Without such a proof, a group belonging to a scope with a preceding history state retains that state and the ordered unresolved-record identities with no resolved edges.

A fixed-radius Fillet scope with exactly one counted construction-operand group uses that group as its edge selection when every member is a compact persistent edge identity and either the group has one member with a nonempty transition-derived radius chain or every member has an exact predecessor-edge proof. A transition-derived radius-candidate set describes the complete state transition and does not assign a candidate subset to each member. The selected chain is the increasing stable-slot-order union of the qualifying predecessor edges. With multiple construction-operand groups, compact identity prefixes do not distinguish edge selections from support-face groups; only a complete edge-recipe carrier assigns the selected-edge role.

An executed `Fillet` or `Chamfer` edge group may use one nonempty transition chain when every compact persistent identity in the group carries the same chain. Without recipe evidence, the chain is complete only when its cardinality equals the member count. When the cardinalities differ, every chain edge must occur in the union of the selected recipe-reference edge sets and the members' changed or deleted boundary sets. Every member with deleted boundary edges must share one with the chain, and a resolved deleted edge must belong to the chain. The admitted chain is ordered by increasing stable edge slot.

Standalone persistent-reference properties named `pt_tag`, `crv_primary_id`, and `crv_secondary_id` store the u32 pair `(2, 14)`, a 14-byte property slot, LP-ASCII `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64`, and the referenced u64. The compact properties embedded in sketch point and curve records omit the `(2, 14)` pair and property slot.

A generic face or edge tag group may carry a zero Design-reference count. Its selector and token remain native attribute data, but the group has no Design join and does not supply a recipe candidate. The group is retained and can be written with an empty reference run.

After the `generic_tag_attrib_def` family name, a generic-tag attribute stores two equal envelope-version integers, integer `−1`, the marker `generic_tag_attrib_def `, and a nonnegative group count. Envelope version `2` stores each body group as four values: entity kind, decimal Design id, Design reference, and integer `0`; its face and edge groups are a selector, a nonempty token, integer `0`, a nonnegative Design-reference count, and that many signed Design references. Version `3` adds one integer `0` to each body group and one integer `0` terminator to each face or edge group. A mixed or unsupported envelope-version pair invalidates the generic-tag payload.

When the final eleven bytes of a sketch point or curve primary record are a marked reference, that reference is the geometry record's direct owner backlink: `0x01 + u32 sketch_container_index + six zero bytes`. It is independent of constraint membership and resolves to a sketch entity container of type `44A64366-4BD3-4B24-881A-F94C206E8F2D` with the same record index in the Design stream. Point class versions 0 and 8, the same-segment form of point class version 10, point class version 11, and line class version 2 carry this final backlink. In the compact NURBS form, the backlink follows the complete knot, weight, and three-coordinate control-point arrays; the indexed NURBS subtype header is nested within the curve record and does not terminate it. A point or curve without this final backlink has no direct owner. A typed sketch relation or the sketch entity container's counted member run can independently assign that record to a Sketch. Other marked references inside an extended geometry payload do not assign an owner. A record with none of these three joins is an unowned Geometry carrier.

A sketch-point geometry payload has type GUID `C2CEDAE7-1716-47C1-B7B1-07B70081D0FB`, add-in name `Geometry`, and class version 0, 8, 10, or 11. Class version 0 has no `pt_tag` property. Its indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes, a marked same-segment `paired_reference`, one flag byte, and two contiguous f64 coordinates `(u,v)`. It has no persistent point identity and no serialized `w`; its neutral depth is zero.

Class versions 8, 10, and 11 carry a `pt_tag` u64 property. An optional `EntityGenesis` u64 precedes `pt_tag` in class version 11. The property block is followed by `paired_reference`. Class versions 8 and 10 then carry seven one-byte flags. Class version 11 carries eight one-byte flags. Three contiguous f64 coordinates `(u,v,w)` follow the flags. Each flag is zero or one. All coordinates use the record form's standard length conversion. A planar Sketch uses `(u,v)` and requires `w = 0`. A spatial Sketch transforms `(u,v,w)` through the owning placement into model space. Point and curve records with the same owner belong to one neutral Sketch domain; an owner with any non-planar curve is spatial and does not also form a planar Sketch.

The class-version-0 tail follows `(u,v)` with twenty zero bytes, f32 one, twelve zero bytes, two f32 values equal to one, bytes `01 01 00 00 00 00 01 00 00 00`, a repeated marked same-segment `paired_reference`, and the direct owner backlink.

The class-version-8 closure follows `(u,v,w)` with a u64 selector, a state byte, eight zero bytes, two f32 values equal to one, bytes `00 01 00 00 00`, a repeated marked same-segment `paired_reference`, and the direct owner backlink. The only selector-state pair is `(0,0)`. The class-version-10 and class-version-11 closures use twelve zero bytes in place of the class-version-8 eight-zero lane. The same-segment class-version-10 sequence uses `(0,0)` or `(0,1)`. The inline-typed class-version-10 sequence uses those pairs or `(2,1)`. Class version 11 uses `(0,0)`, `(0,1)`, `(1,0)`, `(2,1)`, or `(4,0)`. A class-version-11 repeated `paired_reference` can be followed by four additional zero bytes before the direct owner backlink.

The point selector, point state, and versioned point flags do not select the neutral construction Boolean. A point projects as a non-construction point. A curve becomes construction geometry only through a valid `TextFrame` relation; its endpoint point records do not inherit that status. In particular, `(4,0)` point endpoints can belong to a bounded line used by a midpoint relation or to a bounded line with no sketch relation, and neither the points nor that line become construction geometry from the pair alone. Profile admission therefore remains based on the resolved curve graph and the explicit `TextFrame` exclusion, not on point closure values.

The `(2,1)` selector-state pair is the standalone-point form. Its companion has an incident-curve count of zero, and the point remains non-construction.

Class version 10 has a second member sequence. Its first and repeated `paired_reference` members serialize the target type GUID `362B7EC3-0F09-47C8-A3BE-DC066715CDAE` inline between the u64 entity ID and the two same-segment flag bytes. The final member is an inline-typed reference whose target type GUID is `44A64366-4BD3-4B24-881A-F94C206E8F2D`. That final member is not a direct owner backlink.

Class version 11 also has an inline-typed member sequence. Its first and repeated `paired_reference` members serialize the target type GUID `362B7EC3-0F09-47C8-A3BE-DC066715CDAE` inline, and its final member serializes the target type GUID `44A64366-4BD3-4B24-881A-F94C206E8F2D` inline. The final member is not a direct owner backlink.

`paired_reference` resolves to a class-version-0 `Geometry` record of type `362B7EC3-0F09-47C8-A3BE-DC066715CDAE`. The companion has an eleven-byte indexed header followed by one of two prefixes. The fixed prefix is ten zero bytes. The class-version-11 present-zero prefix is nine zero bytes and bytes `01 00 00 00 00`. A u32 incident-curve count follows the prefix, followed by that many ordered sketch-curve references, one zero byte, and a final inverse reference to the point record. The inverse reference ends the companion. A same-segment point form uses marked same-segment references throughout its companion. An inline-typed class-version-10 or class-version-11 point form serializes each incident target's registered type GUID and the point type GUID inline in the inverse reference. The compact 37-byte form uses the fixed prefix, same-segment references, and an incident-curve count of zero.

A sketch-line geometry payload begins with three contiguous f64 triples: start point, displacement from start to end, and unit direction. Coordinates and displacement use the standard centimetre-to-millimetre length conversion; direction is unscaled. The full form appends a unit auxiliary normal as a fourth f64 triple. The compact planar form ends the scalar payload after the direction and immediately stores a marked record reference; its start, displacement, and direction have zero `w` components, and its omitted normal is the sketch-local `+w` axis. A referenced analytic wrapper inserts `0x01 + u32 reference + six zero bytes` before either line form. The bounded endpoints are the start point and start plus displacement. The neutral tangent is the normalized displacement. The auxiliary normal is projected onto the plane perpendicular to that tangent and normalized; when the stored unit auxiliary vector is parallel to the tangent, the first canonical coordinate axis least aligned with the tangent supplies a perpendicular normal.

A legacy inline sketch NURBS begins at curve payload offset 133. It stores an eight-byte nullable carrier reference, `u32 3`, a three-byte decimal subtype class tag, the subtype record index, eight zero bytes, and `0x01 + u32 reference + ten zero bytes`. The f64 fit tolerance, `u32 0`, and one zero pad byte follow. Three contiguous marked references use `0x01 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes`. Two Boolean state bytes, the u32 degree, and the eight-byte token `95 D6 26 E8 0B 2E 11 3E` follow. Knots, weights, and control points are three consecutive counted arrays.
