Each array header is `u32 count, u32 capacity, u32 8`; capacity is greater than or equal to count, knots and weights contain `count` f64 scalars, and control points contain three f64 coordinates per count. The knot count equals the control-point count plus degree plus one. The weights are positive, the knots are nondecreasing, and all scalar values are finite. Fit tolerance and control-point coordinates use the standard centimetre-to-millimetre length conversion; knots and weights are unscaled.

A body-visibility browser-node member has type GUID `D26351F0-5940-4D23-AA20-2C35475A6D9E`, base-type GUID `CB844AB6-240D-4fc9-9C9F-3679DC896D6F`, type version `2`, and add-in name `Fusion`. The type-table entry and primary record index select its exact frame. Its indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes, an LP-UTF16 36-character node GUID, a one-byte hidden flag, `0x01 0x01`, and the node's u64 Design entity suffix. Flag `1` hides the entity in the document display; `0` shows it. Only this `0x01 0x01` member form carries body visibility; other frames registered to the same type do not. **Body visibility join:** BREP-qualified body selector → BREP body-map `entity_suffix` → typed browser-node hidden flag.

A body-presentation owner has type GUID `D3937028-C20C-4E65-B010-94AD418A5C20`, base-type GUID `63D6AD42-466A-40B7-9CCD-EB21BC500EF6`, type version `19`, and add-in name `Body`. If its type-table ordinal is `k`, its stream-local three-digit class tag is `256 + k`. The type entry lists the owner's entity, and the matching primary-index offset starts the owner frame. The next primary offset ends it; the end of the BulkStream ends the last frame.

The named-owner form uses a fixed or `EntityGenesis` entity header that identifies body entity suffix `E`. After the class-prefix members, its material member sequence is an LP-UTF16 browser-node GUID, the eleven bytes `01 01 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00`, an LP-UTF16 physical-material asset GUID, LP-UTF16 physical-material library id `C1EEA57C-3F56-45FC-B8CB-A9EC46A9994C`, an LP-UTF16 `PrismMaterial-###` token, a non-null same-segment reference, a null reference, a second non-null same-segment reference, an LP-UTF16 body display name, class padding ending in f32 opacity `1`, `01 01`, class padding, and an LP-UTF16 visual token. A visual token is a 36-character GUID followed by zero or more literal `_Post2015` suffixes. Equal GUID prefixes with different suffix counts identify different appearance records. The first non-null reference resolves to type GUID `CD57BC48-50EC-47DC-975A-FB6DEA72F4DA`, version `4`. The second resolves to the body cache record `E + 1` of type GUID `702b9cd2-537c-429e-8cc4-22beeeb98c37`, version `1`, add-in name `Scene`. The visual tail is either LP-UTF16 appearance library id `BA5EE55E-9982-449B-9D66-9F036540E140` followed by an optional LP-UTF16 `Prism-###` preset, or the LP-UTF16 identifier pair `08861000-1D69-CF2A-C082-CBD98E7E5D7F`, `005E1000-55CE-AFB6-81A1-36E3EF077C5F`. The browser-node GUID equals exactly one typed body-visibility browser-node GUID whose entity suffix is `E`; neither reference in the three-reference run identifies that browser node.

The bare-owner form stores u64 body entity suffix `E` immediately after its stream-local class tag and stores no component-qualified entity ID. A header-shaped byte run in the frame payload does not replace this primary owner. The material envelope contains adjacent LP-UTF16 identifiers `D87FBE62-3B12-4CA8-9014-BAD31ABDB101` and `C1EEA57C-3F56-45FC-B8CB-A9EC46A9994C`, an LP-UTF16 `PrismMaterial-###` token, one non-null same-segment reference, an LP-UTF16 browser-node GUID, a non-null same-segment reference to node entity `E + 1`, an optional LP-UTF16 body display name, optional f32 opacity `1`, bytes `01 01`, an LP-UTF16 visual token, and the LP-UTF16 identifier pair `08861000-1D69-CF2A-C082-CBD98E7E5D7F`, `005E1000-55CE-AFB6-81A1-36E3EF077C5F`. The envelope belongs to `E` only when it is unique within the exact owner frame and its browser-node GUID resolves to exactly one typed body-visibility browser node whose entity suffix is `E`. Class tag `299` has no fixed body-presentation meaning.

A paired-library envelope is face-scoped only when two lower-case canonical GUID members and the fixed face-presentation carriers precede its visual token in one primary-index owner frame. The second GUID is the B-rep face-attribute identity. The first GUID does not participate in the face join. A paired-library tail without this two-GUID carrier form is not a face assignment. **Body appearance join:** named or bare body-presentation owner → Design entity suffix → the sole body-map pair with that suffix in the same Design stream → the pair's BREP basename and body selector. The basename is part of the body identity; equal selectors in different BREP basenames do not alias. More than one pair with the owner suffix, more than one body with the selector in the named BREP blob, or more than one appearance with the complete visual token makes the join invalid.

A design entity header opens with an indexed record header. The fixed form stores the u64 entity suffix, five zero bytes, an optional slot (`0x00`, or `0x01` and four zero bytes), and the LP-UTF16 entity ID whose numeric suffix equals the entity suffix. The `EntityGenesis` form stores a zero run, `0x01 + u32 1`, LP-ASCII `EntityGenesis`, LP-ASCII `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64`, the u64 origin bitfield, and the LP-UTF16 entity ID; its record index is the entity suffix. An `EntityGenesis`-form sketch entity header is followed by a paired record carrying the same record index. That record's u32 member count at record offset 52 is followed by a marked reference to the sketch's base-point record and `count` entries of `0x01 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes` naming the sketch's owned records. A point or curve record without relation membership joins its owning sketch through the base-point reference or the member run.

In the legacy sketch form, an `MSketch` metadata object names candidate indexed-record identities. A sketch container is the candidate whose primary indexed record is immediately followed by a second indexed record with the same record index. The paired record stores eight zero bytes after its header and a marked u32 head-placement reference. A member-owning tail continues with six zero bytes, a u32 sketch ordinal, seven state bytes, and a u32 member count at record offset 41. The count is followed by exactly that many `0x01 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes` entries naming every record owned by the sketch. A localized tail omits the counted member run; its existence is closed by the referenced head-placement frame and its geometry ownership is supplied by relation owner references. The head-placement record is either 34 bytes or 162 bytes long. The 34-byte form stores ten zero bytes, `01 00`, a marked u32 reference, and six zero bytes after its indexed header and denotes the identity transform. The 162-byte form stores eleven zero bytes, sixteen row-major f64 transform values, and the marked tail `00 01`; its matrix is the sketch-local-to-model transform. The container record index is the sketch identity and owner reference.

A sketch entity container follows its self-validating entity header and UTF-16LE entity ID with an eight-byte base-record slot, `0x01`, `u32 reference_count`, then `reference_count` entries of `0x01 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes`. The base-record slot is either `u32 record_reference` followed by `u32 zero`, or the sentinel `0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF` when the sketch has no base record; the list grammar is identical in both forms. The referenced records contain the sketch's geometry and relations. An `EntityGenesis`-form sketch entity record stores the sketch display name as a u32-count UTF-16LE string at payload offset 169.

The current sketch entity container has type GUID `44A64366-4BD3-4B24-881A-F94C206E8F2D`, base-type GUID `37AD519C-AFB3-4CE2-9E6D-E3269FC6CDB9`, type version `18`, and add-in name `MSketch`. Its entity header is followed by a same-index Geometry member. The member has type GUID `37AD519C-AFB3-4CE2-9E6D-E3269FC6CDB9`, base-type GUID `A7AEA631-985B-4DD1-8CE2-DE2C-14B54081`, type version `4`, and add-in name `Geometry`. The member starts with `u32 3`, its three-digit dynamic class tag, and the owning u64 sketch entity suffix. Four zero bytes and one same-segment marked owner reference follow. The u32 at member offset 30 is the one-based ordinal of the sketch Geometry member in its Design stream. These ordinals form the contiguous sequence from `1` through the stream's sketch Geometry member count. Byte 34 is zero. The one-byte sketch visibility flag is at member offset 35, and byte 36 is `01`. Visibility value `0` hides the Sketch and value `1` shows it.

Typed non-construction sketch curves form neutral profile loops through endpoint incidence at the document linear tolerance. Curves named by a valid `TextFrame` relation are construction geometry: they remain projected for the text-frame constraint but are excluded from profile loops. A line's stored unit normal is an auxiliary orientation vector. Its component parallel to the stored unit line direction is removed and the remainder is normalized; when no remainder exists, the first canonical coordinate axis least aligned with the line direction supplies the perpendicular normal. The resulting normal is perpendicular to the line and retains the stored orientation side when the stored vector provides one. A full circle or full ellipse is a one-curve loop. A bounded line uses its stored endpoints, an arc uses the endpoints evaluated from its center, radius, and angles, a bounded ellipse uses its rotated parametric endpoints, and a nonperiodic NURBS curve uses the points evaluated at knots `degree` and `control_point_count`. An unbranched connected component is a profile exactly when every endpoint vertex has degree two. A branched component consisting entirely of lines uses the counterclockwise angular order of outgoing half-edges at each vertex; every positive-area bounded face is one profile and the negative-area exterior walk is excluded. Profile order starts with the lowest neutral sketch-entity identity, and each subsequent use records whether traversal opposes the entity's stored direction. Open chains, zero-area walks, and branched components containing nonlinear curves do not form neutral profiles. Strictly nested closed line loops, mixed line-and-circular-arc loops, loops containing nonperiodic positive-weight NURBS curves, and full circles form atomic planar regions. Point containment counts analytic horizontal-ray intersections with lines and directed circular arcs using a half-open endpoint rule. Positive-weight NURBS boundaries use the homogeneous control polygon to bound curve speed over each nonzero knot span. Subspan chords carry conservative tubes whose radius bounds the complete rational curve subspan; winding is defined only when every tube excludes the projected point, and two boundaries are disjoint only when every cross-boundary tube pair is disjoint. A region's exterior is the unique strict-containment-minimal loop containing every projected selection point, and its holes are the exterior's immediate children in the strict-containment tree. Polygon-to-circle containment requires the circle center inside the polygon and every polygon edge strictly outside the circle. A mixed-loop exterior contains a circle when the circle center is inside and its radius is smaller than the minimum exact distance to every line or directed arc. Circle-to-polygon and circle-to-mixed-loop containment requires every segment's maximum distance from the center to be smaller than the circle radius. Circle-to-circle containment compares center distance plus the inner radius with the outer radius. Polygon-to-mixed-loop, mixed-loop-to-polygon, and mixed-loop-to-mixed-loop containment require one inner boundary point inside the exterior and no analytic line-line, line-circle, or circle-circle intersection between their bounded segments. Touching, intersecting, or tube-inseparable loops do not form a containment relation. A set of projected points on both the outer loop and one or more immediate child loops selects the unique atomic region whose closure contains every point. Points lying only on one loop remain ambiguous between the regions adjacent to that loop. A point carried by an edge perpendicular to the sketch plane selects the region containing the edge's coincident endpoint projections.

When a line-only branched component has one degree-four tangent junction, exactly two source-oriented cycles, opposite signed areas, and a non-junction probe of the smaller cycle inside the larger cycle, the two cycles form one connected profile boundary. The outer cycle is oriented positive and the inner cycle negative before concatenation at the shared junction. Other tangent junctions remain unresolved.

Atomic-region classification requires a supported boundary model for every profile loop in the selected Sketch and a defined strict-containment relation for every distinct boundary pair. An unclassified sibling loop or boundary-pair relation withholds the region because the relationship may be containment, intersection, or disjointness.

Intersecting and coincident profile loops form a planar arrangement when every curve intersection is represented by an endpoint-incidence node. A bounded line or circular arc splits at every incident node in its interior, and a full circle splits at every incident endpoint angle. Coincident line segments and same-circle subarcs with the same endpoint nodes collapse to one arrangement edge. Distinct analytic edges may meet only at their shared nodes; line-circle and circle-circle pairs account for every bounded intersection, including a second intersection away from a shared node. A full-domain nonperiodic positive-weight NURBS may meet a line only at its endpoint when its endpoint control point lies on the line and every other control point lies strictly in one open half-plane. It may meet a circular boundary only at its endpoint when every other control point lies strictly inside the circle or strictly beyond the circle's supporting tangent at that endpoint. Nonadjacent NURBS-bearing edges are disjoint only when their conservative curve tubes are pairwise disjoint. The counterclockwise tangent order of outgoing half-edges defines every positive-area bounded face; the exterior walk is excluded. A selected arrangement face records each source sketch entity, the stored parameter interval used by that edge, and whether face traversal opposes the source direction.

Nonplanar sketch curves transfer into separate spatial-sketch arenas in model coordinates. The owning Design placement maps points by its rotation and scaled origin and maps direction vectors by its rotation. Spatial lines retain both endpoints. Spatial circles and arcs retain center, unit normal, perpendicular unit reference direction, radius, and arc angles. Spatial nonperiodic NURBS retain degree, knots, control points, and weights. A full circle forms one spatial profile with its stored center, normal, and reference direction. Bounded spatial curves form a profile when one unbranched connected component has exactly one continuation at every endpoint, closes with at least three curves, and its ordered endpoints lie in one nondegenerate plane. The plane normal follows boundary traversal, its u-axis follows the first boundary edge, and every boundary use records whether traversal opposes the stored curve direction. Spatial profile construction excludes construction geometry, open or branched components, nonplanar loops, and zero-area loops. Spatial curves do not participate in planar profile-loop construction or planar sketch constraints.

Class tag `287` uses the compact-owned parameter prefix and a class-specific tail. Its indexed header is followed by fifteen zero bytes, `u32 source_ordinal` at offset 26, `u8 1 + u32 owner_record_index` at offsets 30 and 31, six zero bytes at offsets 35 through 40, the expression at offset 41, and one of the exact five-byte expression trailers `00 00 00 00 00` or `00 00 00 01 00`. The remainder is LP-UTF16 `source_kind`, LP-UTF16 unit and name fields, an f64 evaluated scalar, and the twelve-byte tail `00 01 AF 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00`. The unit field may be an explicit zero-length LP-UTF16 field. The owner reference resolves through the indexed Design record graph when its primary frame exists; an absent primary frame leaves the parameter native without an owner binding.

An indexed Design record header is `u32 class_tag_length`, a three-digit ASCII dynamic-class tag, then `u32 record_index`. `record_index` is a logical reference value; it is independent of the header's byte offset in the `BulkStream`. Record indices and Design entity suffixes are local to one Design `BulkStream`; indexed-record, sketch-owner, parameter-owner, and geometry joins never cross `BulkStream` boundaries.

An indexed parameter record has three fixed header forms. The discriminated form extends the indexed header with eleven zero bytes, a u64 family discriminator, one zero byte, `u32 source_ordinal`, and one of two owner forms. The current discriminated family uses discriminator `6` for the standard `00 01 10` tail, and discriminator `5` for exact `source_kind` `ScaleFactor` in that same tail; both tails are `00 01 10 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00`. The earlier discriminated family uses discriminator `0`, `3`, or `4` for all source kinds, or discriminator `6` for exact `source_kind` `TangencyWeight`, and tail `00 01 13 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00`. Values `0`, `3`, and `4` do not identify `source_kind`. A document parameter stores `u8 0`. A dimension or feature parameter stores `u8 1`, `u32 owner_record_index`, and six zero bytes; the owner resolves through the indexed Design record graph. Both forms then store an LP-UTF16 source expression. The document form follows the expression with eight zero bytes and `u8 1`; the owned form follows it with nine zero bytes. Sheet-metal feature inputs `FlangeHeight`, `FlangeAngle`, `HemGap`, and `HemLength` instead use ten zero bytes in the owned form. The ordinary compact owned form extends the indexed header with fifteen zero bytes, `u32 source_ordinal`, `u8 1`, `u32 owner_record_index`, and six zero bytes. Its LP-UTF16 expression is followed by five zero bytes. It carries no family discriminator and no reserved u32 after `source_kind`; the discriminated form stores reserved u32 `0` there. The legacy compact owned form extends the indexed header with fourteen zero bytes, `u32 source_ordinal`, `u8 1`, `u32 owner_record_index`, and six zero bytes. Its LP-UTF16 expression is followed by five zero bytes, and its twelve-byte tail begins `00 01 12`. The remainder of each header form is LP-UTF16 `source_kind`, an optional LP-UTF16 unit token, LP-UTF16 parameter name, and an f64 evaluated scalar. The non-legacy forms use a twelve-byte tail beginning `00 01 13`. A dimensionless parameter may encode the absent unit as an explicit zero-length LP-UTF16 field. Exact `source_kind` `User Parameter` denotes a document parameter, a value containing `Dimension` denotes a dimensional constraint, and every other value denotes a feature input. Design parameter values use database units independently of the declared display unit: `mm`, `cm`, `m`, `in`, and `ft` lengths are stored in centimetres, while `deg` and `rad` angles are stored in radians. Dimensionless and Boolean parameters omit the unit token. A parameter with another unit token retains that token and its evaluated database scalar without assigning an unproved neutral quantity kind. `source_ordinal` is a native ordering value and can repeat within one Design `BulkStream`. A neutral parameter identity is the byte-length-prefixed Design stream name followed by the indexed-record index; `source_ordinal` and owner-record position do not participate in parameter identity. The first serialized parameter frame for a logical record index is canonical within its Design `BulkStream`; a later frame with the same index is not a second neutral parameter. Parameter names are case-sensitive; letters, digits, `_`, `"`, `$`, `°`, and `µ` remain part of an identifier in expression text. An identifier followed by `(` is a function-call head rather than a parameter reference. `mm`, `cm`, `m`, `in`, `ft`, `deg`, and `rad` following a numeric or parenthesized value are unit suffixes; the same bare token in another expression position remains a parameter identifier. A document parameter expression resolves identifiers against uniquely named document parameters in the same Design `BulkStream`. A feature-owned parameter expression resolves first against a parameter of its owning feature, then document parameters, then a uniquely named parameter of a preceding feature in the same stream. Feature precedence follows authored timeline order, and uniqueness is evaluated after excluding the current and later features. An ambiguous eligible name, self-reference, or reference with no preceding owner does not form a dependency. Within each parameter scope, neutral ordinals are the stable topological order of resolved dependencies; native ordinal values provide the tie-break order. A dependency cycle is broken at its lowest native ordinal, after which topological ordering continues.

Ordinarily, every dimension or feature-input parameter has one indexed owner frame at `owner_record_index`; class-287 parameters may retain that reference after the primary owner frame is absent. The primary indexed header starts the frame. The next indexed header with the same `record_index` ends the frame and is not part of it. A header with another record index does not end the frame. All ordinary owner forms share an eleven-byte indexed header, eight zero bytes, `01 + u32 1`, `01 + u32 scope_record_index`, six zero bytes, `u32 local_ordinal`, a scalar envelope, `01 + u32 parameter_record_index`, six zero bytes, `u32 owned_ordinal`, four zero bytes, a repeated scope field, a companion reference with a seven-byte zero trailer, and a final scope reference with a six-byte zero trailer. The scalar envelope is `00 + f64`, `00 + 01 + u32`, `00 + 00 + u32`, `00 + u32`, or `01 + u32 0 + f64`. The repeated scope field is either `01 + u32 scope_record_index + eight zero bytes` or `01 + u32 scope_record_index + six zero bytes + 01 + u8 variant + 00`. `variant` is zero or one. The variant form occurs with the direct f64, marked u32, and marked-zero-plus-f64 envelopes. The compact `00 + u32` envelope has no variant. The final scope index also equals `scope_record_index`. The scalar value numerically equals the parameter record's evaluated scalar. The owner, parameter, and companion indices are consecutive in owner-parameter-companion, parameter-owner-companion, or owner-companion-parameter order. `(scope_record_index, local_ordinal)` is unique. `owned_ordinal` is a native ordering value and is not unique within a Design stream. The scope record is the owning sketch or construction operation.

The admitted legacy 68-byte owner form is a separate grammar. Its eleven-byte indexed header is followed by eight zero bytes, `u8 1`, thirteen zero bytes, `u8 1 + u32 parameter_record_index`, six zero bytes, `u32 owned_ordinal`, seven zero bytes, `u8 1 + u32 companion_record_index`, and eight zero bytes. It carries no scope or scalar lane: `scope_record_index` and `local_ordinal` are zero sentinels, and the evaluated scalar is the linked parameter's evaluated value. The owner, parameter, and companion indices are consecutive in that order. The decoder admits this form only for class tags `268`, `284`, `282`, `336`, `325`, and `297`; a 68-byte frame with another class tag is not an owner frame.

The admitted legacy 88-byte owner form uses the same prefix through `owned_ordinal`, then stores four zero bytes, `u8 1 + u32 scope_record_index`, eight zero bytes, `u8 1 + u32 companion_record_index`, seven zero bytes, and `u8 1 + u32 scope_record_index` with a six-byte zero trailer. It carries no scalar or local-ordinal lane: `local_ordinal` is zero, and the evaluated scalar is the linked parameter's evaluated value. The two scope references must match and be nonzero. The decoder admits this form only for class tags `284`, `282`, `336`, `325`, and `297`; a frame with another class tag is not an owner frame.

The scalar owner form is 104 bytes. It stores `u8 0 + f64` as its scalar carrier, uses a six-byte zero trailer after the repeated scope reference, and stores `u8 1 + u8 variant + u8 0` before the companion reference. The compact scalar owner is 103 bytes: it shares the scalar form through `owned_ordinal`, uses an eight-byte repeated-scope trailer, and omits the variant fields. The counted owner is 101 bytes and stores `u8 1 + u32 evaluated_count`; every subsequent field is three bytes earlier than in the scalar form. The compact counted owner is 99 bytes and stores the count as a bare u32 at offset 40, uses an eight-byte repeated-scope trailer, and omits the variant fields. The compact typed-counted owner is 100 bytes and stores `u8 1 + u32 evaluated_count` at offsets 40 and 41, uses an eight-byte repeated-scope trailer, and omits the variant fields. The tagged scalar owner is 107 bytes and stores `u8 1 + u32 0 + f64` beginning at offset 39, uses an eight-byte repeated-scope trailer, and omits the variant fields. The tagged scalar variant owner is 108 bytes. It has the same scalar carrier, uses a six-byte repeated-scope trailer, and stores the three-byte variant fields before the companion reference. The counted owner also permits `00 + 00 + u32 evaluated_count` as an equivalent spelling of `00 + 01 + u32 evaluated_count`; both six-byte scalar envelopes store the count at offset 41. The compact typed-counted owner uses the same marker choice at offsets 40 and 41.

The indexed companion record begins with a 58-byte common prefix. Its eleven-byte indexed header is followed by 20 zero bytes, `01 + u32 owner_record_index`, six zero bytes, a nonzero u64 Unix-epoch timestamp in microseconds, and eight zero bytes. The timestamp is a wall-clock value recorded at a parameter authoring event; it does not exceed the document's last save time and is preserved unchanged across subsequent saves. Its `record_index` equals the owner frame's `companion_record_index`. The companion-owned payload interval begins after this prefix and ends at the next indexed parameter record, parameter-owner header, parameter-scope primary header, or primary header referenced by a parameter scope other than the companion owner's scope in the same Design `BulkStream`. A terminating record immediately after the prefix denotes an empty companion payload. A primary header referenced by the owning scope remains inside the payload interval. Another indexed-record header can begin a nested companion record or the following sibling record; byte adjacency alone does not select between those roles. Construction-recipe records whose name fields begin within this interval belong to the companion and retain interval order. Every dimension parameter not used by a projected locus or construction-recipe constraint forms one native sketch constraint under the parameter owner's Sketch scope. The constraint retains the governed parameter and its companion record as a `companion_payload` operand when the payload is nonempty or a `companion` operand when it is empty. A locus frame's governing companion identity, not the companion containing that frame, selects the represented parameter. When a companion physically contains one or more typed dimension frames and no typed frame or recipe names that companion as governor, the payload is only a container for the following dimensions. Its owner remains a scalar parameter and does not form a second sketch constraint from the contained frames.

A `Radius Dimension*`, `Radial Dimension*`, or `Diameter Dimension*` parameter not consumed by a typed locus or recipe relation governs every circular entity in the parameter owner's Sketch whose measured radius or diameter equals the evaluated parameter value within the source kernel's absolute linear resolution. This rule applies when its companion payload is empty and when the payload contains only auxiliary relations or presentation data. `Radius Dimension*` and `Radial Dimension*` both govern radius. One matching entity establishes a radius or diameter constraint. Multiple matching entities establish a repeated radius or repeated diameter constraint controlled by the same parameter. No matching entity establishes a neutral radial relation.

A `Tangent Dimension*` parameter uses a length unit and stores its evaluated scalar in centimetres. Its state-zero counted-locus groups are auxiliary graph records; their owner role and solved loci determine any neutral relation, and the groups do not carry the tangent-distance parameter.

A `Linear Dimension*` parameter not consumed by a typed locus or recipe relation governs every line in the parameter owner's Sketch whose endpoint distance equals the evaluated length within the source kernel's absolute linear resolution. This rule applies when its companion payload is empty and when the payload contains only auxiliary relations or presentation data. One matching line establishes an endpoint-to-endpoint distance. Multiple matching lines establish a repeated-length constraint whose entities follow Sketch entity order. No matching line establishes a neutral line-length relation.

A `Linear Dimension*` parameter not resolved as a line length governs the unique pair of parallel line entities in the parameter owner's Sketch whose supporting-line separation equals the evaluated length. One matching pair establishes an entity distance. Zero or multiple matching pairs do not establish a neutral line-separation relation.

When multiple matching parallel-line pairs remain, a profile-local pair has exactly one intervening entity in the profile's cyclic boundary traversal. Two or more profile-local pairs establish one repeated parallel-line distance when no entity belongs to more than one pair. Pairs follow profile and boundary traversal order. An overlapping local candidate graph does not establish a neutral relation.

When the entity-pair rule does not establish a relation, collinear line entities form carriers within the source kernel's absolute linear resolution. The unique pair of parallel carriers whose perpendicular separation equals the evaluated length and whose bounded member spans overlap along their common direction establishes a parallel-line-set distance when at least one carrier contains multiple entities. Disjoint bounded segments may belong to one carrier. Carrier members follow Sketch entity order. Zero or multiple matching carrier pairs do not establish a neutral parallel-line-set relation.

A `Linear Dimension*` parameter not resolved by either line-only form governs the unique point-line pair in the parameter owner's Sketch whose perpendicular separation equals the evaluated length. Zero or multiple matching point-line pairs do not establish a neutral point-line relation.

A `Linear Dimension*` parameter not resolved by another typed relation governs the unique pair of solved point loci in the parameter owner's Sketch whose Euclidean separation equals the evaluated length within the source kernel's absolute linear resolution. Point entities coincident within that resolution form one solved locus. A horizontal or vertical pair establishes the corresponding directional distance; any other pair establishes a Euclidean locus distance. Zero or multiple matching solved-locus pairs do not establish a neutral point-distance relation.

A `Linear Dimension*` parameter not resolved by a line or point relation governs concentric full-circle pairs whose absolute radius difference equals the evaluated length. One pair forms a distance relation. Multiple pairs form a repeated-distance relation only when every circle belongs to exactly one matching pair. Overlapping candidate pairs do not establish a neutral circular-separation relation.

An `Angular Dimension-2` parameter not consumed by a typed locus relation governs the common direction of the unique state-zero parallel-line group in its companion. Both group loci must be bounded lines whose undirected angles from the positive Sketch `u` axis, or their supplements, equal the evaluated radian value within relative tolerance `1e-9`. The parameter drives the first locus's axis-angle constraint; the parallel group carries that direction to the second locus. Zero or multiple matching companion-local parallel groups do not establish a neutral axis-angle relation.

An `Angular Dimension*` parameter not consumed by a typed locus relation uses a preceding incident-pair proof when it follows its two line operands in Sketch serialization order. A preceding Sketch point identifies the operands when its companion names exactly two incident curve records, both records precede the parameter, and both resolve to bounded lines in the parameter owner's Sketch. The point establishes an angle relation when the lines' undirected angle, or its supplement, equals the evaluated radian value within relative tolerance `1e-9`. Exactly one distinct matching incident pair is required. A later point or curve does not participate in this proof.

A state-zero counted-locus frame for an `Angular Dimension*` parameter can contain one bounded line locus and one point locus. The line is the explicit angular operand. The point selects the unique other bounded line in the same Sketch that has the point as an endpoint and whose undirected angle, or supplement, with the explicit line equals the evaluated radian value within relative tolerance `1e-9`. The resolved two-line pair establishes the parameterized angle relation. A missing, mismatched, or non-unique candidate retains the counted relation without an inferred angular pair.

Within a dimensional companion, every contained construction-recipe family name belongs to the immediately preceding indexed record header. The containing record ends at the next indexed record header or at the end of the companion-owned payload. A nonempty recipe-specific prefix extends from the end of the eleven-byte indexed header to the u32 length field of the recipe-family name. The standard selector-bearing prefix stores ten zero bytes, `u32 1`, `u32 3`, a nonzero u32 value, and one or more persistent Design operands. An operand stores a nonzero u32 persistent-subentity selector, then its ASCII decimal-integer tag token as either LP-ASCII followed by `u32 0` or unframed ASCII followed by four zero bytes, then a nonzero u32 Design-reference count, that many nonzero u32 Design references, and `u32 0`. The token is one or more digits or ASCII minus `0x2D` followed by one or more digits. The first operand uses LP-ASCII. The operand sequence ends with either `u32 0` or the suffix `u32 1, u32 1, u64 0, u32 n`, `n` nonzero u32 references, and four or six zero bytes. Every selector, tag token, Design reference, and byte offset is retained in prefix order. Each operand joins to every active solved face or edge whose persistent-subentity tag contains the same selector, token, and Design reference; the candidate face and edge sets are independently ordered by entity identity and deduplicated. Faces and edges carrying the same token and Design reference under a different selector are retained in separate alternate-selector sets and never enter the exact candidate sets. The bytes after the family name through the containing-record boundary form a nonempty contiguous sequence of little-endian i32 values with no padding. The containing header's byte offset, dynamic class tag, record index, frame length, companion identity, recipe ordinal, complete prefix bytes and offset, program offset, and complete program are retained with the recipe identity. The indexed header, prefix, length-prefixed family name, and program partition the containing record without gaps.

A paired selector-bearing prefix stores ten zero bytes, `u32 1`, `u32 2`, a positive u32 pair count, and exactly that many adjacent operand pairs. The first operand of each pair stores a nonzero u32 selector, an unframed decimal-integer token followed by four zero bytes, a positive u32 Design-reference count, and that many nonzero u32 Design references without a terminator. The second operand stores the same selector, an LP-ASCII decimal-integer token, `u32 0`, the same count and ordered Design references, and a terminating `u32 0`. The prefix ends after the final second-operand terminator. A selector mismatch, a reference mismatch, another token frame, or trailing bytes invalidates the complete paired prefix.

A grouped selector-bearing prefix stores ten zero bytes, `u32 1`, a `u32 N` with `N >= 4`, and exactly `N` groups. Values `2` and `3` select the paired and standard prefix forms. Each group starts with a positive u32 operand count. Each operand stores a nonzero u32 selector, an unframed decimal-integer token followed by four zero bytes, a positive u32 Design-reference count, and that many nonzero u32 Design references without a local terminator. One final `u32 0` terminates the complete prefix. An empty group, a different group count, another token frame, an operand terminator, or trailing bytes invalidates the complete grouped prefix.

For a grouped `SurfacePatch` edge selection, each group member maps to the first recipe reference with a nonempty exact edge-candidate set. That set must contain exactly one edge, and the selected edge identities must be distinct across the complete group. A group that fails this one-to-one proof remains native.

Complete construction-recipe records in a non-locus dimension companion are independent native witness operands for one dimensional selection. No field combines records into an aggregate operation or assigns semantic order to them. Their construction-recipe kinds and programs can differ within one companion. The neutral projection uses the owner-scoped Sketch geometry and evaluated parameter to establish the dimensional relation; the result is one measurement or a repeated relation only under the rules below, not one relation per recipe. The decoder walks records in increasing recipe-family byte offset within the companion-owned payload for deterministic retention. `recipe_ordinal` is a decoder ordinal and is not serialized.

An edge recipe's words after its seven-word common prologue may occur as a contiguous subsequence of a dimension recipe program. Every such byte-identical containment joins the dimension recipe record to that edge operand. Matching operand identities are ordered and deduplicated; repeated equivalent edge recipes remain distinct operands.

A recipe-backed linear dimension whose parameter is not already projected through a governing locus frame measures solved locus pairs in its owning Sketch at the parameter's evaluated separation. A locus frame physically contained by the same companion does not supersede the recipes when that frame projects an independent geometric relation without the dimension parameter. Candidates are axis-aligned point pairs and parallel line pairs. The construction-recipe kind does not restrict the candidate geometry family. Point pairs produce horizontal or vertical point-locus distance according to their shared sketch coordinate. A parallel line pair produces an entity distance. One candidate is a single distance constraint. Multiple candidates form one repeated-distance constraint when they partition their participating entities into disjoint pairs; the pair order is point-pair discovery order followed by line-pair discovery order. A candidate graph that shares an entity between pairs is ambiguous and retains the ordered recipe operands as a native parameter-backed constraint. Zero candidates also retain the ordered recipe operands.

When that candidate graph is ambiguous, a detached point beyond an axis-aligned bounded-line endpoint establishes an extension distance if the detached point lies on the line's infinite carrier and the endpoint pair matches the evaluated separation along the carrier axis. The detached point is not an endpoint of any bounded line in the Sketch. Exactly one matching extension pair is required. Multiple matching extension pairs retain the recipe operands as a native parameter-backed constraint.

A recipe-backed `Radius Dimension*`, `Radial Dimension*`, or `Diameter Dimension*` parameter uses the same owner-scoped circular measurement rule as an empty radial companion. One measurement-matching circle or circular arc establishes a radius or diameter constraint. Multiple matches establish the corresponding repeated constraint. The recipe records remain the retained native construction graph and do not replace the circular measurement.

A paired-locus dimension frame occurs within a dimension companion's owned record interval and is delimited by two indexed headers with the same `record_index`. It may begin immediately after the 58-byte companion prefix or follow intermediate indexed records. Its primary header is followed by eight zero bytes, `01 + u32 3`, `01 + u32 0`, six zero bytes, an opaque u32, `01 + u32 first_geometry_record_index`, six zero bytes, a u32 first-role code, `01 + u32 second_geometry_record_index`, six zero bytes, and a u32 second-role code. Both geometry indices resolve to typed sketch-point or sketch-curve records. The paired header is followed after 59 bytes by the owner of the dimension parameter governed by the frame; that owner's companion identity, rather than the companion containing the frame, selects the parameter. A neutral two-locus measurement requires two distinct neutral entity identities. A linear pair forms a neutral parameter-backed measurement only when its solved geometry equals the evaluated parameter after centimetre-to-millimetre conversion: two points form a horizontal, vertical, or Euclidean locus distance, a point and line form their perpendicular separation, and two parallel lines form their perpendicular separation unless both role codes select the symmetric line-width form below. An angular pair of two lines forms a neutral angle only when their solved angle equals the evaluated radian value. Other role-dependent pairs retain their typed geometry, role codes, and parameter through the native constraint.

A paired-locus linear frame whose two loci are parallel lines and whose two role codes are nonzero is a symmetric line-width measurement. Its converted parameter equals twice the perpendicular separation of the two infinite line carriers. The typed parameter-backed distance uses the two line entities; a zero role on either locus does not select this symmetric form.

An annotated `EntityGenesis` dimension frame occurs either in the parameter-scope prefix before its first parameter companion or within a parameter companion's owned payload and is delimited by same-index primary and paired headers. A containing companion may belong to a dimension or feature input. The primary header is followed by eight zero bytes, `01 + u32 operand_count`, and `operand_count` entries of `01 + u32 geometry_record_index + six zero bytes + u32 role`; zero is the null locus and every nonzero index resolves to typed sketch geometry. The operand run is followed by `01 + u32 1`, LP-ASCII `EntityGenesis`, LP-ASCII `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64`, the u64 origin bitfield, and the annotation byte payload. A marked reference to the governed dimension's parameter-owner record ends that payload. Six zero bytes, a u32 return count, and the padded marked return-reference run follow. The return run is a permutation of the non-null operand indices, and zero padding extends to the paired header. The paired record stores eight zero bytes, `01 + u32 sketch_entity_suffix`, and six zero bytes after its eleven-byte header. The suffix resolves to the owning Sketch. The referenced parameter owner's companion, rather than the frame's physical interval, selects the governed parameter.

A direct dimension-presentation frame is a stream-local typed frame whose primary class is registered under type GUID `6CCF41D5-40BE-48ED-A834-18F3EAED6C57` or `8C780195-72C0-4a56-A911-E43AB14357F2`. Its indexed header is followed by eight zero bytes, `u8 1`, `u32 operand_count`, and `operand_count` entries of `u8 1 + u32 geometry_record_index + six zero bytes + u32 role`; the count is between one and 64, and every geometry index resolves to a Sketch point or curve in the same `BulkStream`. Opaque presentation bytes follow the operand run through a paired indexed header with the same `record_index`. The paired class is the stream-local class registered under type GUID `90055C05-546C-4EE7-B3C9-3DD922AD0C9C`; its body stores eight zero bytes, `u8 1`, `u32 owner_reference`, and six zero bytes. The first following dimension parameter-owner frame whose `scope_record_index` equals the scope record of the Sketch entity named by `owner_reference` governs the presentation frame; its parameter and companion identities are retained. The operand run is the authoritative measured-geometry selection. A one-circle or one-arc `Tangent Dimension*` frame establishes a radius constraint only when its radius equals the converted parameter. A line plus circle or arc establishes a tangent distance only when exactly one of the centerline-distance-plus-radius or absolute centerline-distance-minus-radius values equals the converted parameter. Two circles or arcs establish a tangent distance only when exactly one of the four absolute boundary combinations `d + r1 + r2`, `d - r1 - r2`, `d + r1 - r2`, and `d - r1 + r2` equals the converted parameter, where `d` is center distance. A one-line `Linear Dimension*` frame establishes endpoint distance, and a two-point `Linear Dimension*` frame establishes horizontal, vertical, or Euclidean point distance from the solved geometry. A missing or non-unique measurement retains the native dimensional constraint.

An annotated `Linear Dimension*` offset frame has one null locus and two non-null curve operands. Its return run contains those two curve indices exactly once. The curve with a null secondary persistent identity is the source; the curve with a non-null secondary persistent identity is the generated result. Both curves belong to the frame's owning Sketch. The single-source form has one null locus and one non-null source curve, and its one-entry return run names that source; the generated result is the unique same-Sketch curve with a non-null secondary identity whose solved parallel or concentric offset matches the parameter. In either form, a zero offset is invalid, the converted parameter magnitude and measured offset agree within `max(T, 1e-9 * (1 + max(|s|, |m|)))`, and the parameter factor is `1` for a positive stored value or `-1` for a negative stored value.

A parameter companion may own one or more non-overlapping counted-locus dimension frames in stream order. Each indexed header is followed by eight zero bytes, `01 + u32 locus_count`, and `locus_count` entries of `01 + u32 geometry_record_index + six zero bytes + u32 role`. The entries are followed by `00 01 + u32 sketch_entity_suffix + six zero bytes + u32 owner_role`, a u32 constraint-state mask, a second u32 equal to `locus_count`, and that many `01 + u32 geometry_record_index + six zero bytes` return entries. One zero byte ends the frame immediately before the next indexed header. Every geometry index resolves to a typed sketch-point or sketch-curve record. The return run is a permutation of the locus geometry indices. The sketch suffix resolves to a Sketch-typed Design entity. Paired-locus and counted-locus frames may coexist in one companion-owned interval; the counted form may repeat.

In a counted-locus linear offset frame, the owner role is zero, the constraint-state mask is `0x20`, and every locus and return reference resolves to a curve. The locus count is even, and its first and second halves have equal cardinality. In the role-partitioned form, the first half contains nonzero source-role codes and the second half contains role zero. In the identity-partitioned form, role codes in both halves are nonzero, every first-half curve has a null secondary persistent identity, and every second-half curve has a nonnull secondary persistent identity. The return run alternates one source-set reference and one result-set reference, defines their pairwise correspondence, and uses every locus exactly once. The source entries form the ordered source set and the result entries form the ordered generated set. Every solved pair has one common positive offset magnitude. An arc pair has the same center, equal nonzero sweep orientation, and overlapping angular intervals; trimming against adjacent offset curves can change its start and end angles. A full-circle pair has the same center, and its signed offset is the source radius minus the result radius. The positive converted dimension magnitude is the operation-level offset distance. The dimension parameter factor is `1` for a positive stored value and `-1` for a negative stored value. The generated set identifies the realized offset branch. Offset construction can change curve carriers and topology.

The counted-locus frame's counted references also satisfy the generic sketch-relation prefix grammar. A frame reached through a Dimension parameter companion is a dimension frame only; a generic sketch relation at the same Design `BulkStream` byte offset is not a second constraint.

A null-locus dimension frame has the same companion-owned interval rule and is delimited by primary and paired indexed headers with the same `record_index`. The primary header is followed by eight zero bytes, `01 + u32 2`, `01 + u32 0`, six zero bytes, a u32 null-role code, `01 + u32 geometry_record_index`, six zero bytes, and a u32 geometry-role code. A variable annotation payload extends from that fixed operand prefix to the paired header. The nonzero geometry index resolves to a typed sketch-point or sketch-curve record owned by the governed parameter scope's sketch. The paired header is followed after 59 bytes by the governed dimension parameter's owner. A companion-owned interval containing a null-locus frame contains no paired-locus or counted-locus frame. For an `Angular Dimension-2` parameter, null role `14` followed by line role `3` measures the line from the positive sketch-u axis. The line's undirected angle or its supplement must equal the evaluated parameter within relative tolerance `1e-9`; the parameter then drives a neutral axis-angle constraint.

A null-locus frame or counted-locus frame is a radial dimension when the parameter `source_kind` begins with `Radius Dimension`, `Radial Dimension`, or `Diameter Dimension`. The first two forms govern radius. One measurement-matching full circle or circular arc among the selected loci establishes a radius or diameter constraint. Multiple distinct matching circular loci establish a repeated radius or repeated diameter constraint in locus order; other loci carry annotation geometry. When a counted-locus frame contains no circular entity, a selected point can identify the unique measurement-matching circle or arc in the same Sketch whose center equals that point. A radius parameter's centimetre scalar multiplied by ten equals the solved millimetre radius; a diameter parameter's converted scalar equals twice that radius. Converted scalar `s` and measured value `m` agree when `|s - m| <= max(T, 1e-9 * (1 + max(|s|, |m|)))`, where `T` is the document linear tolerance in millimetres. No matching circular entity, a repeated circular identity, a nonfinite scalar, or a scalar/geometry mismatch retains the native dimensional relation.

A state-zero radial counted-locus frame with one point and one bounded line can be an extension annotation. The point lies on the line's infinite carrier, and the two loci do not identify the governed circular entity. When every otherwise unresolved counted frame for the companion has this form, the parameter governs the owner Sketch's unique measurement-matching circle or circular arc. The extension frames do not form additional constraints.

When a paired-locus frame's two typed loci completely determine a linear or angular constraint, that frame is the companion's governing dimensional relation. Counted-locus frames in the same companion are auxiliary graph records for that relation, not additional dimensions; they retain their native roles and order without duplicating the parameter-backed constraint. A governing paired-locus, null-locus, or recipe-backed dimension has a neutral constraint identity formed from its stable parameter identity and frame-form discriminator. Relocating either indexed frame does not change that identity. Repeated counted-locus auxiliary relations retain distinct frame identities.

For a model-space Sketch, a linear paired-locus frame whose two loci resolve to distinct point entities measures their Euclidean model-space separation. The parameter drives a neutral spatial point-distance constraint when its absolute evaluated length equals that separation within relative tolerance `1e-9`. Both points must belong to the frame's owning spatial Sketch. A non-length parameter, non-point locus, cross-Sketch pair, or scalar/geometry mismatch retains the native dimensional relation.

A state-zero counted-locus group owned by a spatial Sketch with owner role `0x400` is a reflection-symmetry relation. Its three loci contain exactly one nondegenerate bounded line and two distinct point entities. The infinite carrier of the line is the reflection axis: the point midpoint lies on that carrier and the point-to-point vector is perpendicular to it. The group is an auxiliary relation and does not carry the companion's dimensional parameter.

The constraint identity key contains the byte-length-prefixed key portion of the neutral parameter identity followed by the byte-length-prefixed frame-form discriminator. The parameter identity's namespace and `#` separator are not nested inside the constraint key.

Except for the angular line-and-point dimension form, state-zero counted-locus groups are first classified as auxiliary graph relations independently of the companion's dimension family. Coincident point pairs, coincident point-on-curve loci, centered circular or elliptical pairs, perpendicular or parallel line pairs, collinear line pairs, and three-locus reflection symmetry are determined by typed sketch geometry. Distinct circular or elliptical members with matching centers resolve as `Concentric`; two circular members with matching centers and radii resolve as `Coradial`. A reflection group contains one bounded-line axis and two points, bounded lines, equal-radius full circles, or equal-radius circular arcs that map onto each other across the axis carrier. Reflected arcs have equal sweep magnitude and reflected endpoint sets. Owner role `0x40` on one constant-`v` line is horizontal and owner role `0x80` on one constant-`u` line is vertical. Owner role `0x100` on one bounded line and one circular arc is tangent when they share a bounded endpoint and the line direction is perpendicular to the radius there. On two distinct circular entities, including full circles and bounded circular arcs, `0x100` is tangent when their supporting circles are externally or internally tangent and the tangent point lies in each bounded arc's angular sweep; a full circle accepts every tangent point. Owner role `0x800` on two lines, two circular entities, or two ellipses is equal when their solved nonzero lengths, radii, or pair of semiaxes agree respectively. Point-on-curve membership includes bounded lines, circular arcs, elliptic arcs, and nonperiodic NURBS curves over their knot domain; a full circle or ellipse uses its entire parameter domain. Auxiliary relations do not carry the dimensional parameter. Only a state-zero two-locus group that is not an auxiliary relation can carry a linear companion's distance parameter.

For a linear companion governed only by counted-locus frames, a two-point group is the governing directional-distance relation when exactly one of its absolute coordinate differences equals the evaluated parameter. A match on the `u` difference is horizontal distance and a match on the `v` difference is vertical distance. Matching both coordinate differences is directionally ambiguous and does not transfer as a typed dimension. The evaluated parameter is stored in centimeters and the sketch coordinates are millimeters. A remaining state-zero group with exactly two typed loci is the parameter-backed distance relation.

A counted linear frame with state mask `0x20` stores an offset graph. Its locus list uses one of the source/result partitions defined above. Every locus resolves to a bounded line or circular arc. The return run alternates one source index and one result index, uses every locus exactly once, and supplies the source/result bijection. A line pair has parallel directions and constant signed separation. An arc pair has the same center, equal nonzero sweep orientation, and overlapping angular intervals; trimming against adjacent offset curves can change its start and end angles. Its signed separation follows the sweep's left normal and is the signed difference from source radius to result radius. Every pair has the same positive separation magnitude. A pair whose result lies on the source curve's right side reverses the source traversal before applying the common distance along its left normal. When the common magnitude equals the companion parameter's magnitude after centimetre-to-millimetre conversion, the parameter drives the offset relation. The distance-to-parameter factor is `1` for a positive parameter and `-1` for a negative parameter. A null-locus frame governing that companion is the offset dimension's annotation frame and does not form a second constraint.

An angular paired- or counted-locus frame may encode one line and one point instead of two lines. The point is an endpoint indirection. Among lines owned by the same sketch and incident to that point at a stored endpoint, exactly one forms either the stored angle or its supplement with the explicit line. That unique incident line replaces the point in the parameter-backed angular constraint. No line is selected when endpoint incidence and the evaluated radian value do not determine one unique candidate.

A parameter scope is one logical indexed record delimited by two headers carrying the same `record_index`. Both three-digit class tags are per-file dynamic values. The primary header begins the scope payload. In fixed forms, an LP-UTF16 feature-family name ends exactly 72, 76, 77, 78, or 87 bytes before the paired header. Immediately before the LP-UTF16 field are a u32 history-state identity and an ordered reference table. The table is `u32 reference_count` followed by `reference_count` entries of `01 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes`; it ends at the history-state identity. The fixed tail starts with a nonzero u32 ordinal among scopes of the same feature family. Its preceding history-state identity is at tail offset 30 in the 72- and 76-byte forms, tail offset 31 in the 77- and 78-byte forms, and tail offset 41 in the 87-byte form. The 82-byte tail of the 347-byte `JointOrigin` form and the 104-byte tail of the 705-byte `Assemble` form have no preceding history-state identity. A history-state identity is either `0xffffffff` for null or the `state_id` of an ASM delta-state record; the current and preceding identities are null together. When one scope's preceding identity equals another scope's current identity, the former follows and depends on the latter in the modeling history. Every member resolves to the primary header of an indexed Design record in the same stream. The feature-family name is localized. `Sketch`, `Esquisse`, `Skizze`, and `Esboço` select Sketch; `Extrude`, `Extrusion`, and `Extrusão` select Extrude; `Fillet`, `Congé`, `Abrundung`, and `Arredondamento` select Fillet; `Chamfer` and `Chanfrein` select Chamfer; and `Shell` and `Schale` select Shell. The serialized token remains the native scope kind and participates verbatim in configuration naming. A named parameter scope has a variable tail. After the feature-family name, it stores a nonzero u32 ordinal, a zero u32, an LP-UTF16 label of `L` code units at tail offset 8, seven zero bytes, and a 59-byte lane block at tail offset `19 + 2L`; its complete tail is `78 + 2L` bytes and it has no preceding history-state identity. The lane block carries one repeated u64 lane value `V`, where `V` is zero or one. It is `01 + u8 field_id + u64 V + two zero bytes + positive u32 + u32 0xfc + finite f64 + u32 0xfc`, followed by `01 + u8 field_id + u64 V + 00 01 00 00`, followed by `01 + u8 field_id + u64 V + three zero bytes`; each `field_id` is nonzero. A `Sketch` scope in a stream whose sketch entity headers use the fixed form contains exactly one `01 + u32 entity_suffix + six zero bytes` reference to its Sketch-typed Design entity header; this joins that scope form to the sketch container and its relation graph. An `Esquisse` scope, and a `Sketch` scope in a stream whose sketch entity headers use the `EntityGenesis` form, uses the generic ordered reference table. The `EntityGenesis`-form `Sketch` scope's payload before the table carries one `01 + u32 entity_suffix + six zero bytes` reference to its sketch entity header and one `01 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes` reference to its placement record, whose index also appears in the ordered table. Before insertion into a neutral textual key, an identity component replaces each UTF-8 byte of `#`, `%`, or a whitespace code point with uppercase `%HH`; other code points remain unchanged. A neutral feature identity consists of the encoded Design stream component and encoded feature-family component, each prefixed by its encoded UTF-8 byte length, followed by the family-local scope ordinal and the scope's indexed record identity. The indexed record identity disambiguates equal family-local ordinals carried by separate component histories in one Design stream. Its canonical configuration name is the unescaped feature-family name, one ASCII space, and the decimal family-local ordinal; the indexed record identity does not participate in configuration naming.

A `Canvas` scope names its image geometry from one of two fixed prologues. The expanded prologue has nine zero bytes at scope offsets 11 through 19, a marked null u32 at offset 20, and a marked geometry-record reference at offset 25. The compact prologue has ten zero bytes at offsets 11 through 20 and the marked geometry-record reference at offset 21. The referenced geometry record stores a 15-byte prologue after its eleven-byte indexed header. Its first ten bytes and bytes 11 through 13 are zero, byte 10 is zero or one, and byte 14 is the Canvas visibility Boolean. Four f64 values at record offsets 26, 34, 42, and 50 form the first plane-local boundary segment. A marked supporting-plane entity suffix occurs at offset 58 with six trailing zero bytes. The plane entity belongs to a Body or Geometry Design object. A 77-byte fixed payload occupies offsets 69 through 145. Its first four bytes are a normalized finite f32 opacity followed by one zero byte. Three consecutive f64 triples begin at payload offsets 5, 29, and 53. The first triple is the model-space plane origin in centimetres; the second and third triples are orthogonal unit directions for increasing plane-local u and v. Marked references at record offsets 146, 157, and 169 name the Canvas scope, owning component entity suffix, and image-asset record; their trailing fields contain six, seven, and six zero bytes respectively. The image-asset reference is followed by `01` at offset 180 and four f64 values at offsets 181, 189, 197, and 205 forming the opposite boundary segment. The two segments are parallel opposite edges of a nondegenerate axis-aligned rectangle within floating-point roundoff. An LP-UTF16 persistent label begins at offset 213 and ends at the paired same-index geometry header. The paired header is followed by eight zero bytes and a marked reference to the same owning component with six trailing zero bytes. A standalone image-asset record follows immediately; its eleven-byte indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes and an LP-UTF16 archive-entry basename ending at the Canvas scope header. The basename uniquely identifies an entry in `Images.BlobParts`. Plane-local boundary coordinates are stored in centimetres.

The ordered Canvas boundary segments encode raster mirroring. For horizontal segments, decreasing u within each segment mirrors image u, and decreasing v from the first segment to the second mirrors image v. For vertical segments, decreasing u from the first segment to the second mirrors image u, and decreasing v within each segment mirrors image v.

A `Decal` scope is a presentation timeline object and has no modeling-history state or geometry outputs. Ten zero bytes occupy scope offsets 11 through 20. A marked image-asset record reference occurs at offset 21 with six trailing zero bytes. Mapping mode `0x60` at offset 32 scales the complete raster to the selected faces' native parameter domain. A marked target-group reference occurs at offset 33 with six trailing zero bytes. The target is a single-member construction-operand group with role `0x0000000400000000`. The member is a whole-body recipe envelope whose persistent reference clauses identify the decal target faces. The member's nested `body_recipe_data` records precede its enclosing `N+4` boundary.

The referenced Decal image asset has two consecutive indexed records. The primary record is 30 bytes. Eight zero bytes occupy offsets 11 through 18, followed by a marked Fusion Design entity suffix at offset 19 and six zero bytes at offsets 24 through 29. The image-name record begins at offset 30 and has the primary record index plus one. Ten zero bytes occupy its offsets 11 through 20. An LP-UTF16 archive-entry basename begins at offset 21 and ends at the next indexed-record header. The basename uniquely identifies an entry in `Images.BlobParts`.

`CopyPasteBodies` uses the same scope framing with a 110-byte tail instead of the 78-byte tail. Its family-local ordinal is at tail offset 0 and its preceding history-state identity is at tail offset 53. Primary-header offsets 29 and 40 carry marked references to a counted body group and a source-to-copy relation record. The body group has ten zero bytes after its indexed header, followed by `u32 N` and `N` marked body-operand record indices; these are the scope reference table's members after the group record. The relation record has eight zero bytes after its indexed header, a marked u32 value `2N`, and `N` ordered source/copy body-entity-suffix pairs. Each source suffix is a marked u32 with ten trailing zero bytes. Each copied suffix is a marked u32 with six trailing zero bytes; four zero alignment bytes separate it from a following pair. Source and copied suffixes are distinct. A copied suffix joins through the active Design body map to the independent neutral body introduced by the operation.

`Component Insert` has one ordered reference and a generation-specific scope prologue. A 399-byte class-`259` frame has nine zero bytes after its primary header, `01 00 00 00 00`, an eight-byte occurrence identity, four zero bytes, a marked reference at offset 37, six zero bytes, `01 00`, and a row-major rigid transform at offset 50. A 381-byte class-`261` frame has the same fields through the marked reference, six zero bytes, `01`, and the transform at offset 49. A 395-byte class-`258` frame has ten zero bytes after its primary header, an eight-byte occurrence identity, four zero bytes, a marked reference at offset 33, six zero bytes, `01 00`, and the transform at offset 46. A 404-byte frame has nine zero bytes after its primary header, `01 00 00 00 00`, four zero bytes, an eight-byte occurrence identity, four zero bytes, a marked reference at offset 41, six zero bytes, `01 00`, and the transform at offset 54. The marked reference equals the scope's sole ordered reference. The first three forms name a 57-byte relation record. That record has ten zero bytes after its indexed header and marked references at offsets 21, 34, and 46; their zero tails are eight, seven, and six bytes. The first names the grouped component-occurrence carrier, and the third is a back-reference to the scope. The 404-byte form names a 58-byte relation record with ten zero bytes after its indexed header and marked references at offsets 21, 35, and 47; their zero tails are six, seven, and six bytes. The first names the grouped component-occurrence carrier, and the third is a back-reference to the scope. The carrier class tag is segment-local and does not select the carrier. The exact scope frame and its relation record select it. In the first three forms, the carrier stores an LP-UTF16 occurrence-role GUID followed by two zero bytes and a rigid transform equal to the scope transform. A class-`288` carrier stores consecutive identity and occurrence-role GUIDs, a linked-asset GUID, a qualified asset identity, the equal transform, and the repeated qualified identity. The 404-byte carrier placement stores the equal transform before an LP-UTF16 occurrence-role GUID, followed by `00 01 06 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00`. When the container supplies the external-reference table, the role and transform select exactly one occurrence and the scope projects as an inserted-component feature naming that occurrence. Scope-bound carrier transforms are the complete occurrence set for the same role in the same Design stream; matching placement records do not add occurrences. If no scope-bound carrier exists, each matching placement record supplies one occurrence. An absent external-reference table retains the decoded construction without an occurrence projection.

The compact identity form is a 261-byte class-`296` frame paired with class `263`. Relative to its primary indexed header, it has nine zero bytes at offsets 11 through 19, `01 00 00 00 00` at offsets 20 through 24, an occurrence identity u64 at offset 25, four zero bytes at offsets 33 through 36, a marked reference to the sole relation at offset 37, six zero bytes at offsets 42 through 47, bytes `01 01` at offsets 48 and 49, u32 code-unit count 36 at offset 50, and the 36-code-unit null GUID `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` at offset 54. It stores no scope transform; its transform is the identity.

The identity form names the same 57-byte relation record as the 381-, 395-, and 399-byte forms. Its first marked reference names a class-`382` grouped identity carrier and its third references the scope. The carrier is 695 bytes from its primary indexed header to the relation header. It has eight zero bytes at offsets 11 through 18, byte `1` at offset 19, u32 `1` at offset 20, byte `1` at offset 24, an occurrence identity u64 at offset 25, byte `1` at offset 33, and four zero bytes at offsets 34 through 37. At offset 38 it stores an LP-UTF16 component GUID, one zero separator byte, an LP-ASCII type GUID, and an LP-UTF16 occurrence-role GUID. The role is followed by `00 01 00 00 00 00 01 00 00 00`, two LP-UTF16 metadata GUIDs, and `00 01 03 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 01 00 00 00 00`. It then repeats the component GUID, separator byte, type GUID, and role GUID, followed by `00 01 00 00 00 00`, a final role GUID, and `00 01 04 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00`. Every GUID has 36 code units or ASCII bytes, repeated component, type, and role GUIDs are equal, and the carrier has no matrix. Its placement is the identity.

The class-`283` / class-`262` generation has two exact scope frames. In both forms, `byte_offset` is the primary class-`283` indexed header and the paired class-`262` indexed header begins at `byte_offset + frame_length`. Both store ten zero bytes at offsets 11 through 20, an occurrence identity u64 at offset 21, four zero bytes at offsets 29 through 32, a marked relation reference at offset 33 with its u32 value at offset 34, and six zero bytes at offsets 38 through 43. The 257-byte form stores `01 01` at offsets 44 and 45, an LP-UTF16 null component GUID with code-unit count 36 at offset 46 and payload at offset 50, three zero bytes at offsets 122 through 124, one ordered reference at offset 125 with marker 1 at offset 129 and the relation index at offset 130, six zero bytes at offsets 134 through 139, and the current history-state identity `0xffffffff` at offset 140. Its kind count is 16 at offset 144 and its `Component Insert` payload starts at offset 148. The feature ordinal is at offset 180 and the preceding history-state identity is at offset 211. The 385-byte form stores `01 00` at offsets 44 and 45, a row-major rigid transform of 16 little-endian f64 values at offset 46, the null component GUID count at offset 174 and payload at offset 178, three zero bytes at offsets 250 through 252, one ordered reference at offset 253 with marker 1 at offset 257 and the relation index at offset 258, six zero bytes at offsets 262 through 267, and the current history-state identity `0xffffffff` at offset 268. Its kind count is 16 at offset 272 and its `Component Insert` payload starts at offset 276. The feature ordinal is at offset 308 and the preceding history-state identity is at offset 339. The remaining fixed tail bytes occupy the spans between these typed lanes and the paired header; the frame lengths are 257 and 385 bytes.

The relation for both class-`283` forms is the 57-byte three-reference form described above. Its first reference names a class-`334` carrier. A class-`334` carrier stores a 36-code-unit component identity as an LP-UTF16 field at offset 38, then a direct UTF-16LE neutron-role payload at offset 159. The role payload ends at a ten-byte discriminator tail consisting of byte `0`, a nonzero marker byte, four zero bytes, and a nonzero u32; a second 36-code-unit LP-UTF16 identity follows that tail. The class-`334` carrier has no placement matrix. The 257-byte scope supplies the identity placement, and the 385-byte scope supplies the placement matrix at offset 46.

The Component Insert generation map is:

| Primary / paired class | Scope length | Scope transform | Relation length | Carrier placement |
| --- | ---: | --- | ---: | --- |
| dynamic / 259 | 399 | matrix at +50 | 57 | role GUID, zero separator, equal matrix |
| dynamic / 261 | 381 | matrix at +49 | 57 | role GUID, zero separator, equal matrix; class 288 also supplies the legacy carrier |
| dynamic / 258 | 395 | matrix at +46 | 57 | role GUID, zero separator, equal matrix |
| dynamic / any | 404 | matrix at +54 | 58 | equal matrix, role GUID, placement marker |
| 296 / 263 | 261 | implicit identity | 57 | grouped class 382 identity carrier |
| 283 / 262 | 257 | implicit identity | 57 | class 334 carrier: direct role UTF-16, no matrix |
| 283 / 262 | 385 | matrix at +46 | 57 | class 334 carrier: direct role UTF-16, no matrix |


A local component occurrence is an indexed carrier with a 229-byte unplaced or 357-byte placed frame. The carrier class tag is a per-file dynamic value and does not select the frame; the frame length and its fixed members identify the carrier. Each frame stores the one-based occurrence ordinal at offset 40, an LP-UTF16 component-definition GUID at offset 44, and an LP-UTF16 occurrence GUID at offset 120. Marked component-record references at offsets 24 and 197 carry the same u64 value within one carrier. The unplaced frame has ordinal one. The placed frame appends a row-major rigid local-to-model transform at offset 209. A class-`256` carrier uses a placed frame only for an ordinal greater than one; any other class tag can use a placed frame for ordinal one. Matrix translations are in centimetres. Occurrence GUIDs are unique within a Design stream. The component-definition GUID is the reusable local-definition identity across carriers; the component-record reference is carrier provenance and may differ between carriers.

Legacy `CopyPaste` is a local component-occurrence operation. Its scope has one ordered reference and rigid source and copied placements 156 bytes apart. The 529-byte frame places them at offsets 38 and 194; the 525-byte frame omits one four-byte prologue field and places them at offsets 34 and 190. The ordered reference names the same 57-byte relation shape used by component insertion; its first reference names the generated class-`256` occurrence carrier and its third references the scope. The generated carrier's component GUID selects exactly one earlier ordinal-1 carrier for the source occurrence, and its transform equals the copied scope transform. The operation projects the copied occurrence as an inserted-component feature while retaining both source and copied placements in product structure.

When a one-direction `R-Pattern` repeats a local component occurrence, every generated placement after the seed is the transform of one generated class-`256` carrier. Those carriers share one component GUID and have consecutive one-based occurrence ordinals. The unique earlier ordinal-1 carrier with that component GUID is the seed occurrence; the pattern scope's first serialized instance transform supplies its placement. The neutral pattern seed is that occurrence and the remaining placements are product occurrences of the same local component.

Scope framing is independent of parameter ownership. A scope with no owned parameters has no parameter-owner backlink and is still identified by its paired indexed headers, feature-family field, reference table, state identities, and family-local ordinal.

`SpirePrimitive` selects the Coil feature family. A `SpirePrimitive` scope stores its result operation at primary-header offset 20 with values `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, and `4 = new body`. Offset 24 is the clockwise Boolean. Offset 26 is u32 `2`. Offset 30 selects the driving dimensions: `1 = revolutions and height`, `2 = revolutions and pitch`, `3 = height and pitch`, and `4 = planar spiral by revolutions and radial pitch`. Offset 92 selects the section: `0 = circle`, `1 = square`, `2 = external triangle`, and `3 = internal triangle`. Offset 107 value `4` places the section inside the reference trajectory. Every form has exactly one `Diameter` and one `SectionSize` parameter. Axial forms additionally have exactly one `TaperAngle` and exactly the two parameters named by their extent. The spiral has exactly one `Revolutions` and one `Pitch` and no taper parameter. Join, cut, and intersect require one construction-operand group with role `0x0000000800000000`; new body has no such group.

`CoilPrimitive` is the compact Coil dialect. Its scope has seven or eight ordered references. Offset 20 value `1` creates a new body, offset 24 is the clockwise Boolean, and offset 26 is u32 `4`. Offset 30 selects the driving dimensions: `1 = revolutions and height`, `2 = revolutions and pitch`, `3 = height and pitch`, and `4 = planar spiral by revolutions and radial pitch`. Offset 92 selects the section position: `1 = inside`, `2 = on center`, and `3 = outside`. Offset 107 selects the section shape: `1 = circle`, `2 = square`, `3 = external triangle`, and `4 = internal triangle`. Its first two references retain the placement construction. An axial form has five parameter references for `Diameter`, `SectionSize`, `TaperAngle`, `Revolutions`, and its extent parameter, followed by a counted selection group with exactly one member. A spiral has four parameter references for `Diameter`, `SectionSize`, `Revolutions`, and `Pitch`, followed by the same selection group.
