The selection group member is a persistently identified entity record carrying the same asset and context GUIDs as the placement identity. The result state inserts one body without replacing or updating any preceding body.
The compact new-body form has a 411-byte, 432-byte, or 442-byte scope frame. The 411-byte form is the seven-reference spiral form and carries a preceding history-state identity. The 432-byte form has a 78-byte trailer after the feature-family string; the 442-byte form has an 88-byte trailer. The 432- and 442-byte forms carry no preceding history-state identity.

The legacy eight-reference new-body form is a class-`393` scope paired with class `258` and has a 427-byte frame. Its second placement reference is a class-`395` frame paired with class `258` and has a 186-byte span. Relative to that carrier's primary indexed header, bytes 11 through 47 are zero, offset 48 is a marked reference with index zero, bytes 53 through 58 and 59 through 75 are zero, offset 76 is u32 `2`, bytes 80 through 83 are zero, and offset 84 is u32 `1`. A marked reference at offset 88 names the first placement reference and has six zero trailing bytes; bytes 99 through 100 are zero and offset 101 is u32 `1`. A second marked reference at offset 105 is nonzero and distinct from the selection, transform, and owning-scope record indices, with six zero trailing bytes. Bytes 116 through 119 are zero, offset 120 is u32 `4`, bytes 124 through 133 are zero, offset 134 is u32 `109`, offset 138 is a positive finite f64, and offset 146 is u32 `109`. Marked references at offsets 150 and 163 name the transform record plus two and plus one, respectively; their six-byte trailing fields are zero. Byte 174 is zero, and the marked reference at offset 175 names the owning Coil scope with six zero trailing bytes. The carrier has no matrix and represents the identity transform.

The modern eight-reference new-body form is a class-`353` scope paired with class `259` and has a 427-byte frame. Its second placement reference is a class-`450` carrier paired with class `259` and has a 315-byte span. Relative to that carrier's primary indexed header, bytes 11 through 49 are zero and sixteen row-major f64 matrix values begin at offset 50. Bytes 178 through 203 are zero, offset 204 is u32 `512`, bytes 208 through 211 are zero, and offset 212 is u32 `256`. A marked reference at offset 217 names the first placement reference and has six zero trailing bytes. Bytes 228 and 229 are zero, offset 230 is u32 `1`, and a marked reference at offset 234 names the transform record plus 25 and has six zero trailing bytes. Bytes 245 through 247 are zero, offset 248 is u64 `1024`, and offset 256 is u64 `0x7000000000000000`. Bytes 264 through 267 are zero, and offset 268 is a nonzero u64 whose most-significant byte is `0x70`; bytes 276 through 278 are zero. Marked references at offsets 279, 292, and 304 name the transform record plus two, the transform record plus one, and the owning Coil scope, respectively; their six-byte trailing fields are zero, with two zero bytes at offsets 290 through 291 and one zero byte at offset 303. The matrix is finite, orthonormal, right-handed, and has final row `(0, 0, 0, 1)`; its translation column is in centimetres and its columns zero and two are the radial direction and trajectory axis. This carrier supplies the explicit Coil placement when the first selection carrier is complete.

A nested persistent entity-selection identity has a 45-byte expanded form or a 29-byte compact form from its identity header to the following indexed header. Both forms store ten zero bytes at identity offsets 11 through 20. The compact form stores one u64 identity at offset 21. The expanded form stores the selected Sketch entity suffix at offset 29 and the selected Sketch curve's primary identity at offset 37; its following record index is the selection record index plus four. When the u64 at offset 21 is nonzero in the expanded form, it is the selected curve's secondary identity and zero is the null value. Thus a SplitFace path member carries `(curve secondary, Sketch suffix, curve primary)` in the three u64 slots, while a generic entity member carries the pair at offsets 29 and 37. The asset and context UUIDs qualify every identity in the frame.

In the complete eight-reference compact form, the first placement reference is a nested support-selection frame. Its persistent selection identity carries the asset and context UUIDs and the ordered persistent identity pair; the eighth scope reference names the same selected entity. The persistent prefix stores zero bytes at offsets 11 through 20, u8 `1` at offset 21, the nested record index at offset 22, zero bytes at offsets 26 through 31, u32 `1` at offset 32, and the asset UUID's UTF-16 code-unit count at offset 36. The modern eight-reference new-body form uses a 41-byte selection prefix: bytes 11 through 21 are zero, offset 22 is u8 `1`, offset 23 is the nested record index, bytes 27 through 32 are zero, offset 33 is u32 `1`, and offset 37 is the asset UUID's UTF-16 code-unit count. The context UUID follows the asset UUID. A face-recipe selection frame uses the same asset and context UUID prefix and carries one exact face construction recipe instead of the persistent identity pair. Its prefix stores zero bytes at offsets 11 through 22, u8 `1` at offset 23, the nested record index at offset 24, zero bytes at offsets 28 through 33, u8 `1` at offset 34, zero bytes at offsets 35 through 37, and the asset UUID's UTF-16 code-unit count at offset 38. The modern prefix is also used by the modern face-recipe form. In both prefixes the context UUID is followed by u32 `2` and four zero bytes. The second placement reference is a rigid frame carrier. Its marker at offset 55 is u8 `1`. The identity carrier is 213 bytes to its same-index paired header, stores zero bytes at offsets 56 through 64, stores u8 `1` at offset 65, and omits the matrix; it represents the identity transform. The explicit carrier is 341 bytes to its same-index paired header, stores zero bytes at offsets 56 through 64, stores u8 `0` at offset 65, and stores 16 row-major f64 values at offset 66. Both forms have a finite orthonormal right-handed basis and final row `(0, 0, 0, 1)`. Matrix columns zero and two are the radial direction and trajectory axis. The translation column is in centimetres. The neutral origin converts that column to millimetres; the axis and radial vectors remain unit directions. A placement is explicit only when both carriers and their selection semantics satisfy these invariants; otherwise the placement remains a native Coil construction.

The 442-byte compact scope form also has a 233-byte owner-referenced identity carrier. It has the same identity marker block as the 213-byte carrier. Its bytes 213 through 221 are zero, offset 222 is a marked reference, the u32 at offset 223 equals the owning Coil scope record index, and bytes 227 through 232 are zero. It represents the identity transform. The owner reference is part of the carrier and must resolve to the containing scope before the placement is typed.

The ten-reference `CoilPrimitive` form has a 450-byte Boolean, a 572-byte matrix Boolean, or a 578-byte matrix new-body scope frame. All forms store eleven zero bytes at offsets 11 through 21, a u32 at offset 22, and u32 `1` at offset 26. The 450-byte form maps offset 22 values `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, and `3 = intersect`. The 572-byte form stores a finite 16-value f64 matrix at offset 77 for Boolean operations, and the 578-byte form stores the same matrix for new body. The 572-byte form maps the same three operation values; the 578-byte form maps offset 22 value `2` to new body. Each matrix has final row `(0, 0, 0, 1)`. Marked references at offsets 30 and 41 repeat the fifth and ninth ordered scope references. The form fixes a circular section inside the trajectory and counterclockwise angular travel; it has no fixed extent selector. The owned parameter source kinds select the extent: `Diameter`, `SectionSize`, `TaperAngle`, `Revolutions`, and `Height` select revolutions-and-height; replacing `Height` with `Pitch` selects revolutions-and-pitch; replacing `Revolutions` with `Pitch` selects height-and-pitch; and the four kinds `Diameter`, `SectionSize`, `Revolutions`, and `Pitch` select the spiral. Its first ordered reference is a role-`0x0000000400000000` construction group with one body-recipe member. Boolean forms use that group as their target; the new-body form retains it as generated-body construction and does not use it as a target.

A `Combine` scope stores one of three primary prologues. All offsets are relative to the primary indexed header. The standard prologue stores nine zero bytes at offsets 11 through 19, the Boolean operation u32 at offset 20, zero at byte 24, the keep-tools Boolean at offset 25, and seven zero bytes at offsets 26 through 32. The class-387 form paired with class 258 has a 314-byte token-independent frame. Its compact prologue stores ten zero bytes at offsets 11 through 20, the operation at offset 21, the keep-tools Boolean at offset 25, three zero bytes, `01 00`, u32 one at offset 31, and a marked nonzero same-segment u64 reference at offset 35 with a two-byte zero trailing field. The class-329 form paired with class 261 has a 363-byte frame. Its extended-reference prologue stores eighteen zero bytes at offsets 11 through 28, byte one at offset 29, the keep-tools Boolean at offset 30, the operation at offset 31, and a marked nonzero same-segment u64 reference at offset 35 with a two-byte zero trailing field. Operation values are `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, and `3 = intersect`.

The ordered reference table of a `Combine` scope has an even count of at least four. Each selected body occupies two consecutive references: an operation-identity record containing a marked reference to the following body-selection record, followed by that body-selection record containing two consecutive GUID strings and one bounded whole-body recipe. Exactly one operation-identity record uses the compact form with ten zero bytes, u32 one, and the marked selection reference after its indexed header; that pair selects the target body. Every other operation-identity record uses the expanded metadata form containing `DcFeatureOperationIdFlag` and `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64`; those pairs select the tools in source-table order. The neutral operation stores the target before the ordered tools independently of their serialized scope positions.

A tool body-selection record with primary index `N` can carry a cross-document body-selector path. Its indexed header is followed by fourteen zero bytes, a same-segment reference to `N+3`, u32 one, consecutive LP-UTF16 asset and context GUIDs, and u32 values `2`, `0`, and `1`. A nonzero same-segment occurrence reference and u32 one follow. The cross-document body reference is byte one, a nonzero u64 body reference, byte one, a u32 segment, an LP-UTF16 asset GUID equal to the selector asset GUID, byte zero, and a nonempty LP-UTF16 link name. Its optional-tail byte is zero for no tail. Value one adds an LP-UTF16 property GUID and a nonempty LP-UTF16 version URN. The selector tail is u32 `9`, u16 `2`, a retained u64 value, u32 `48`, a second retained u64 value, a same-segment reference to `N+2`, two zero bytes, a same-segment reference to `N+1`, one zero byte, and a same-segment reference to the owning scope. The same-index paired header starts immediately after the scope reference. The persistent external body identity contains the selector asset and context GUIDs, occurrence reference, external body reference and segment, external asset GUID, link name, and optional property and version strings.

A `Face` scope has an ordered source-shape carrier. The carrier's primary and paired indexed records are consecutive entries in the scope reference table. Relative to the primary indexed header, offsets 11 through 20 are zero, offset 21 is a marked reference to the owning scope, and offset 32 is the source-reference count. Each source-reference entry is `u8 1 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes`. The source-reference slots, scalar lane, frame length, and paired class are keyed by the primary and paired class tags:

| Primary / paired class | Frame length | Source count | Source-reference entry offsets | Scalar lane | `byte_offset` anchor |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- | --- | --- |
| `398 / 462` | 348 | 4 | `+36`, `+47`, `+58`, `+69` | `+80: u32 100`; `+84: finite f64`; `+92: u32 100` | Primary indexed header |
| `394 / 311` | 231 | 2 | `+36`, `+47` | `+58: u32 109`; `+62: finite f64`; `+70: u32 109` | Primary indexed header |
| `356 / 309` | 231 | 2 | `+36`, `+47` | `+58: u32 109`; `+62: finite f64`; `+70: u32 109` | Primary indexed header |

The paired indexed header begins at `byte_offset + frame_length` and is not part of the primary frame. Each source reference names an indexed source record with the fixed persistent identity frame: ten zero bytes, a local identity u64, asset and context LP-UTF16 GUIDs, and the optional tail slot. The source identities stay in reference order and may name different source-shape families.

A form-three body-recipe reference with Design reference `r` and recipe selector `s` selects persistent tags whose Design references contain `r` and whose native selector is `s`. Active-BREP face identities use either the history-source-qualified namespace or the source-less default active-BREP namespace. A form-33 operand's complete persistent identity is its stream, asset and context GUIDs, ordered `(Design reference, form)` clauses, recipe Design id, and recipe selector. Repeated occurrences of that identity select the same stable history body when every resolved occurrence agrees. A tool resolves in the target history when every clause selects the same input-state body. Otherwise, target-history candidates are discarded and every clause must select the same model body. Displayed bodies supersede cache bodies when at least one displayed candidate exists. A complete homogeneous tool list transfers as an ordered historical or model-body selection set only when every native member resolves to a distinct body. An unresolved direct-tool family with one body-recipe reference per member has one stream, asset GUID, context GUID, Design-reference form, and recipe Design id. Its selectors are the integers one through the family member count exactly once. Every nonempty archived member candidate set is identical, contains exactly the family member count of input-state bodies, contains every individually resolved family member, and excludes the Combine target. Such a family supplies that complete unordered body set. Multiple exact or family sets must be disjoint, and their union cardinality must equal the native tool count. When a Combine has exactly one circular body-pattern dependency, that pattern has one resolved historical body seed, and the number of distinct stable output-body identities plus the seed equals the circular instance count, the seed and outputs form the complete pattern body set. If the resolved Combine target is a member and removing it leaves exactly as many distinct bodies as native tools, the remainder is the historical unordered tool set. These collective proofs do not assign individual native tool members to bodies. No clause or member arbitrates when a form-33 identity has no unique body proof: zero or multiple body candidates leave the native selection unresolved, and a writer rejects the selection instead of choosing a body.

A `Draft` scope has a variable-length frame and six or more ordered references. The ordered reference table is in record-index order, so no role holds a fixed position in it: the two scalar lanes precede the operand groups in one document and follow them in another. Two references are fixed scalar records owned by the scope, at local ordinals zero and one, and those ordinals order them. The first scalar is a finite signed draft angle in radians, including zero; the second scalar is zero and reserves the opposite-side angle. One counted construction-operand group with role `0x0000001000000000` selects the drafted faces. A neutral-plane draft has one counted construction-operand group with role `0x0000002100000000`; its face-recipe members select the neutral plane. A WorkPlane-neutral form has one role-`0x0000002100000000` group with one entity-selection member whose primary identity `p` names the WorkPlane scope at record index `p + 1`; the WorkPlane matrix's third column is the pull direction, and the WorkPlane feature is the pull-plane dependency. A parting-line draft has exactly two role-`0x0000002100000000` groups. One group has exactly one entity-selection member naming the WorkPlane in the same way, and the other group has the face-recipe members that select the parting tool. Every group record and every group member is a reference of the owning scope. Face-recipe members resolve against the preceding history state. A bounded-face Draft member without `resolved_face_slots` is selected directly only when its structured counted program is complete, its historical-support and alternate-selector lanes are empty, and either its nonempty `candidate_faces` lane has no unreferenced candidate or its exact recipe-reference lanes have one unique largest face set after lanes with alternate selectors are excluded. If that direct admission does not resolve the member, a counted bounded-face member is resolved from the Draft transition. When alternate-selector candidates are present, exactly one such candidate must exist in both the preceding and result topologies and its actual surface geometry must change between them; surface-carrier record identity alone is not sufficient. When no alternate-selector candidate is present, the exact recipe-reference candidate lane must contain exactly one candidate and that face must exist in both topologies. Missing, ambiguous, unchanged, or unsupported surface geometry does not resolve the member. The group selection is the union of the selected lane. A member with `resolved_face_slots` uses those slots.

An entity-selection member in a neutral-plane group can carry a persistent topology identity instead of a WorkPlane reference. Its `historical_face_candidates` list gives the face incident to that identity in each named history state. When the group has one member and exactly one candidate face slot contains the scope's effective preceding history state, that slot is the neutral face selection. This form has no WorkPlane transform and no pull-plane dependency. No neutral face is selected when the state has no candidate or when candidates for that state name different face slots.

When that identity is a coedge, the neutral plane is resolved from its complete radial cycle. Every cycle member must name the same edge, belong to one loop, and map through one face relation to one incident face. The exact plane carriers among the incident faces must form exactly one coincident set. An open cycle, an ambiguous relation, a missing carrier, or non-coincident plane carriers leaves the neutral plane unresolved.

F3D stores no independent material-side bit for Draft. The pull direction is the signed-angle sign composed with the oriented neutral carrier: a face uses its surface normal adjusted by face sense, and a WorkPlane uses its transform's third column. The neutral `outward` value is true exactly when the signed angle is negative; a zero angle therefore has `outward=false`.

Draft scope frame layouts are keyed by the primary and paired class tags. Each `byte_offset` is the start of the primary 11-byte indexed header. The paired header starts at `byte_offset + frame_length` and is not part of the frame. A reference entry is `u8 1 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes`; the table below gives the entry start and the value offset. `kind_offset` names the first UTF-16LE code unit, so its u32 code-unit count is four bytes earlier. The current and preceding history values are u32 state identities; `0xffffffff` is null. The six ordered references in the four primary generation forms below include the two scalar owners, one drafted-face group, and the neutral-plane or parting-tool groups according to the Draft form.

| Primary / paired class | Frame length | Relative offset/type/discriminant table | Anchor and variant |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| `318 / 260` | 336 | `+171: u32 reference_count=6`; `+175 + 11i: reference entry`, `i=0..5`, value at `+176 + 11i`; `+241: u32 current_history_state`; `+245: u32 code_unit_count=5`; `+249: UTF-16LE Draft`; `+259: u32 feature_ordinal`; `+290: u32 previous_history_state`; `+336: paired header` | `byte_offset` anchors the compact form. There is no reserved u32 before the count. |
| `318 / 260` | 340 | `+171: u32 0`; `+175: u32 reference_count=6`; `+179 + 11i: reference entry`, `i=0..5`, value at `+180 + 11i`; `+245: u32 current_history_state`; `+249: u32 code_unit_count=5`; `+253: UTF-16LE Draft`; `+263: u32 feature_ordinal`; `+294: u32 previous_history_state`; `+340: paired header` | `byte_offset` anchors the shifted form. The reserved zero u32 shifts the reference table and every following field by four bytes. |
| `318 / 258` | 373 | `+171..+174: u32 0`; `+175: u32 reference_count=9`; `+179 + 11i: reference entry`, `i=0..8`, value at `+180 + 11i`; `+278: u32 current_history_state`; `+282: u32 code_unit_count=5`; `+286: UTF-16LE Draft`; `+296: u32 feature_ordinal`; `+327: u32 previous_history_state`; `+373: paired header` | The paired class and nine-reference count discriminate this legacy envelope from `318 / 260`. `byte_offset` remains the primary-header anchor. |
| `372 / 259` | 340 | `+171: u32 0`; `+175: u32 reference_count=6`; `+179 + 11i: reference entry`, `i=0..5`, value at `+180 + 11i`; `+245: u32 current_history_state`; `+249: u32 code_unit_count=5`; `+253: UTF-16LE Draft`; `+263: u32 feature_ordinal`; `+294: u32 previous_history_state`; `+340: paired header` | The paired class `259` discriminates this generation. `byte_offset` anchors the primary header, not the kind string. |
| `393 / 261` | 339 | `+171: u32 0`; `+175: u32 reference_count=6`; `+179 + 11i: reference entry`, `i=0..5`, value at `+180 + 11i`; `+245: u32 current_history_state`; `+249: u32 code_unit_count=5`; `+253: UTF-16LE Draft`; `+263: u32 feature_ordinal`; `+293: u32 previous_history_state`; `+339: paired header` | The 76-byte tail and paired class `261` discriminate this form. `byte_offset` anchors the primary header. |
| `448 / 263` | 340 | `+171: u32 0`; `+175: u32 reference_count=6`; `+179 + 11i: reference entry`, `i=0..5`, value at `+180 + 11i`; `+245: u32 current_history_state`; `+249: u32 code_unit_count=5`; `+253: UTF-16LE Draft`; `+263: u32 feature_ordinal`; `+294: u32 previous_history_state`; `+340: paired header` | The paired class `263` discriminates this generation. `byte_offset` anchors the primary header. |

The Draft scalar-owner generation mapping is one tuple. The owner column names the primary owner class and frame length; the parameter and companion columns name the primary parameter class and the companion indexed-header class. A row is selected by the scope primary/paired class pair and its frame length, not by a class tag in isolation. The owner tuple applies to the two scalar owners at local ordinals zero and one; other parameters in the same stream can use the separately specified 68- or 88-byte owner grammar.

| Scope primary / paired class | Draft scope frame lengths | Owner class / length | Parameter class | Companion class |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `318 / 260` | 336, 340 | `291 / 103` | `358` | `296` |
| `372 / 259` | 340 | `335 / 103` | `317` | `268` |
| `393 / 261` | 339 | `319 / 103` | `341` | `364` |
| `448 / 263` | 340 | `317 / 103` | `320` | `268` |
| `414 / 258` | 361, 383 | `441 / 103` | `260` | `315` |
| `467 / 259` | 361 | `406 / 103` | `328` | `306` |
| `318 / 258` | 373 | `284 / 103` | `379` | `393` |
| `290 / 262` | 336, 340, 373, 395 | `336 / 103` | `348` | `278` |
| `367 / 261` | 336, 340, 351, 358, 362 | `325 / 103` | `378` | `321` |
| `346 / 262` | 336, 340 | `297 / 103` | `281` | `300` |
| `381 / 262` | 340 | `313 / 103` | `353` | `317` |
| `438 / 266` | 340, 351 | `282 / 103` | `323` | `290` |

`Hole` scopes own one point-and-direction carrier and one direct support-face selection. The point carrier has type GUID `F2A7590D-6654-4674-B393-A2AEF4FEC48A`, with type version `1` or `4`; its dynamic class tag does not select the grammar. Relative to the point-data primary indexed header, the fixed prefix has an indexed header at offset `0`, eight zero bytes at offsets `11` through `18`, a leading-block presence byte at offset `19`, a property-block presence byte at offset `20`, and a bounding-box index at offset `21`. The position triple is at offset `25`, the model-space direction triple at offset `49`, the two construction parameters at offset `73`, and `refType` at offset `89`. Position and construction coordinates are centimetres; the direction is a finite unit vector and is not scaled. Version `1` stores the counted input-reference count at offset `93` and omits tangent-point data. Version `4` stores a tangent-data prefix byte at offset `93`, a tangent triple at offset `94`, and the counted input-reference count at offset `118`. The count is positive and each reference is non-null with a target entity index representable as `u32`. A version-4 frame ends immediately after its counted reference run. A version-1 frame permits its class-level tail after the counted reference run and ends at the next indexed header, which is its paired same-index header.

The direct support-face selection has type GUID `5A1BF548-241F-46FD-9FB5-E4B05126EB9D` and type version `1`. Its compact persistent-selection prefix is forty bytes through the asset UUID code-unit count: ten zero bytes at offsets `11` through `20`, `u8 1` at offset `21`, nested record index at offset `22`, six zero bytes at offsets `26` through `31`, `u32 1` at offset `32`, and the asset UUID code-unit count at offset `36`. The asset and context values are LP-UTF16 identifiers; the context is followed by `u32 2` and four zero bytes. The selection envelope is followed by paired and nested indexed records with indices `N`, `N+1`, `N+2`, and `N+3`; the `N+3` identity record stores ten zero bytes and the primary persistent identity at identity-relative offset `21`, and the next indexed record is `N+4`. That primary identity is the support-face key. The selection belongs directly to the Hole scope and is not a construction-operand group member. When its identity has exactly one history-qualified face candidate, the support-face selection uses the Hole feature input topology. An edge identity with no direct face candidate resolves from the exact preceding-to-current history transition only when the transition inserts one or more cylindrical face carriers whose axis line is coaxial with the point carrier's position and direction, and exactly one preceding face updated by the transition is a planar carrier that contains the point and whose oriented normal follows the direction. A non-coaxial cylinder, missing candidate, or multiple candidate faces leaves the support-face selection native.

`SpherePrimitive` and `TorusPrimitive` scopes store the result-operation u32 at primary-header offset 25 with the same `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, and `4 = new body` values, followed by byte `0x01` at offset 29. A sphere scope is 462 bytes to its paired same-index header. Marked references begin at offsets 30, 41, and 52 for the placement, diameter, and result records; its row-major rigid placement matrix begins at offset 64. A torus scope is 486 bytes. Marked references begin at offsets 30, 41, 52, and 63 for the major diameter, placement, minor diameter, and result records; its matrix begins at offset 75. Matrix column 2 is the primitive axis and column 3 is its centre in centimetres. Each diameter reference names a 104-byte same-index paired scalar frame whose positive finite f64 at primary-record offset 40 is a diameter in centimetres. Sphere radius is half its diameter. Torus major radius and tube radius are half the major and minor diameters respectively.

`BoxPrimitive` and the named `CylinderPrimitive` form use the named parameter-scope prologue. Bytes 11 through 19 are zero, the result-operation u32 is at primary-header offset 20 with values `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, and `4 = new body`, byte 24 is zero, and byte 25 is `0x01`. The ordered parameter owners are joined to the scope by native stream, scope record index, reference membership, and local ordinal. A box has five owners in ordinal order: `DistanceX`, `DistanceY`, `DistanceZ`, `OffsetDistanceX`, and `OffsetDistanceY`; the first three are positive lengths in centimetres and the last two are signed translations along the source x and y axes. A cylinder has two owners in ordinal order: `Height` and `Diameter`; both are positive centimetre lengths and the result is a full-turn cylinder. The box and named-cylinder result operation is the value at offset 20.

The shifted `CylinderPrimitive` form is keyed by its primary and paired class tags and its frame length:

| Primary class | Paired class | Frame length |
| ---: | ---: | ---: |
| `297` | `258` | `352` or `502` |
| `375` | `258` | `352` or `502` |
| `414` | `272` | `502` |

Its bytes 11 through 20 are zero, byte 21 is `0x01`, the result-operation u32 is at offset 22, and bytes 23 through 25 are zero. The first reference at offset 26 is `0x01`, `0x01`, a u32 target, and five zero bytes. Ordinary eleven-byte marked references begin at offsets 38, 49, and 60; these four targets are the last four ordered scope references in reverse order. The 352-byte form stores byte `0x01` at offset 71, u32 `1` at offset 72, one marked reference at offset 76, eight zero bytes at offsets 87 through 94, and a 36-code-unit LP-UTF16 GUID at offset 95. Its generic scope reference table begins at offset 174. The 502-byte form stores a row-major rigid 4x4 f64 frame at offset 72, eight zero bytes at offsets 200 through 207, a 15-byte construction-reference block at offset 208 (`0x01`, u32 `0x01000000`, the first ordered scope reference as u32, and six zero bytes), and a 36-code-unit LP-UTF16 GUID at offset 223; bytes 299 through 301 are zero and its generic scope reference table begins at offset 302. At offsets 174 and 302 respectively, the generic suffix stores the reference count, the ordered eleven-byte reference run, the current history state, the 17-code-unit `CylinderPrimitive` kind, the feature ordinal, and the previous history state at offsets 306 and 456 respectively. The shifted result operation uses the same four values as the named form. A cylinder frame represents a zero-origin cylinder along the positive source z-axis; its rigid frame preserves that geometry when translation is zero and its third column is the positive z-axis. The 502-byte frame is retained in the native primitive record.

A `Base Feature` scope records a result-body snapshot whose construction carries no parameter-history records; a closed direct-modeling session and an imported body both produce one. For `N > 0` result bodies, bytes 11 through 18 are zero, byte 19 is `0x01`, and offset 20 stores u32 `2N`. Two parallel runs start at offset 24: `N` marked u64 Design body entity suffixes followed by `N` marked u64 passive body-reference record indices; every value has a six-byte trailing field. The ordinary form then stores a marked null with a ten-byte zero tail, u32 `N`, and the passive references repeated as marked u32 values with six-byte trailing fields. One zero byte and one marked u64 shared metadata-record index with a six-byte trailing field follow, then u32 `N` and `N` marked u32 result-record indices with six-byte trailing fields. The class-384 form paired with class 264 instead stores `0x01`, six zero bytes, and u32 `N` in one eleven-byte field before the repeated passive-reference run; its shared metadata-record index has a two-byte trailing field. The class-420 form paired with class 258 stores `0x01`, five zero bytes, `0x01`, and u32 `N` in the eleven-byte count field. Its repeated u32 run contains the body entity suffixes instead of the passive references, and its shared metadata-record index has a two-byte trailing field. The class-452 form paired with class 266 uses the same compact count field, body-suffix repeat run, two-byte metadata field, and result-body ordering. The three body runs are parallel and preserve result-body order. The body entity suffixes join through the active Design BREP body map to solved bodies. The zero-body form omits the counted body runs and retains only its shared metadata reference. The ordinary `N > 0` frame length is `271 + 52N` bytes. The class-384 form paired with class 264, the class-420 form paired with class 258, and the class-452 form paired with class 266 have frame length `262 + 52N` bytes. The zero-body frame length is 267 bytes. The class-314 primary paired with class-259 stores a body-snapshot form. At offsets 11 through 18 it stores eight zero bytes, then `u8 1` at offset 19 and u32 `N` at offset 20. Each body entry at offset `24 + 15i` is `u8 1 + u64 body entity suffix + six-byte field`. The body run is followed by either the eight-byte sequence `01 00 00 00 01 00 00 00` or the nine-byte sequence `01 00 00 00 00 01 00 00 00`, then two consecutive LP-UTF16 GUIDs of 36 code units. Let `A = 184 + 15N`, the linkage-tail anchor. In the eight-byte form, the first GUID starts at `32 + 15N` and the second ends immediately before `A`; in the nine-byte form, the first GUID starts at `33 + 15N` and its second GUID ends at `A + 1`, with its final zero byte shared by the linkage tail at `A`. The linkage tail at `A` stores two zero bytes, `01 01 00 00 00`, a marked first-body suffix at offsets `A + 7` and `A + 8`, three zero bytes, a marked linkage record at `A + 19` and `A + 20`, six zero bytes, u32 `1` at `A + 34`, a marked auxiliary record at `A + 38` and `A + 39`, six zero bytes, and four zero bytes. A third 36-code-unit LP-UTF16 GUID starts at `A + 57`. Let `T = A + 133`. The generic scope suffix stores three zero bytes, the one-member reference table at `T + 3`, the current history-state identity at `T + 18`, and the LP-UTF16 scope kind at `T + 22`; the reference value is at `T + 8`, and the kind payload begins at `T + 26`. This form has no preceding history-state identity. If the kind has `L` UTF-16 code units, its frame length is `431 + 15N + 2L` bytes; the English `Base Feature` form has `L = 12` and therefore length `455 + 15N`. The three GUIDs and the linkage and auxiliary records are retained in the native construction record. A legacy result-body Base Feature uses current and previous ASM state identities to name the transition that installs its result bodies: the preceding identity is the state before the capture and the current identity is the state holding the captured bodies. A legacy Base Feature that installs at least one body carries two distinct identities, and the delta state named by the current identity holds the insert changes for the captured entities. The captured construction is not exposed as parametric steps, so no scope names a state between the two.

The class-290 primary paired with class-261 has a legacy compact result-body envelope. For `N > 0` it uses the count field `u8 1`, six zero bytes, `u32 N`, a passive-reference repeat run, and a two-byte shared-metadata field; its frame length is `261 + 52N`. The class-360 primary paired with class-258 has the expanded result-body envelope. For `N > 0` it uses the expanded count and passive-reference repeat form; its frame length is `262 + 52N`. The class-409 primary paired with class-262 has the same expanded result-body envelope. Its zero-body frame is 258 bytes. The class-444 primary paired with class-263 has a passive compact result-body envelope. For `N > 0` it uses a passive-reference repeat run and the count field `u8 1`, five zero bytes, `u8 0`, and u32 `N`; its frame length is `262 + 52N`.

The class-377 primary paired with class-259 uses a direct body-reference envelope. Its frame length is 363 bytes. Relative to the primary indexed header, bytes 11 through 18 are zero, byte 19 is `u8 1`, and offset 20 stores u32 `2`. Marked 15-byte elements at offsets 24 and 39 carry the PM body-reference record and the Design body entity suffix respectively; each element has a u32 value followed by ten zero bytes. The property block at offset 54 has one entry named `TagBodyBasedOnFaces` with type `IntrinsicMetaTypebool` and u16 value `1`. The parameter-reference group at offset 109 has count `1` and repeats the PM body-reference record at offset 115; seven bytes after that member are zero. The scope-reference member at offset 126 repeats the generic scope reference and has six zero trailing bytes. The auxiliary group starts at offset 137 with three zero bytes, then a marked auxiliary record at offset 141 and fourteen zero trailing bytes. A 36-code-unit LP-UTF16 envelope GUID starts at offset 160, followed by three zero bytes at offsets 236 through 238. The generic scope suffix starts with u32 `1` at offset 239, a marked copy of the scope reference at offset 243, six zero bytes, the current history-state identity at offset 254, the 12-code-unit `Base Feature` kind at offset 258, and the feature ordinal after its payload. The previous history-state identity follows the post-ordinal scope tail at offset 317. The body suffix at offset 40 joins through the active Design body map and is the body exposed by the neutral Base Feature definition. The PM record, auxiliary record, envelope GUID, and stored property are retained in the native construction record.

The class-keyed result-body envelopes are:

| Primary class | Paired class | Count field after the two 15-byte runs | Repeated values | Metadata field | Frame length |
| ---: | ---: | --- | --- | ---: | ---: |
| `290` | `261` | `u8 1`, six zero bytes, u32 `N` | passive-reference records | 2 bytes | `261 + 52N` |
| `360` | `258` | `u8 1`, six zero bytes, u32 `N` | passive-reference records | 2 bytes | `262 + 52N` |
| `384` | `264` | `u8 1`, six zero bytes, u32 `N` | passive-reference records | 2 bytes | `262 + 52N` |
| `409` | `262` | `u8 1`, six zero bytes, u32 `N` | passive-reference records | 2 bytes | `262 + 52N` |
| `444` | `263` | `u8 1`, five zero bytes, `u8 0`, u32 `N` | passive-reference records | 2 bytes | `262 + 52N` |
| `420` | `258` | `u8 1`, five zero bytes, `u8 1`, u32 `N` | body entity suffixes | 2 bytes | `262 + 52N` |
| `452` | `266` | `u8 1`, five zero bytes, `u8 1`, u32 `N` | body entity suffixes | 2 bytes | `262 + 52N` |
| other result-body forms | — | `u8 1`, ten zero bytes, u32 `N` | passive-reference records | 6 bytes | `271 + 52N` |

For the class-360/class-258, class-384/class-264, and class-409/class-262 rows, let `C = 24 + 30N`, the offset immediately after the two parallel runs. The discriminant layout is:

| Primary/paired class | Relative offset | Type | Value |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| `360`/`258`, `384`/`264`, `409`/`262` | `C` | u8 | `1` |
| `360`/`258`, `384`/`264`, `409`/`262` | `C + 1 .. C + 7` | bytes[6] | zero |
| `360`/`258`, `384`/`264`, `409`/`262` | `C + 7` | u32 | `N` |
| `360`/`258`, `384`/`264`, `409`/`262` | `C + 11 + 11i` | `u8 1 + u32 passive-reference record + bytes[6]` | `0 <= i < N` |

For the class-444/class-263 row, let `C = 24 + 30N`, the offset immediately after the two parallel runs. Its discriminant layout is:

| Primary/paired class | Relative offset | Type | Value |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| `444`/`263` | `C` | u8 | `1` |
| `444`/`263` | `C + 1 .. C + 6` | bytes[5] | zero |
| `444`/`263` | `C + 6` | u8 | `0` |
| `444`/`263` | `C + 7` | u32 | `N` |
| `444`/`263` | `C + 11 + 11i` | `u8 1 + u32 passive-reference record + bytes[6]` | `0 <= i < N` |

The class-409 zero-body form has, relative to its primary indexed header, offsets 11 through 19 zero, offset 20 `u8 1`, offsets 21 through 31 zero, offset 32 the shared-metadata marker, offset 33 its u64 record index, offset 41 the six-byte field, and offsets 47 through 54 zero before the first GUID. The metadata record is the sole retained reference in that form.

The class-444 primary paired with class-263 has a 258-byte zero-body frame. Relative to its primary indexed header, it stores nine zero bytes at offsets 11 through 19, `u8 1` at offset 20, eleven zero bytes at offsets 21 through 31, and `u8 1` at offset 32. Its shared metadata u64 is at offset 33; bytes 41 through 54 are zero. A u32 36 at offset 55 precedes the 36-code-unit GUID at offset 59; bytes 131 through 133 are zero. The generic scope prefix stores u32 1 at offset 134, `u8 1` at offset 138, the repeated metadata u32 at offset 139, six zero bytes at offsets 143 through 148, the current history-state u32 at offset 149, and u32 12 at offset 153. The `Base Feature` kind starts at offset 157. The primary `byte_offset` anchors the indexed header, and the paired class-263 header begins at `byte_offset + 258`.

After excluding the two serialized bytes per UTF-16 feature-family code unit, an `OffsetFaces` scope has a 264-byte four-reference form and a 253-byte three-reference form. In both forms its marked reference at primary-header offset 25 names the last ordered reference. That reference is a 104- or 105-byte same-index paired scalar frame whose finite f64 at primary-record offset 40 is the signed face-normal distance in centimetres. A `Shell` scope has three ordered references. One 268-byte payload form stores its outward Boolean at primary-header offset 25, zero at 26, the marked last reference at 27, u32 one at 51, and the marked first reference at 55. Its first reference is a role-`0x0000001000000000` construction group containing the faces removed to open the shell. A second 268-byte payload form stores nine zero bytes at offsets 11 through 19, one at 20, its outward Boolean at 21, three zero bytes, `01 00` at 25, the marked first reference at 27, nineteen zero bytes, u32 one at 51, and the marked second reference at 55. Its first reference is the wall-thickness scalar. Its second reference is a role-`0x0000001000000000` construction group containing the removed faces. The 258-byte compact payload form stores ten zero bytes after the indexed header, its outward Boolean at offset 21, the marked last reference at 22, fifteen zero bytes, u32 one at 42, and the marked first reference at 46. Its first reference is a role-`0x0000000400000000` construction group containing the target bodies; it removes no faces and therefore creates a closed shell. The thickness reference is a 103-, 104-, or 105-byte same-index paired scalar frame whose positive finite f64 at primary-record offset 40 is wall thickness in centimetres. The 103-byte scalar has class 354, stores marked references at offsets 24, 48, 67, 80, and 92, repeats its owning-scope reference in the first, third, and fifth slots, and uses two zero bytes before the fourth slot and seven before the fifth. A true outward Boolean grows the wall outside the original boundary; false grows it inside. One `Thicken` form with `N` selected-face members has `N + 2` ordered references and payload length `276 + 11N` for positive `N`. Its first reference is the signed-thickness scalar, its second reference is the construction group, and the remaining references are that group's ordered members. The primary frame stores the marked group reference at offset 34, a Boolean at 45, and the marked scalar reference at 47. Other `Thicken` scopes have a 281- or 287-byte token-independent frame. The 281-byte form stores a Boolean at offset 45 and its marked scalar reference at offset 46; the 287-byte form stores its marked scalar reference at offset 47. In those forms the marked reference names the last of three ordered references. Every form resolves through the same scalar frame; the nonzero scalar is signed thickness in centimetres. Its magnitude is the finished thickness, a positive value adds material along the selected-face normal, and a negative value adds material opposite that normal. Direct face operands use the face-recipe envelope and historical-face resolution rules below. OffsetFaces and Shell role-`0x0000001000000000` construction groups supply their complete selected face sets; when member recipes cannot be joined to solved historical faces, the exact group identity is the native face selection of the neutral operation. A Thicken bounded-face recipe can store the contiguous i32 recipe program without segmented node openers; its complete program and prefix references still identify the operand. A Thicken role-`0x0000000500000000` whole-body construction group or role-`0x0000001200000000` bounded-face construction group supplies the selected sheet faces; when its recipe cannot be joined to solved historical faces, the exact group identity is the native face selection of the neutral Thicken operation.

A class-347/258 Thicken scope has a 291-byte primary frame. Ten zero bytes occupy offsets 11 through 20, u32 `4` is at offset 21, and u32 `1` is at offset 25. A marked reference at offset 29 names the first ordered group. Bytes 40 and 41 are `01 01`; the marked reference at offset 42 names the last ordered scalar. Offset 53 stores u32 `1`, followed by a marked auxiliary reference at offset 57. Eight zero bytes at offsets 68 through 75 precede a 36-code-unit LP-UTF16 GUID at offset 80; its count is u32 `36` at offset 76. Three zero bytes at offsets 152 through 154 precede the three-entry ordered reference table at offset 155. The current history-state identity is at offset 192. The scope kind is u32 `7` at offset 196 followed by `Thicken` at offset 200; the feature ordinal follows at offset 214. The fixed 77-byte scope tail places the preceding history-state identity at offset 245, and the paired class-258 header begins at offset 291. This form orders the group, its bounded-face member, and the signed-thickness scalar as its three references; the scalar is complete only when the marked reference at offset 42 names that last member and its scalar frame is valid. The class pair and frame length are the admission discriminator; the dynamic class tag alone does not select this form.

A class-365 whole-body member starts after its indexed header with ten zero bytes, a nonnegative u32 reference count, and that many ordered `(u64 persistent Design reference, u32 form)` pairs. A legacy `Combine` tool form can store zero clauses; the marker then follows the count, and the empty identity supplies no persistent-face candidate or neutral body selection. A `0x01`-tagged u64 reference to indexed record `N+3`, two zero bytes, u32 one, an LP-UTF16 asset UUID, an LP-UTF16 context UUID, `u32 2`, and a four-byte selector-tail member follow the pair table. Class `365` permits any value in this four-byte member; class `367` stores `01 00 00 00`. A same-index paired header and nested indexed headers `N+1`, `N+2`, and `N+3` follow. Exactly one `body_recipe_data` record occurs after `N+3` and before the enclosing `N+4` header. This structural interval has no independent byte-length limit; the bounded Design `BulkStream` and the enclosing `N+4` header limit it. The recipe payload may contain its own indexed records; those records remain inside the member and do not replace the enclosing `N+4` boundary. Each persistent reference joins to persistent face tags independently. A Thicken whole-body member has one form-three reference. Candidates outside the history BREP namespace are excluded; inserted result faces are excluded by intersecting the candidates with the exact feature-input topology. A single remaining face resolves as the historical sheet-face selection. Missing state links or multiple input candidates retain the native group selection. A complete counted Shell bounded-face recipe can select a face set. The set resolves when every candidate in the preceding topology has exactly one historical support context, every context maps its active face slot to the same single preceding slot, no context reports a changed predecessor, and the contexts cover the candidates exactly.
A class-367 whole-body member uses the same reference, UUID, nested-record, and recipe envelope. Its four bytes after `u32 2` are `01 00 00 00`; this form supplies whole-body selections for Scale and shape-target Extrude.

A modern uniform `Scale` or `Maßstab` scope has a 303-byte token-independent frame and five ordered references. Primary-header offset 20 stores u32 factor kind `1`, byte 24 is zero, and the positive finite f64 at offset 25 is the uniform scale factor. The marked references at offsets 33 and 44 name the fifth ordered reference and the first ordered reference. Offset 55 stores u32 `1`, byte 59 is zero, offsets 60 and 64 each store u32 `1`, and the marked reference at offset 68 names the second ordered reference. That second reference is a role-`0x0000000400000000` counted construction group selecting the transformed bodies. The first reference is the class-292 `ScaleFactor` owner, and the fifth reference is a point-data construction record whose finite `point3d` value is the scale center in centimetres; each coordinate is multiplied by ten for neutral millimetres. The body group first resolves against the scope's declared preceding history state when every member supplies one distinct input-body slot. If that historical proof does not resolve, each member must map through its complete body recipe to one current model body; displayed bodies take precedence over cache bodies, and duplicate member bodies leave the group unresolved. An unresolved group remains the exact native body selection.

A legacy English `Scale` scope has a 307-byte frame with five ordered references or a 318-byte frame with six ordered references; each additional ordered reference adds 11 bytes. Its fixed operation prefix has five zero bytes at primary-header offsets 16 through 20, the positive finite f64 uniform factor at offset 21, and marked references at offsets 29, 40, and 64 naming the final, first, and second ordered references. Offset 51 stores u32 `1`, byte 55 is zero, and offsets 56 and 60 each store u32 `1`. The second ordered reference is the role-`0x0000000400000000` counted construction group selecting the transformed bodies. The final ordered reference is a point-data construction record; its `point3d` value is in centimetres and is the scale center, so each coordinate is multiplied by ten for neutral millimetres. The first ordered reference is the `ScaleFactor` owner. The group-selection and native fallback rules are the same as the modern form.

A `Move` scope references one rigid-transform record and one role-`0x0000000400000000` whole-body construction group. Class `296`, `362`, `433`, `447`, `293`, `393`, `442`, `451`, and `456` transform records are 253 bytes to their same-index paired headers; the class-447 form uses paired class `263`, and the class-456 form uses paired class `258`. Class `349` transform records are 254 or 274 bytes, and class `368` transform records are 254 bytes. In all forms, offsets 11 through 42 are zero, offset 43 stores u32 form `1` or `5`, offset 47 is zero, and sixteen row-major f64 values begin at offset 48. The matrix has an orthonormal three-column basis and homogeneous final row `(0, 0, 0, 1)`. Translation components are centimetres. The matrix projects as a model-space rotation about the origin followed by its translation; an identity rotational basis has no axis-angle component.

A `RemoveBody` scope references exactly one nonempty construction-operand group with role `0x0000000400000000`. The group members are the whole bodies deleted by the operation. Both features use the legacy body-group grammar below.

The legacy body-group frame is keyed by its primary and paired class tags. `byte_offset` anchors the primary indexed header, and the paired header is outside the frame. The common prefix after the payload prologue is `u32 member_count`, the member reference run, two nullable references, `u32 trailing_count`, the trailing reference run, a u64 role with low word zero, ten zero bytes, `u32 opaque_ordinal`, and one finite f64 opaque scalar. The body-group tail then stores a second copy of `opaque_ordinal`, a reference to `N+2`, a two-byte discriminant, a reference to `N+1`, byte `0`, a reference to the owning scope, and the same-index paired header. A same-segment reference is `u8 1`, a u64 target whose high word is zero, and two zero bytes; a null optional reference is one zero byte. The ordinary one-member, two-null-auxiliary, one-trailing-reference envelope is 123 bytes. Its primary/paired class pairs are 257/262, 323/262, 328/263, 338/261, 282/262, and 302/258; the first four use discriminant `01 01`, and the last two use `00 01`. The class-328 `Move` form is a separate 123-byte variant:

| Primary/paired class and scope | Relative offset | Type | Value |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| `328`/`263`, `Move` | `+36` | u8 | null auxiliary reference |
| `328`/`263`, `Move` | `+37` | `u8 1 + u64 + bytes[2]` | present auxiliary reference to `N+13` |
| `328`/`263`, `Move` | `+48` | u32 | trailing count `0` |
| `328`/`263`, `Move` | `+52` | u8 | retained null trailing-slot byte `0` |
| `328`/`263`, `Move` | `+53` | u64 | role; low word zero |
| `328`/`263`, `Move` | `+98` | u8 | leading tail byte `0` |
| `328`/`263`, `Move` | `+99 .. +100` | bytes[2] | discriminant `01 01` |
| `328`/`263`, `Move` | `+101` | `u64 + bytes[3]` | unmarked reference to `N+1` |
| `328`/`263`, `Move` | `+112` | `u8 1 + u64 + bytes[2]` | owning-scope reference |

The class-328 `Move` form has no counted trailing-reference run; its paired class is 263.

| Primary class | Paired class | Frame length | Tail discriminant | `variant` |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | --- |
| `257` | `262` | 123 | `01 01` | `true` |
| `323` | `262` | 123 | `01 01` | `true` |
| `328` (`Move`) | `263` | 123 | `01 01` | `true` |
| `338` | `261` | 123 | `01 01` | `true` |
| `282` | `262` | 123 | `00 01` | `false` |
| `302` | `258` | 123 | `00 01` | `false` |

In the one-member envelope, the primary-header-relative fixed fields are `+11..+20: zero`, `+21: u32 member_count`, `+25: member reference marker`, `+26: member reference value`, `+53: u64 role`, `+71: u32 opaque_ordinal`, `+75: f64 opaque scalar`, `+83: repeated ordinal`, `+87: reference to N+2`, `+98: two-byte tail discriminant`, `+100: reference to N+1`, `+111: zero`, `+112: owning-scope reference`, and `+123: paired header`. Member, auxiliary, and trailing reference counts extend the variable runs; the paired-header index and the class-keyed discriminant close the frame.

The ordered persistent-face clauses of a body-recipe member identify its body through complete body-region-shell-face incidence. ASM state identifiers are scoped to their owning history; equal numeric identifiers in another history are unrelated. Resolve the unique history containing both the current and preceding states before walking the state chain. Prefer a unique preceding-history body slot. When that history state is not represented, a unique current model body is valid. Ambiguous or incomplete joins retain the exact construction-group identity as the native body selection.

A one-sided fixed-angle `Revolve` scope has a 372-byte token-independent frame with seven ordered references. Primary-header offset 25 stores the result operation with values `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, and `4 = new body`; offset 29 stores extent kind `2`, and offset 33 stores direction kind `0`. References zero and two are counted construction groups. Role `0x0000004100000000` supplies the profile and role `0x0000002100000000` supplies the axis selection; each group has its following reference as sole member. A role `0x0000000400000000` or role `0x0000000800000000` group supplies existing-body operands for a Boolean operation. References four and five are 105-byte owned scalar frames with lane ordinals zero and one. Lane zero is the positive angular travel in radians and lane one is zero. The class-407 form paired with class 258 has a 363-byte token-independent frame and eight ordered references. Offset 21 stores the result operation, offset 25 stores extent kind `2`, and offset 29 stores direction kind `0`. Offset 30 stores u32 one. The marked u64 reference at offset 34 has a two-byte trailing field and names reference six, which is the sole indexed angle owner at local ordinal zero. This form omits the zero-valued opposite-side scalar. The marker-one form uses class 409 paired with class 257 and a 345-byte token-independent frame, class 385 paired with class 262 and a 355-byte token-independent frame, or class 323 paired with class 260 and a 345- or 367-byte token-independent frame. Offset 20 is marker `1`, offset 21 is u32 zero, offset 25 stores the result operation, offset 29 stores extent kind `2`, offset 33 stores direction kind `0`, and offset 34 stores u32 one. It has six or eight ordered references and exactly one referenced indexed angle owner at local ordinal zero. This form also omits the zero-valued opposite-side scalar. All forms use the same profile, axis, and existing-body roles. New-body operations have only the profile and axis groups; Boolean operations have exactly one additional existing-body group. The neutral revolution retains the exact profile group when its profile geometry is unresolved. An axis edge uses the edge-recipe envelope and historical-topology resolution rules below. An entity-selection axis stores either the owning Sketch entity suffix and selected curve identity or a persistent face identity. The unique matching line is transformed by that Sketch's placement and supplies the model-space axis. A straight carrier contributes its origin and direction; a circular or elliptical carrier contributes its center and normal. A selected planar face contributes its carrier origin and normal. A selected cylindrical, conical, or toroidal face contributes its carrier axis. An axis group can instead contain one face-recipe operand. Its exact selector-reference candidate lane supplies the selected face carriers. Every candidate must have an analytic carrier, and all carrier axes must define the same model-space axis line.

The class-403/258 one-sided fixed-angle `Revolve` form has a 387-byte frame and eight ordered references. Offset 21 stores the result operation, offset 25 stores extent kind `2`, offset 29 stores direction kind `0`, and offset 30 stores marker `1`. A marked angle-owner reference starts at offset 34 and names the sole positive angular owner at local ordinal zero. Four auxiliary marked references start at offsets 45, 57, 73, and 96; every inline target occurs in the ordered reference table. A 36-code-unit null GUID starts at offset 107, the ordered-reference count is `8` at offset 186, the history-state field is at offset 278, the LP-UTF16 `Revolve` kind has seven code units at offset 282, and the feature ordinal is at offset 300. The previous history-state field is at offset 341. This form omits the zero-valued opposite-side scalar.

The reference-aware `Extrude` or `Extrusion` prologue stores its result-operation u32 at primary-header offset 28. An indexed-reference prefix instead stores byte `01` at offset 25, the referenced record index at offset 26, and seven or eight zero bytes beginning at offset 30; the operation follows at offset 37 or 38 respectively. The offset-38 form stores either eight zero bytes or seven zero bytes followed by marker byte `01` at offset 37. The referenced record also occurs in the scope reference table. Operation values are `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, and `4 = new body`. The two immediately following u32 values are the travel direction and the face-extend option; they are not an extent pair. Direction values are `1 = one side`, `2 = two sides`, and `3 = symmetric`. Offset `operation + 12` is the direction-reversal Boolean, offset `operation + 13` is the geometry-kind Boolean (`0 = sheet`, `1 = solid`), and offset `operation + 14` is the start-support byte: `0 = profile plane`, `1 = offset profile plane`, and `2 = selected face`. The current reference-aware layout stores three zero bytes at `operation + 15` through `operation + 17`. Its profile normal is three contiguous unit-length f64 values at `operation + 18`. Seven nullable reference slots immediately follow the profile normal. An absent slot is one zero byte. A present slot is `01 + u32 record_index + six zero bytes`, and its record index occurs in the scope reference table. The start support determines the profile reference set exactly: a profile-plane start stores neither a `ProfileOffset` nor a profile-from-entity reference, an offset-profile-plane start stores only the `ProfileOffset`, and a selected-face start stores both.

The extent form is carried by two further u32 values, one per side, after the profile normal and seven nullable reference slots. Except in the shape-target form, the first-side value immediately follows the seventh slot. A first-side value other than `2` is followed by nine zero bytes and the second-side value at first-side offset `+13`. A first-side value of `2` stores its target payload after the first-side value and stores the second-side value four bytes before the scope reference-count field. In the shape-target form, the seventh nullable slot names the target group. A u32 zero-based scope-reference ordinal immediately after the seventh slot names the same target-group record, one zero byte follows the ordinal, and the first-side value `2` follows that byte. Extent values are `0 = absent`, `1 = distance`, `2 = to entity with an offset`, `3 = to next`, and `4 = through all`. The valid `(direction, first side, second side)` tuples are `(1, 1, 0)` for one-sided distance, `(1, 2, 0)` for one-sided to entity, `(1, 3, 0)` for one-sided to next, `(1, 4, 0)` for one-sided through all, `(2, 1, 1)` for two-sided distance, `(2, 2, 0)` for two-sided face targets, `(3, 1, 0)` for symmetric distance, and `(3, 4, 4)` for symmetric through all. The direction and side values are one redundant extent key. A reader admits only the listed tuple and rejects a frame whose values disagree; no member arbitrates between them. A writer emits the matching tuple for the selected extent. The face-extend option does not select the extent tuple. The first side is never absent, and the second side is absent when the direction is one-sided. A first side of `1` stores the `AlongDistance` input and no termination reference. It can also store a zero `Side1Offset` default, which has no geometric effect. A first side of `2` stores one target form and no `AlongDistance`; and each implication holds in both directions. A one-sided face target stores one role-`0x0000001100000000` termination group and one `Side1Offset` owner. The class-`330`/`258` 476-byte reference-aware form omits that owner and its parameter when the offset is zero; the absent owner is the zero offset, and this omission cannot encode a nonzero offset. A two-sided face-target form stores two role-`0x0000001100000000` termination groups in scope-reference order, the `Side1Offset`, `TaperAngle`, `Side2Offset`, and `Side2TaperAngle` owners, and no distance owners. Its second-side extent value is `0`; the two target faces and side-local offsets carry the two independent termination laws. A shape target stores one role-`0x0000000500000000` target group whose members are whole-body recipe operands, no termination face group, and no nonzero `Side1Offset`. Each member's persistent-reference candidate faces constrain the identity of one body in the effective preceding history state; they do not form the target boundary. The effective predecessor is the scope predecessor when present and otherwise the current ASM state's unique direct transition predecessor. The target face set is the union of every face in the complete body-region-shell-face closure of every member body. Within each closure, the body, regions, shells, and faces occur exactly once, each relation owner and member is unique, and every body, region, and shell has a nonempty child run. Every member uses the same effective preceding state. A through-all cut can retain one role-`0x0000000500000000` whole-body group for each active extent side. These groups repeat the affected-body selection and do not replace the through-all termination. A second side of `1` stores the `AgainstDistance` parameter and the second taper angle and no nonzero second offset. A symmetric direction points the `AgainstDistance` and `AlongDistance` references at one parameter record rather than at two records of equal value, does the same for the two taper angles, and leaves the second offset null. Two through-all values with a symmetric direction select through-all travel on both sides and store no distance parameter. A two-sided direction gives the second side its own `AgainstDistance` and taper angle. A selected-face start adds one role-`0x0000001100000000` start group; a role-`0x0000000500000000` group is a start-face group only when the start support is selected face. Scope-reference order and the extent form distinguish the start group from the termination group when both use role `0x0000001100000000`. A complete counted bounded-face termination recipe can select a stable face set. It resolves under the same exact predecessor-coverage invariant as a Shell face set. Join, cut, and intersect require an existing-body operand group; new body has no existing-body operand group.

The class-keyed `415 / 265` reference-aware form is a symmetric-distance Extrude variant. Its primary header anchors the frame, and the paired header begins at the class-keyed frame length:

| Scope primary / paired class | Frame length | Reference-count offset | Ordered reference count |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| `415 / 265` | 447 | `+288` | 5 |
| `415 / 265` | 469 | `+288` | 7 |

Its fixed frame is:

| Relative offset | Type | Discriminant or invariant |
| ---: | --- | --- |
| `+20` | u32 | `1` |
| `+24..+26` | bytes[3] | zero |
| `+27` | u8 | operation prefix marker `1` |
| `+28` | u32 | result operation: `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, `4 = new body` |
| `+32` | u32 | travel direction `3` (symmetric) |
| `+36` | u32 | face-extend value `2` |
| `+40` | u8 | direction reversal: `0` or `1` |
| `+41` | u8 | geometry kind: `0` or `1` |
| `+42` | u8 | start support: `0 = profile plane`, `1 = offset profile plane`, `2 = selected face` |
| `+43..+45` | bytes[3] | zero |
| `+46..+69` | f64[3] | finite unit profile normal |
| `+70..+116` | bytes[47] | seven nullable slots in order: absent, present, present, present, absent, present, absent; an absent slot is `0`, and a present slot is `01 + u32 record index + six zero bytes` |
| `+117` | u32 | first-side extent `1` (distance) |
| `+121..+129` | bytes[9] | zero |
| `+130` | u32 | second-side extent `1` (distance) |
| `+134..+287` | bytes[154] | intervening scope envelope; no extent discriminator |
| `+288` | u32 | ordered reference count: `5` or `7` according to frame length |
| `+292 + 11i` | bytes[11] | ordered reference entry; `i = 0..4` or `i = 0..6` |

The class-keyed `415 / 265` one-sided forms use a shorter extent lane and their own frame anchors:

| Scope primary / paired class | Form | Frame length | Reference-count offset | Ordered reference count |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| `415 / 265` | one-sided to face | 481 | `+278` | 9 |
| `415 / 265` | one-sided distance | 449 | `+268` | 7 |

Both forms anchor the primary header at `byte_offset`, use the operation prefix marker `1`, and store the common operation, direction-reversal, geometry-kind, start-support, and finite unit profile-normal fields at the offsets above. The one-sided forms store one-sided travel direction `1` at offset `+32`. The one-sided to-face form stores face-extend option `1` at offset `+36`, first-side extent `2` (to face) at `+107`, and a marked `Side1Offset` owner reference at `+111`. Its second-side extent `0` four bytes before the reference count is at `+274`. The distance form stores face-extend option `2` at offset `+36` and first-side extent `1` (distance) at `+107`; the distance form has no first-side owner lane. Its second-side extent `0` four bytes before the reference count is at `+264`. In both forms, scope-local reference lanes precede the first-side extent, the remaining scope envelope precedes the ordered reference-count field, and each ordered reference entry is `01 + u32 record index + six zero bytes`. The paired header begins at the frame length shown in the table. These frame lengths, reference counts, and the `415 / 265` class pair are admission keys; the side pairs `[2, 0]` and `[1, 0]` select one-sided to-face and one-sided distance respectively.

The ordered reference table is followed by the common scope tail: current history state, the LP-UTF16 feature kind `Extrude`, a nonzero feature ordinal, the preceding history state when present, and the paired indexed header. The class pair and frame length are admission keys. The direction/face-extend pair `[3, 2]` is not an extent tuple in this form; the side pair `[1, 1]` selects `SymmetricDistance`.

The shifted Extrude prologue stores u32 `1` at primary-header offset 20 and three zero bytes at offsets 24 through 26. It stores the result-operation u32 at offset 27, the travel direction at offset 31, the face-extend option at offset 35, the direction-reversal Boolean at offset 39, the geometry-kind Boolean at offset 40, and the start-support byte at offset 41. A one-sided or symmetric form uses one of three extent lanes. A scope reference-count field at primary-header offset 252, 262, or 263 selects the compact lane, whose first-side extent is at offset 106 and second-side extent is at offset 110. An offset-profile form with its reference-count field at offset 262 instead uses the widened extent lane at offsets 116 and 130. A reference-count field at offset 272 selects the same widened lane. A reference-count field at offset 283 uses the same widened extent lane for one-sided and symmetric forms. A long-reference form with its reference-count field at offset 692 uses the compact extent lane at offsets 106 and 110. A symmetric through-all form with its reference-count field at offset 294 stores the first-side extent at offset 116 and the second-side extent at offset 129. Values in the unselected lanes have no extent semantics. A to-entity first side stores its selected-entity payload after the first-side value and stores the second-side extent four bytes before the scope reference-count field in either lane. The ordinary two-sided distance form stores its first-side and second-side extent values at offsets 155 and 178. Its marked parameter references begin at offsets 139, 159, and 182; five zero bytes separate the first reference from the first-side extent, and eight zero bytes separate the second reference from the second-side extent. The offset-283 two-sided form stores the side extents at offsets 166 and 181, with marked parameter references at offsets 139 and 170, a trailing marked entity reference at offset 185, and eight zero bytes at offsets 196 through 203. Every field uses the same enum as the current prologue. Because the two u32 after the operation are the direction and the face-extend option, neither of them selects an extent form; the later per-side values select it, and the stored parameter set follows those.

The class-keyed `397 / 262` shifted form is a symmetric-distance variant with a 473-byte primary frame. Its extent pair is `[1, 1]`; this pair is admitted only for this class and frame because the common symmetric tuple is `[1, 0]`. The primary frame is:

| Relative offset | Type | Discriminant or invariant |
| ---: | --- | --- |
| `+20` | u32 | `1` |
| `+24..+26` | bytes[3] | zero |
| `+27` | u32 | result operation: `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, `4 = new body` |
| `+31` | u32 | travel direction `3` (symmetric) |
| `+35` | u32 | face-extend value `2` |
| `+39` | u8 | direction reversal: `0` or `1` |
| `+40` | u8 | geometry kind: `0` or `1` |
| `+41` | u8 | start support: `0 = profile plane`, `1 = offset profile plane`, `2 = selected face` |
| `+42..+44` | bytes[3] | zero |
| `+45..+68` | f64[3] | finite unit profile normal |
| `+69..+125` | bytes[57] | seven nullable slots: present, present, present, present, absent, present, absent; a present slot is `01 + u32 record index + six zero bytes` |
| `+126` | u32 | first-side extent `1` (distance) |
| `+130..+138` | bytes[9] | zero |
| `+139` | u32 | second-side extent `1` (distance) |
| `+203..+278` | bytes[76] | LP-UTF16 GUID: 36 code units |
| `+279..+281` | bytes[3] | zero |
| `+282` | u32 | ordered reference count `8` |

The paired `262` header begins at `+473`. The bytes between the extent pair and the GUID are the class-local parameter/reference envelope; the ordered reference table follows the count. The `397 / 262` frame length, reference-count offset, ordered count, class pair, and extent-lane offsets are admission keys.

The shifted reference-aware two-sided face-target `Extrude` form uses class-keyed frame lengths. Its primary header anchors the frame, and the paired header begins at the class-specific frame offset. The form has six scope-class generations:

| Scope primary / paired class | Frame length | Reference-count offset | Ordered reference count |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| `357 / 258` | 538 | `+292` | 13 |
| `275 / 262` | 538 | `+292` | 13 |
| `361 / 262` | 538 | `+292` | 13 |
| `349 / 266` | 538 | `+292` | 13 |
| `397 / 262` | 538 | `+292` | 13 |
| `323 / 263` | 516 | `+292` | 11 |

The class-397 row uses this 538-byte grammar only when its first-side parameter discriminator at `+135` is `1`; the class-local `+135 = 2` envelope is not this face-target form. The 538-byte class pairs use these owner and parameter classes:

| Scope primary / paired class | Owner class / length | Parameter class |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `357 / 258` | `260 / 103` | `305` |
| `275 / 262` | `312 / 103` | `342` |
| `361 / 262` | `279 / 103` | `283` |
| `349 / 266` | `282 / 103` | `323` |
| `397 / 262` | `297 / 103` | `281` |

The fixed frame is:

| Relative offset | Type | Discriminant or invariant |
| ---: | --- | --- |
| `+20` | u32 | `1` |
| `+24..+26` | bytes[3] | zero |
| `+27` | u32 | result operation: `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, `4 = new body` |
| `+31` | u32 | travel direction `2` |
| `+35` | u32 | face-extend option `1` |
| `+39` | u8 | direction reversal: `0` or `1` |
| `+40` | u8 | geometry kind: `0` or `1` |
| `+41` | u8 | start support: `0 = profile plane`, `1 = offset profile plane`, `2 = selected face` |
| `+42..+44` | bytes[3] | zero |
| `+45..+68` | f64[3] | finite unit profile normal |
| `+69..+71` | u8[3] | three absent nullable slots, each `0` |
| `+72`, `+83`, `+94`, `+105` | bytes[11] | present nullable slots: `u8 1 + u32 record index + six zero bytes`; each index is in the scope reference table |
| `+116` | u32 | first-side extent `2` |
| `+120` | bytes[11] | `Side1Offset` owner reference |
| `+131..+134` | bytes[4] | zero |
| `+135` | u32 | `1` |
| `+139` | u32 | `2` |
| `+143` | u8 | `0` |
| `+144` | bytes[11] | `Side2Offset` owner reference |
| `+155..+158` | bytes[4] | zero |
| `+159` | bytes[11] | `Side2TaperAngle` owner reference |
| `+170..+174` | bytes[5] | zero |
| `+175` | u32 | `1` |
| `+179` | bytes[11] | profile construction-group reference |
| `+190..+197` | bytes[8] | zero for the 538-byte class pairs |
| `+198` | u32 | body construction-group count `1` for the 538-byte class pairs |
| `+202` | bytes[11] | body construction-group reference for the 538-byte class pairs |
| `+213` | u32 | UTF-16 code-unit count `36` |
| `+217..+287` | UTF-16LE bytes | first 35 code units of the 36-code-unit GUID; the final zero high byte is shared with the second-side field |
| `+288` | u32 | second-side extent `0`; this is `reference_count - 4` |
| `+292` | u32 | reference count: `13` for the 538-byte class pairs; `11` for `323 / 263` |
| `+296 + 11i` | bytes[11] | ordered reference entry; `i = 0..12` for the 538-byte class pairs and `i = 0..10` for `323 / 263` |
| `+439` | u32 | current history state |
| `+443` | u32 | UTF-16 code-unit count `7` |
| `+447` | UTF-16LE | `Extrude` |
| `+461` | u32 | nonzero feature ordinal |
| `+492` | u32 | previous history state |

The `323 / 263` class pair uses a shifted tail: trailing-reference count `+190`, unlisted trailing reference `+194`, zero padding `+205..+212`, and the GUID prefix remains at `+213`.

| Relative offset | Type | Discriminant or invariant |
| ---: | --- | --- |
| `+190` | u32 | trailing-reference count `1` |
| `+194` | bytes[11] | marked trailing reference; its record index is not in the ordered scope-reference table |
| `+205..+212` | bytes[8] | zero |

The 36-code-unit GUID payload ends at offset `+289`; its final high byte is the first byte of the zero second-side extent lane, and bytes `+289..+291` are zero. The three absent and four present nullable slots, the fixed tail references, the count, and the class-keyed paired-header distance jointly admit this grammar. The `+296 + 11i` reference run has `i = 0..12` for the 538-byte class pairs and `i = 0..10` for `323 / 263`. The four owned parameters are `Side1Offset`, `TaperAngle`, `Side2Offset`, and `Side2TaperAngle` in the 538-byte class pairs; the extent is `TwoSidedToFaces`, and the two termination groups remain in the scope's ordered reference table. This form is distinct from both the current reference-aware prologue and the shifted prologue without reference-aware fields.

For this form, `direction_reversed = 0` selects `ProfileNormal` and `direction_reversed = 1` selects `ReversedProfileNormal`. The value does not change the two face-termination groups or their side order.

The `323 / 263` class pair uses the 485-byte symmetric-through-all form. Its reference-count field is at `+272`, its ordered reference table has ten entries, and its paired header starts at `+485`. The shared shifted reference-aware prologue through the seven nullable slots is unchanged. The class-specific prefix is:

| Relative offset | Type | Discriminant or invariant |
| ---: | --- | --- |
| `+116` | u32 | symmetric-through-all first-side extent `4` |
| `+120..+128` | bytes[9] | zero bytes at `+120..+128` |
| `+129` | u32 | symmetric-through-all second-side extent `4` |
| `+133..+138` | bytes[6] | zero bytes at `+133..+138` |
| `+139` | bytes[11] | marked symmetric-extent reference |
| `+150..+154` | bytes[5] | zero bytes at `+150..+154` |
| `+155` | u32 | profile construction-group count `1` |
| `+159` | bytes[11] | profile construction-group reference |
| `+170..+177` | bytes[8] | zero bytes at `+170..+177` |
| `+178` | u32 | trailing-reference count `1` |
| `+182` | bytes[11] | marked trailing reference |
| `+193` | bytes[76] | UTF-16 code-unit count `36` |
| `+269..+271` | bytes[3] | zero bytes at `+269..+271` |
| `+272` | u32 | ordered reference count `10` |

The symmetric-extent reference, profile construction-group reference, trailing reference, and four present nullable slots resolve to members of the ten-entry ordered reference table. The symmetric extent is `SymmetricThroughAll`; it has no distance owners or termination face groups. The profile construction group supplies the profile operand, and the trailing group supplies the existing-body operand when the operation is Boolean. This grammar is distinct from the 516-byte class-`323` face-target form and from the shifted prologue without reference-aware fields.

The compact legacy Extrude prologue stores u32 `1` at primary-header offset 20 and zero bytes at offsets 24 through 25.
