It stores the result-operation u32 at offset 26, the travel direction at offset 30, the face-extend option at offset 34, the direction-reversal Boolean at offset 38, the geometry-kind Boolean at offset 39, and the start-support byte at offset 40. For the one-sided distance and symmetric-distance forms, the scope reference-count field is at offset 251, the first-side extent is at offset 105, and the second-side extent is at offset 109. The valid tuples are `(1, 1, 0)` for one-sided distance and `(3, 1, 0)` for symmetric distance. These fields use the same operation, direction, geometry-kind, start-support, and extent enums as the shifted prologue.

The compact legacy mixed two-sided Extrude form stores direction `2`, face-extend option `0`, first-side extent discriminator `1`, and second-side extent discriminator `2`. Its scope reference-count field is at offset 281, its first-side extent is at offset 124, and its second-side extent is at offset 128. Its ordered reference table has eleven members; its side-specific parameter owners are `Side2Offset` and `Side2TaperAngle`, and its face-operand group has the termination role. The neutral extent is two-sided with a blind first side from `AlongDistance` and a face-terminating second side; `Side2Offset` is the second-side termination offset and `Side2TaperAngle` is its draft.

A class-`296` primary Extrude scope paired with class `261` uses a one-sided-to-face envelope distinct from the compact distance and mixed forms. Its primary indexed header is the byte-offset anchor. It stores the common operation fields at offsets `+20`, `+24..+25`, `+26`, `+30`, `+34`, `+38`, `+39`, and `+40`; the first-side extent discriminator is `u32 2` at `+105`, the second-side discriminator is `u32 0` at `+257 = reference_count - 4`, and the ordered reference count is at `+261`. The travel direction is `1`; the face-extend option is `1` or `2`; the direction-reversal, geometry-kind, and start-support bytes use the common Extrude Boolean and start-support enums. The paired header distance and ordered reference count are fixed as `(440, 7)`, `(462, 9)`, or `(473, 10)`. These exact frame shapes select `OneSidedToFace` and do not widen the compact distance grammar.

A class-`296` primary Extrude scope paired with class `261` uses a 450-byte symmetric-distance envelope. Its primary indexed header is the byte-offset anchor. It stores `u32 1` at `+20`, zero bytes at `+24..+25`, the result operation at `+26`, direction `3` at `+30`, face-extend option `2` at `+34`, direction reversal at `+38`, geometry kind at `+39`, and start support at `+40`. The first-side extent discriminator is `u32 1` at `+128`, the second-side discriminator is `u32 0` at `+267 = reference_count - 4`, and the ordered reference count is `7` at `+271`. The paired header begins at `+450`. This exact frame selects `SymmetricDistance` and remains separate from both the compact distance grammar and the class-296 one-sided-to-face grammar.

A class-`296` primary Extrude scope paired with class `261` uses a 536-byte two-sided-to-faces envelope. Its primary indexed header is the byte-offset anchor. It stores `u32 1` at `+20`, zero bytes at `+24..+25`, the result operation at `+26`, direction `2` at `+30`, face-extend option `1` or `2` at `+34`, direction reversal at `+38`, geometry kind at `+39`, and start support at `+40`. Three zero bytes at `+41..+43` precede a unit profile normal at `+44`; the seven nullable slots at `+68` have presence vector `absent, absent, absent, present, present, present, present`, and the slot run ends at `+115`. The first-side extent discriminator is `u32 2` at `+115`, the second-side discriminator is `u32 0` at `+287 = reference_count - 4`, and the ordered reference count is `13` at `+291`. The paired header begins at `+536`. This exact frame selects `TwoSidedToFaces` and remains separate from the other class-`296` grammars.

The 515-byte class-`296`/`261` legacy one-sided-to-face frame stores u32 `1` at `+20`, a zero byte at `+24`, a marked reference at `+25` with six trailing zero bytes through `+35`, the result operation at `+36`, direction `1` at `+40`, face-extend option `1` at `+44`, direction reversal at `+48`, geometry kind at `+49`, start support at `+50`, and three zero bytes at `+51..+53`. The 515-byte class-`296`/`261` legacy one-sided-to-face frame stores its profile scalar at `+54` when `+62..+77` are zero, or at `+70` when `+54..+69` are zero; the scalar is finite and has unit absolute value. Its seven nullable slots at `+78` have presence vector `present, absent, absent, present, absent, present, present`, ending at `+125`. The first-side extent discriminator is `u32 2` at `+125`, the second-side discriminator is `u32 0` at `+277 = reference_count - 4`, and the ordered reference count is `12` at `+281`. The paired header begins at `+515`. This exact frame selects `OneSidedToFace`.

The 483-byte class-`296`/`261` legacy one-sided-distance frame stores the same indexed anchor and common fields through `+53`, with face-extend option `2` at `+44`. The 483-byte class-`296`/`261` legacy one-sided-distance frame stores its profile scalar at `+70` when `+54..+69` are zero; the scalar is finite and has unit absolute value. Its seven nullable slots at `+78` have presence vector `present, absent, present, present, absent, present, absent`, ending at `+125`. The first-side extent discriminator is `u32 1` at `+125`, the second-side discriminator is `u32 0` at `+267 = reference_count - 4`, and the ordered reference count is `10` at `+271`. The paired header begins at `+483`. This exact frame selects `OneSidedDistance`; both legacy envelopes are separate from the compact class-`296` grammars.

The marked shifted Extrude prologue inserts marker byte `01` at primary-header offset 27. The result operation is at offset 28, travel direction at offset 32, face-extend option at offset 36, direction reversal at offset 40, geometry kind at offset 41, and start support at offset 42. A fully shifted symmetric distance form moves its extent fields, marked references, and scope reference-count field one byte forward: its reference-count field is at offset 284, its first-side extent `1` is at offset 117, and its second-side extent `0` is at offset 131. The marker does not change any field enum or parameter invariant.

The early distance-only Extrude prologue begins with one nullable field at primary-header offset 20. An absent field is one zero byte and places the operation u32 at offset 21. A present field is byte `01` and u32 zero and places the operation at offset 25. The extent-kind u32 value `2` follows the operation and selects one-sided fixed-distance travel. This dialect has no travel-direction field and no second-side extent lane. The direction-reversal Boolean follows the extent kind and reverses the profile-normal direction. A u32 geometry-kind discriminator follows the Boolean with `0 = sheet` and `1 = solid`. These forms use the profile plane as their start support. Their scope reference-count fields are at offsets 208 and 212 respectively.

An Extrude scope can carry one or both of its distance and taper inputs as owned fixed scalar frames among its ordered references. Each scalar frame is 100, 103, 104, or 105 bytes and stores `01 + u32 owning_scope_record_index` at primary-record offset 24, a u8 lane ordinal at offset 35, and a finite f64 value at offset 40. Lane zero is the nonzero signed `AlongDistance` in centimetres and lane one is the `TaperAngle` in radians. Every fixed Extrude lane names the Extrude scope as owner. The lane ordinals are unique. Indexed Design parameters can supply either input that has no fixed lane. When both carriers supply one input, their values are equal.

Another one-sided distance form stores the `AlongDistance` magnitude in a 100-byte owned construction frame. Ten zero bytes follow its indexed header. A marked reference to the owning Extrude scope occurs at offset 21, followed by six zero bytes and u32 one. Offset 36 stores a marked nonzero dimension reference, followed by six zero bytes. Offsets 47 and 59 both store u32 `210`, and the positive finite distance in centimetres begins at offset 51. Marked references at offsets 63 and 77 name records `N+2` and `N+1` for construction-frame record `N`; the first has six trailing zero bytes, and the second is preceded by bytes `01 00 00` and has six trailing zero bytes plus one zero byte. A second marked reference to the owning scope occurs at offset 89 and has six trailing zero bytes. In this form, owner-local scalar ordinal zero is the `Side1Offset` default and ordinal one is the taper angle. The distance construction frame replaces the ordinary ordinal-zero `AlongDistance` interpretation. The side-offset default is zero. The prologue direction-reversal Boolean selects the direction of this positive magnitude.

An Extrude face operand may use a 143-byte direct construction-group frame instead of the common counted-tail form. Ten zero bytes follow its primary indexed header. Offset 21 stores u32 one as the member count. Marked references at offsets 25, 36, and 47 name the sole member, the first auxiliary record, and the second auxiliary record; their indices equal the group index plus nine, three, and six respectively. Offset 58 stores u32 one. Offset 62 stores a marked nonzero persistent-selection discriminator with six trailing zero bytes. The u64 role at offset 73 is `0x0000001100000000`. The 26 bytes at offsets 81 through 106 are retained as opaque selection payload. Marked references at offsets 107 and 120 name the group index plus two and plus one; the first has six trailing zero bytes and two additional zero bytes, and the second has six trailing zero bytes followed by one zero byte. The marked reference at offset 132 names the owning Extrude scope and has six trailing zero bytes. A same-index paired header begins at offset 143. The sole member is the persistent entity-selection operand for the face.

A selected-face start group with role `0x0000000500000000` can contain a nested entity-selection operand instead of a face-recipe operand. The operand stores an asset UUID and context UUID and has same-index paired, `N+1`, `N+2`, and `N+3` indexed frames. The `N+3` identity frame has ten zero bytes followed by one u64 persistent identity and ends after 29 bytes at the next indexed header. The next record index is independent of `N`. The expanded entity-selection identity frame instead has eighteen zero bytes, two u64 identities, length 45, and is followed by record `N+4`.

The first identity in a selected-face entity operand is resolved independently in each ASM history namespace. A face identity supplies itself, a loop supplies its owning face, a coedge supplies the face owning its loop, a pcurve supplies the face owning each loop of a using coedge, and a surface supplies each face using that carrier. One history supplies a face proof only when every state containing the normalized identity maps it to the same single face. The operand resolves only when exactly one history supplies a face proof. Other identities in the construction path describe selection context and do not intersect the face candidate. When the selected history differs from the feature's operation history, the selected face is a history-qualified member of the feature's composite input topology.

Within a `Fillet` scope, an owned parameter whose `source_kind` is `Radius` carries a fillet radius. One positive finite radius selects the constant-radius form. `ChordLen` instead carries the positive transverse distance between the fillet's two support boundaries and selects the chordal form. The ordered pair `EdgeOffset1` and `EdgeOffset2` selects the asymmetric form and stores the positive offset on each support-face side. A grouped Fillet assigns every owned dimensional parameter and `TangencyWeight` parameter exactly once; every tangency weight is finite. An ungrouped constant-radius Fillet has exactly one `Radius` and no `TangencyWeight`. Other parameter families or unrepresentable scalar values retain the native feature. Fillet dimensional scalars use the parameter record's centimetre storage. Within a `Chamfer` scope, `Distance` or `leftDistance` alone selects an equal-distance chamfer; `Distance 1` with `Distance 2`, or `leftDistance` with `rightDistance`, selects the two-distance form; and `Distance` together with `Angle` or `Rotate Angle` selects the distance-angle form. These forms and parameter namespaces are mutually exclusive, and every owned parameter belongs to the selected form. Every distance is positive and finite; a distance-angle form's angle is finite and strictly between zero and pi radians. An incomplete, excess, mixed, unclassified, or unrepresentable parameter lane retains the native feature. The distance scalars use the parameter record's centimetre storage and the angle scalar uses its radian storage.

A Fillet scope without indexed Design parameters carries ordered fixed scalar lanes. One lane is one positive constant radius without an explicit tangency weight. An even lane count is one or more `(tangency_weight, constant_radius)` pairs; both values are positive, and the pairs correspond in order to the scope's counted edge-operand groups. An odd lane count of at least three is one variable law: lane zero is a positive dimensionless tangency weight, lanes one and two are nonnegative start and end radii, and the remaining lanes are zero or more `(intermediate_radius, normalized_parameter)` pairs. Intermediate radii are nonnegative. Their parameters are finite, strictly increasing, and strictly between zero and one. At least one radius is positive. The neutral variable law orders the start radius at parameter zero, the intermediate pairs in lane order, and the end radius at parameter one.

A Chamfer scope without indexed Design parameters carries one or two positive fixed scalar lanes in owner-local order. One lane is an equal-distance chamfer. Two lanes are the independently oriented first-face and second-face setback distances. Both forms have exactly one counted edge-operand group.

A `Thread` scope has a direct prefix or an owner-marked prefix. The direct prefix stores ten zero bytes at offsets 11 through 20 and f64 `60.0` at offset 21. Its five-byte form marker starts at offset 29, its four-byte form token starts at offset 34, and its three LP-UTF16 fields start at offset 38. The owner-marked prefix stores nine zero bytes at offsets 11 through 19, u32 `1` at offset 20, one zero byte at offset 24, and f64 `60.0` at offset 25. Its form marker starts at offset 33, its form token starts at offset 38, and its three LP-UTF16 fields start at offset 42. In either prefix, the standard form marker is `01 02 00 00 00` and its form token is `36 00 67 00`. The compact form marker is `00 02 00 00 00` and its form token is `36 00 48 00`.

The three LP-UTF16 fields are the designation, nominal-size text, and profile name. After the profile name, the five-byte construction marker is `00 01 00 00 00` in the standard form and `01 02 00 00 00` in the compact form. Major diameter is at marker-relative offset 5, minor diameter at 13, the pitch marker at 21, pitch at 22, and pitch diameter at 30. The pitch marker is `1` in the standard form and `0` in the compact form. The standard trailer at relative offset 38 is `00 01`; the compact trailer is `00 00 00 01`. The nominal-size text parses as one positive finite decimal value. The four physical scalars are finite and positive, and `minor diameter < pitch diameter < major diameter`. A Thread scope has a positive even reference count. The standard form's first reference is its face-selection group. Compact references are ordered `(face-selection group, sole group member)` pairs. Every Thread face-selection group has role `0x0000001000000000`. Each group member carries a face-recipe operand. The first persistent Design reference in that operand names the threaded-face candidate lane. Later references are construction context and do not enter that lane. The selected historical face is the sole first-reference candidate in the immediately preceding ASM topology. If several candidates occur in that topology, the selected face is the sole candidate deleted or updated across the complete state chain to the Thread result state. The lane can carry one local face candidate for each saved BREP revision. When these local indices are not stable identities in the preceding topology, exactly one candidate must belong to the BREP that carries the selected ASM history. The threaded face is then the sole deleted or updated preceding face bound to a right-circular cylinder whose radius, in millimetres, is within the inclusive interval from half the stored minor diameter through half the stored major diameter after centimetre-to-millimetre conversion. A plural Thread concatenates member selections in face-group order, removes repeated faces after their first occurrence, and requires every member to resolve against the same preceding state.

A `Loft` has at least two ordered section groups. Role `0x0000004100000000` and role `0x0000004300000000` identify section groups. A role-`0x0000000500000000` group that follows two or more such sections identifies a rail. One role-`0x0000000700000000` group identifies a centerline when the Loft has no rail. Rails and a centerline are mutually exclusive. A NewBody unguided form can instead use role `0x0000000500000000` consistently across every section group. A heterogeneous point-to-profile NewBody form has one role-`0x0000000500000000` endpoint group with one point-selection member, one role-`0x0000004300000000` profile group, and one or more further role-`0x0000000500000000` profile-boundary groups. The point group is first or last, and the remaining role-`0x0000000500000000` groups have multiple members. Scope-reference order is section or rail order. Mixing these forms or retaining fewer than two sections does not define a complete neutral loft.

The compact fixed-prefix `Loft` scope has ten zero bytes after its indexed header, four bytes of value `1`, and the result-operation u32 at primary-header offset 25. Byte 29 is zero, offsets 30 through 33 are `ff ff ff ff`, and offsets 34 through 44 are zero. The operation values are `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, and `4 = new body`. Other `Loft` scope forms store the same operation at offset 29.

A role-`0x0000004100000000` or role-`0x0000004300000000` Loft section group with one member can carry the sketch-profile frame defined for Extrude. Its asset UUID and decimal Sketch entity suffix identify the section Sketch in the same Design stream.

A sketch-profile record `N` can carry a nested profile-region selection after its same-index paired header. Indexed records `N+1`, `N+2`, and `N+3` occur in that order. The `N+3` selection starts with its eleven-byte indexed header, ten zero bytes, a marked reference to `N`, six zero bytes, u32 `1`, and a nonzero u32 region count. The first region starts with a nonzero u32 member count. Each later region starts with byte `01` and a nonzero u32 member count. Each member is 40 bytes: u32 kind `3`, one nonzero u32 primary persistent Sketch-curve identity, three zero u32 values, three incidence values, and two zero u32 values. The first incidence value is zero or one. The second and third incidence values are one or two. Five zero bytes terminate the region run immediately before the same-index `N+3` companion header. Region order and member order are significant. A record without this nested selection selects the complete referenced Sketch. For a spatial Sketch, it selects every neutral profile in profile order.

For a spatial Sketch Loft section, the first member of each selected region identifies one unique neutral profile boundary. Further members identify curves on that boundary or geometrically coincident full-circle revisions of its curves. Coincident circles have equal centers and radii within the document linear tolerance and parallel or opposite normals within the document angular tolerance. Every selected region must resolve, and different regions must resolve to different profile indices. Otherwise, the section retains its native spatial-Sketch selection.

A one-member role-`0x0000000500000000` Loft section group can carry one entity-selection frame. Its first identity names the spatial Sketch entity suffix, and its second identity names one persistent curve. It selects the unique neutral profile whose boundary contains that curve. No matching profile or more than one matching profile leaves the section unresolved.

A fixed `Pipe` scope has four owner-local scalar references at ordinals zero through three, one settings reference, and one counted path-selection group at reference ordinal five. Two trailing references follow the path members, so the scope reference count is eight more than the path-member count. Primary-header offset 25 stores u32 `4`, which selects a new-body result. Offset 29 value `1` selects a circular section and offset 30 value `1` selects a filled section. Scalar ordinals zero and one are the forward and reverse path fractions; both are `1.0` for the full-path form. Scalar ordinal two is the circular-section diameter in centimetres. Scalar ordinal three retains the positive inactive section-thickness value. The role-`0x0000000500000000` path group contains the persistent edge selections in path order. The full-path circular filled form projects as a solid sweep of a generated disk whose radius is half the section diameter. It has no sketch-profile operand and no wall thickness.

Class-`421` paired with class-`257` uses the fixed `Pipe` operation prefix and a legacy reference table. It has four class-`342` parameter-owner references of length `103`, one settings reference, one role-`0x0000000500000000` path group, and that group's nonempty ordered members. The four owners have scope-record index equal to the `Pipe` scope and local ordinals zero through three. Their finite scalar values are, in order, the forward path fraction, reverse path fraction, circular-section diameter, and section thickness. The scope reference count is six more than the path-member count. No other construction group or trailing scope reference is part of this form.

Class-`405` paired with class-`259`, and class-`475` paired with class-`260`, use a legacy `Pipe` operation prefix. Relative to the primary indexed header, offsets `11` through `19` are zero, offset `20` is u8 `1`, offsets `21` through `25` are zero, offset `26` is the result-operation u32, offset `30` is the section-shape u8, and offset `31` is the filled-section u8. The operation values are the same as the fixed `Pipe` form. Neutral projection admits the legacy reference form when the scope carries exactly four owner-local scalar references, one settings reference, one role-`0x0000000500000000` path group, and that group's nonempty ordered members, with no other construction group or trailing scope reference. It retains other legacy reference forms as native records.

A `SurfacePatch` scope in the fixed path form has `3n + 1` ordered references and length `340 + 44(n - 1)` or `354 + 44(n - 1)` bytes for positive boundary-component count `n`. Each counted construction-operand group has role `0x0000000400000000`, one member, and the member immediately follows the group. The settings record immediately follows that member. The group, member, and settings triples occur in boundary order. The one remaining reference is the tool body and occurs at the first or last scope-reference ordinal. The ordered group members together form the complete closed path boundary of the generated surface patch. This form has no support-face group.

A `SurfacePatch` scope in the single-group path form has `R` ordered references and length `277 + 11R` bytes. Reference zero is one counted construction-operand group with role `0x0000000400000000`. Its ordered, nonempty member list equals references one through `R - 2`. Reference `R - 1` is the tool body. The group is the complete closed path boundary. This form has no boundary-settings record and no support-face group.

The single-group path form has implicit connected continuity because it has no boundary-settings record. Its neutral `FilledSurface` continuity is `Contact`, and its boundary-continuities vector is empty.

A surface-patch boundary-settings record has an empty display name and a class-version prologue of two zero bytes. Its class level is `u8 IsSeedSel`, `u32 PatchContinuity`, `u32 PatchFlip`, `f64 PatchScale`, and the `rPatchModelRef` reference, at payload offsets 2, 3, 7, 11, and 19; the base level's reference run closes the record. The three named settings apply to the one boundary component the record belongs to, not to the patch as a whole. `PatchContinuity` value `0` imposes positional continuity on that component, `1` imposes tangency, and `2` imposes curvature. `rPatchModelRef` names that component's model reference: it equals the construction-operand group record immediately preceding the settings record, so the settings record and the boundary group name the same component from both directions.

A `SurfacePatch` creates a new surface body. Its neutral `FilledSurface.merge_result` is therefore `false`; the scope has no independent merge-operation field.

The tool-body reference of a fixed-path settings-bearing `SurfacePatch` scope is the unclaimed endpoint reference. Its record's payload contains the scope's own entity id. In the single-group path form, the tool body is the last ordered reference. The sketch-profile form has no separate tool-body reference.

A `SurfacePatch` scope in the sketch-profile form is 339 bytes and has three ordered references. Reference zero is one counted construction-operand group with role `0x0000004100000000`; that group has reference one as its sole member and is the complete closed sketch boundary. Reference two carries patch settings. This form has no support-face group.

A `SurfaceExtend` scope has at least three ordered references. Reference zero is a 104-byte owned fixed scalar record. It has the fixed-scalar prefix, names the owning scope at marked-reference bytes 24, 67, and 93, and has owner-local ordinal zero. Its positive f64 at byte 40 is the extension distance in centimetres. Marked references at bytes 48 and 81 name scalar records `N - 1` and `N + 1`, respectively, where `N` is the distance-scalar record index. The u32 at byte 59 is nonzero. Reference one is the boundary carrier. Every remaining scope reference is an edge-recipe operand, in boundary order.

For `n` edge operands, the boundary carrier is `113 + 11n` bytes. Its first header is followed by ten zero bytes, a u32 count `n`, and `n` marked references of 11 bytes each. The references equal the scope references after the first two. An 88-byte tail follows the reference run. The tail starts with two zero bytes and a u32 extension-method enum: zero continues the source surface parameterization, one creates tangent faces, and two creates perpendicular faces. A nonnull marked reference starts at tail byte 6. The u32 at tail byte 21 is 8. The f64 at tail byte 39 is a positive modelling tolerance. The u32 values at tail bytes 35 and 47 are both 210. Marked references at tail bytes 51 and 65 name boundary-carrier records `N + 2` and `N + 1`, respectively, where `N` is the boundary-carrier record index. A marked reference at tail byte 77 names the owning `SurfaceExtend` scope. The distance scalar and complete boundary carrier are one atomic construction.

A `SurfaceOffset` scope has one of two support forms. Its 104-byte owner-local scalar stores a finite signed offset distance in centimetres and has owner-local ordinal zero. The boundary-carrier form uses the ordered distance-scalar, boundary-carrier, and edge-recipe partition of `SurfaceExtend`. Its boundary carrier has the same count-dependent length, reference run, auxiliary references, tolerance fields, and 88-byte tail. The boundary-mode u32 at tail byte 2 is 1, and the u32 at tail byte 21 is 65. The operation copies the selected support surfaces at the signed normal offset.

The grouped support form places the distance scalar at reference zero. Every remaining scope reference is either a counted construction-operand group or a member of one of those groups. Each group has role `0x0000004100000000`, opaque index `252`, and a nonempty member list. The group records and their ordered members partition the remaining scope references. Each member is a bounded-face recipe, and the union of the group members is the selected support-face set. This form has no boundary carrier or edge-recipe list. The operation copies those support faces at the signed normal offset.

A `BaseFlange` scope is 416 bytes with four ordered references. Reference zero is one standard counted construction group with sole member reference one and role `0x0000004100000000`; that member is the scope's sketch-profile record. Reference two is the indexed thickness-construction record and reference three is the operation-settings record. The scope repeats references three, two, and zero at primary-record offsets 82, 113, and 146. A positive finite f64 at primary-record offset 123 stores the sheet thickness in centimetres. The operation creates a planar sheet body from the closed sketch profile and places the thickness along the sketch plane's positive normal.

A `SurfaceStitch` scope has `2n + 2` ordered references for positive input-surface count `n`. At each even ordinal `2i`, a metadata-prefixed construction-operand group has reference `2i + 1` as its sole member and role `0x0000000500000000`. The group prefix after its indexed header is nine zero bytes, `01 + u32 1`, LP-ASCII `DcFeatureOperationIdFlag`, LP-ASCII `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64`, and a u64 metadata value; the standard counted-group member count follows that value. The penultimate scope reference is a fixed scalar record owned by the scope at ordinal zero; its positive finite f64 is the maximum stitched-boundary gap in centimetres. The final reference names the operation-settings record. The operation merges coincident entities, joins every selected surface body, and converts a closed result to a solid body. Each group member is a nested entity-selection record. When its primary identity resolves to exactly one history face candidate for the operation input, the complete ordered member set resolves to a historical face selection only when every member has one candidate and the complete group/reference grammar matches. Otherwise the native selection is retained.

A `BoundaryFill` scope in the two-cell body form is 413 bytes and has eight ordered references. Reference zero is a counted boundary-tool group with role `0x0000000400000000` and references one and two as its ordered body-recipe members. References three and five are counted selected-cell groups with role `0x0000000500000000`; their sole body-recipe members are references four and six respectively. Reference seven carries the operation settings. Each selected-cell group produces one solid result body. For each body-recipe reference, persistent candidate faces present in the exact preceding topology supply their owning body set through complete body-region-shell-face incidence. The intersection across every reference in one member must contain exactly one body. A complete group resolves to its member-ordered, deduplicated historical body selection; an unresolved member retains the complete native group.

A `Split` scope has two nonempty counted construction groups that partition its ordered references. Reference zero is the tool group. Its members follow contiguously. The next reference is the target group with role `0x0000000400000000`; every remaining reference is one of its body-recipe members. A role-`0x0000000900000000` tool group has exactly one member. That member is a face recipe with the complete terminal program `[0, −1]`, no positive header value, and no topology nodes. Its unique referenced face in the exact preceding topology is the splitting tool. A role-`0x0000002100000000` tool group has one or more surface or plane construction members and retains the complete group as the splitting tool. Both tool roles use the `DcFeatureOperationIdFlag` metadata prefix before the member count. The operation partitions every target body with the tool surfaces and retains every result body.

A `SplitFace` scope with `r` ordered references is `290 + 11(r - 1)` bytes, or one byte longer in its alternate frame. The class-418/266 and class-277/258 forms are `286 + 11(r - 1)` bytes. These compact forms share a fixed prefix with eleven zero bytes at offsets 11 through 21, byte `01` at offset 22, four zero bytes at offsets 23 through 26, bytes `01 01` at offsets 27 and 28, and three zero bytes at offsets 29 through 31; the first marked construction reference starts at offset 32. Their u32 ordered-reference count is at offset 168, the first ordered-reference marker is at offset 172, and the LP-UTF16 kind payload starts at `180 + 11r`. Every frame contains exactly two nonempty counted construction-operand groups. The first reference is the splitting-tool group with role `0x0000002100000000`; it uses the `DcFeatureOperationIdFlag` metadata prefix defined for `SurfaceStitch` groups. A path tool has nested entity-selection members whose primary identity names one planar Sketch and whose secondary identity names one curve in that Sketch. An edge path tool instead has nested entity-selection members with no secondary or curve-secondary identity; each primary identity must resolve through history to exactly one edge in the feature's preceding topology. The ordered edge slots form a historical path in the feature input topology. If any member fails this proof, the tool remains native. A plane tool has one or more compact entity-selection members. Each member's sole identity `p` names the WorkPlane scope at record index `p + 1`. The ordered WorkPlane features are the tool dependencies. The second group immediately follows the tool members in the scope reference table, has role `0x0000001000000000`, and contains the bounded-face operands partitioned by the operation. The two groups and their members consume the complete reference table. A path projects its ordered Sketch curves or historical input edges onto the selected faces; every plane extends through them. Both forms retain every resulting face region.

In a `SplitFace` target group, a complete counted bounded-face member whose effective active candidate set is wholly present in the preceding face candidates contributes that set. When the member's own candidate lanes are empty, faces named by its nested recipe references are topology supports. Stable surface-carrier identity maps each support to the preceding topology. A unique preceding support, a unique changed preceding support, or a unique complete preceding boundary set whose total edge count equals the counted recipe header contributes the mapped face set. Without one of those proofs, the member is topology context and contributes no target face. Every other member must contribute a proven face set, and at least one member must contribute. The target list follows member order and removes repeated stable face slots after its first occurrence.

When a `SplitFace` transition has an updated face set whose cardinality equals the target-group member count, that transition supplies a group proof when per-member recipe proofs are incomplete. Every member must have a nonempty preceding candidate lane, and the union of those lanes intersected with the updated face set must equal the updated face set. The projected target list is that union in first member and preceding-lane order. A member with no updated candidate is topology context. Duplicate updated slots, an uncovered updated slot, an empty preceding lane, or a cardinality mismatch rejects this proof. Inserted face slots are result geometry and do not enter the target list.

An `Extrude`, `Fillet`, or `Chamfer` scope with null current and preceding history-state identities is suppressed. An active scope in these families carries both identities and its current state is the operation result. A `Sketch` scope does not modify ASM topology and may carry null state identities without being suppressed. A neutral native feature fallback retains every scope-table reference as its zero-based ordinal and decimal record index; repeated indices remain repeated at distinct ordinals.

Every resolved Fillet or Chamfer edge group identifies stable edge slots in the scope's immediately preceding ASM topology. The neutral feature-input topology roster contains every body, face, and edge slot in that state. The group projects its ordered deduplicated slots as typed historical edge identities in that roster and retains the native group identity. Historical edge identities never address the active B-rep edge arena and require no active descendant.

A Fillet or Chamfer scope containing exactly one edge operand resolves that operand to the sole preceding edge deleted across its complete state chain. This proof does not require a face-reference envelope because no other edge operand or deleted predecessor edge can participate in the operation.

Every `Fillet` and `Chamfer` scope has at least one edge operand in its ordered reference table. An edge operand's primary indexed header has record index `N`. It is followed by a same-index paired header and three consecutive nested indexed headers with record indices `N+1`, `N+2`, and `N+3`. Exactly one `edge_recipe_data` construction recipe occurs after the `N+3` header and before the following indexed header. The bytes after the `N+3` header and before the family-name length field are retained as the recipe prefix; selector-bearing prefixes use the persistent Design operand grammar defined above, and every decoded selector/reference pair carries independently bound face and edge candidates. From the end of the recipe-family name to the following indexed header, the payload is a nonempty, contiguous sequence of little-endian i32 values with no padding. Its first seven words comprise an i64 null sentinel and five-i32 prologue; delimiter words in the prologue follow the side-clause delimiter form. The complete sequence and its first-byte offset are retained as the recipe program. The recipe's nonnegative record-index field is its persistent Design reference and joins to every active face tag carrying that reference; the candidate face set is ordered by face identity and deduplicated.

Each operand retains the candidate faces and their stable boundary-edge slots present in the scope's result ASM topology. Each prefix reference uses its exact-selector face set when nonempty and otherwise uses its alternate-selector face set for historical boundary context. Exact-selector faces take precedence when both sets are nonempty. The scope's current state reaches its declared preceding state through one or more transitions in the bound ASM history. Every intermediate state and transition must be present, and a repeated state makes the chain invalid. The operand retains the candidate faces present in the declared preceding topology, the subset deleted or assigned a different revision by any transition in the complete chain, the stable edge slots on those preceding face boundaries, and the subset of boundary edges changed along the chain. Deleted boundary slots occur in the preceding topology, do not occur in the result topology, and occur in a deleted-edge delta in the chain. Updated boundary slots occur in both endpoint topologies and occur in a deleted-edge or updated-edge delta in the chain. Their ordered union is the changed boundary set. Result treatment faces occur in the result topology and not in the preceding topology. A missing or invalid chain produces no transition-derived proof. Every changed boundary edge retains each incident coedge in stable coedge-slot order with its owner loop and face, loop boundary count, zero-based coedge ordinal, and preceding and following edge slots.

A topology-entry selector is nonnegative, occurs at most once in each ordered clause, and increases strictly within a clause. Equal selector values group topology-context entries across clauses; a selector absent from a clause is one-sided. Each topology triplet is `[v, e, v]`, where `v` is a one-based loop-vertex ordinal not exceeding the entry's boundary-edge count and `e` is a signed i32. `e = v - 1` names the edge preceding that vertex, and `e = v` names the following edge. The decoded zero-based edge ordinal is `(v + boundary_edge_count - 2) mod boundary_edge_count` for the preceding form and `v - 1` for the following form. Other signed middle values retain no incident-edge derivation. When both triplets have incident-edge derivations, the ordered pair is conjunctive: the selected historical edge occurs at both named coedge ordinals among its incident loops. Each clause retains the changed historical edge slots occurring at a loop position named by a derived triplet. A selector containing an underived triplet has no incidence proof. For each selector context, the decoder also retains every changed boundary edge whose distinct incident-loop boundary counts satisfy all present clause entries. Selector contexts are ordered by selector value.

An edge operand has six exact candidate proofs. The reference proof intersects every nonempty changed reference-edge set and requires at least two such sets. The incidence proof intersects every selector's complete boundary-count-and-coedge-ordinal candidate set with every nonempty changed reference-edge set. The cardinality proof intersects every selector's boundary-count candidate set with those same changed reference-edge sets. The common-triplet proof applies when both triplets of an entry name the same incident-edge ordinal; it intersects their historical edge sets across every such entry and every nonempty changed reference-edge set, then resolves only a single common stable edge slot. The cross-clause triplet proof pairs the first triplet of one selector's first side with the first triplet of its second side and likewise pairs the two second triplets. It intersects each nonempty positional pair, every other nonempty positional pair, and every nonempty changed reference-edge set, then resolves only a single common stable edge slot. The deleted-triplet proof collects every historical edge named by every topology triplet and intersects that set with the preceding boundary edges deleted by the feature transition; it resolves only a single stable edge slot. A suppressed scope has no feature transition. For every prefix reference, the operand retains the boundary-edge set of its exact-selector faces, or its alternate-selector faces when the exact set is empty, in the retained recipe-state topology; these sets are indexed by zero-based prefix-reference ordinal. A structured recipe's nonzero local topology references select those sets by one-based ordinal in source-field order. The reference, incidence, cardinality, common-triplet, and cross-clause proofs use the selected boundary-edge sets in place of changed reference-edge sets. A proof resolves when its intersection contains exactly one stable edge slot. Every resolving proof must name the same slot. The proven slot is retained on the native operand independently of whether every member in its containing group resolves. A group without recipe operands uses its ordered persistent selection identities and retains unresolved identities in a partial historical selection. A group with recipe operands also resolves through those identities when every identity resolves and no member has a structured recipe, resolved recipe slot, changed recipe boundary, or treatment-radius candidate. In every other recipe group, identity fields are topology context and do not override recipe evidence. For group assignment, a selector-free operand contributes the complete intersection of its first two reference-edge sets. An operand without two reference-edge sets is topology context. Empty or disjoint leading reference sets identify an unresolved edge-bearing operand. A structured operand contributes its complete selector/reference candidate set. When those per-operand proofs do not resolve a group, each operand can instead contribute the intersection of all of its nonempty changed reference-edge sets. Every member must contribute a nonempty set in this group-level form. A group resolves when these edge-bearing member sets admit exactly one perfect assignment of distinct stable edge slots. Removing any assigned member-slot pair must eliminate every perfect assignment. The projected edge list follows edge-bearing member order and removes repeated proven identities after their first occurrence. A constant-radius Fillet group additionally uses inserted treatment faces adjacent to each operand's result candidate faces. The inserted face's surface carrier must be absent from the preceding topology and have a positive analytic or constant-blend characteristic radius. Every adjacent result face used as a support must map through one stable surface carrier to exactly one preceding face. A deleted preceding edge shared by two such supports is a radius-qualified candidate. Radius-qualified candidates remain transition evidence when they are outside that member's changed or deleted boundary. They contribute through the constant-radius chain proof and not through the unique member assignment. A group also resolves as an unordered set when its members have distinct operand identities, every edge-bearing member carries the same nonempty set of deleted preceding boundary-edge slots, and the number of distinct slots equals the edge-bearing member count. Members without changed boundary edges are topology context. This cardinality form projects the slots in increasing stable-slot order. Edge groups in one scope also form an exact partition when every operand identity is distinct, every operand carries the same nonempty deleted-slot set, the total operand count equals that set's cardinality, exactly one group contains unresolved members, and every member of every other group resolves to a distinct member of the set. The incomplete group resolves as the increasing-slot-order complement of the sibling assignments when its member count equals the complement cardinality and each of its already resolved members belongs to the complement. When every member identity was replaced by a lost-reference record, a complete compact edge-treatment transition set whose cardinality equals the member count resolves independently of recipe-boundary context. When no complete group proof exists but at least one member has an exact proof, the neutral selection retains the ordered deduplicated proven historical edges and the stable native identities of every unresolved edge-bearing member as a partial historical selection; topology-context members are omitted from both lists. All forms retain the native group identity. A missing operand, duplicate operand identity, contradictory proof, or absent complete group proof prevents complete resolution. A lost-reference marker prevents complete resolution only when no independent complete group proof exists. For every prefix reference in source order, the operand retains its referenced faces in both the result and preceding topologies. Every referenced face retains its face slot, its loops in face-membership order, and each loop's aligned coedge and edge slots in cyclic membership order. When adjacent loop edges share exactly one vertex and its carrier bindings are unique, the loop also retains aligned stable vertex slots, point slots, and model-space point positions. A referenced result face also retains a preceding support face when the result face has exactly one surface-carrier binding and that carrier has exactly one face owner in the preceding topology. The support face retains the same ordered boundary context. A prefix reference's changed reference-edge set contains every deleted or updated boundary edge of its directly persistent preceding faces or unique preceding support faces. Each topology side separately retains the stable edge slots shared by the directly referenced face boundaries and the primary candidate-face boundaries. The recipe order follows the scope reference-table order.

Loft section, guide, and centerline groups can carry the same edge-recipe operand envelope and selector grammar. Role-`0x0000004100000000` and role-`0x0000004300000000` Loft section groups can instead carry the bounded-face recipe operand envelope. Their recipe candidates are evaluated in the Loft scope's preceding history topology.

For an edge-recipe Loft section group, each member carries one direct face context for each recipe reference. A target-face clause is a `(selector, token, Design reference)` tuple that occurs exactly once in every member and has one exact face candidate. Its candidate face, preceding face, result face, and unique preceding support face are the same stable face in every member. Exactly one common target-face clause whose complete preceding boundary has one edge for every group member resolves the section as that historical input face. A missing, competing, or contradictory clause retains the native group.

A complete Loft guide, Loft centerline, Sweep path, or SurfacePatch boundary group identifies an ordered path in the preceding history topology. A group with an unresolved member does not identify a complete path. A Sweep edge recipe resolves to the unique incidence-compatible edge carried by at least two distinct referenced face boundaries. Repeated local-reference ordinals name the same boundary set and do not add independent support.

A `SurfacePatch` edge operand uses an alternate recipe tail after the seven-word edge-recipe prologue. The tail starts with root `2` and contains exactly two clauses. Each clause has six `-1`-delimited nonnegative fields, a nonnegative topology-entry count, exactly eight words per counted topology entry, and a `-1` delimiter. The fields are, in order: one scalar or `[2, reference]`, one face-reference scalar, `[2, reference]`, `[0, 0]`, one edge-reference scalar, and `[0, 0]`. The reference is the field's final scalar. The two clauses share exactly one face-reference ordinal and exactly one edge-reference ordinal. The shared face reference supplies the supporting face; the shared edge reference supplies the target edge candidates. Exact candidate faces take precedence, and alternate-selector faces are used when the exact set is empty. The target candidates intersect the shared supporting face's boundary edges in the preceding history topology. Exactly one stable edge slot resolves the operand. An empty, disjoint, or non-unique intersection remains unresolved. The counted topology entries use the standard eight-word entry grammar. A final zero word may follow the second clause delimiter. This alternate tail is retained as `SurfacePatch` structure and is not parsed as the standard two-side tail.

In the settings-bearing fixed-path form, boundary groups resolve in scope-reference order. When every group resolves in the same preceding history state, their ordered edge identities form one historical path and retain the SurfacePatch scope selection as the native path identity. If any group is unresolved or the groups do not share one history state, the complete native boundary selection is retained.

A nested entity-selection member has primary indexed record `N`. Its header is followed by ten zero bytes, `01 + u32 N+3 + six zero bytes`, `u32 1`, an LP-UTF16 asset UUID, an LP-UTF16 context UUID, `u32 2`, and four zero bytes. A same-index paired record and nested records `N+1`, `N+2`, and `N+3` follow in that order. The `N+3` record is 45 bytes: its eleven-byte indexed header, eighteen zero bytes, two u64 identity values, then the `N+4` indexed header. The complete member retains its owning construction group and group-member ordinal. Each identity is normalized through the complete history entity-version map. In the feature's exact preceding topology, an edge identity supplies itself, a coedge supplies its incident edge, a pcurve supplies the edges of its using coedges, a curve supplies its using edges, and a vertex or point supplies its incident edges. Identities absent from the preceding state contribute no candidate. The member resolves when the intersection of every available identity candidate set contains exactly one edge. A complete ordered group of resolved members supplies a historical path. Loft section groups can carry this member form. In a role-`0x0000000500000000` Loft section or guide group, the first identity can name the unique owning spatial Sketch entity and the second identity names one unique persistent curve owned by that Sketch. The member then supplies a source-native profile or path selection within the neutral spatial Sketch; both ownership and curve identity are required.

In a Loft guide or centerline group whose members use nested entity-selection frames, the group member run is an ordered curve selection. Every member has one unique operand at its group ordinal, all members share one asset UUID, context UUID, and owning Sketch identity, and each second identity names one unique curve identity owned by that Sketch. Duplicate member records or curve identities, missing neutral curve entities, multiple placements, or any other ambiguity retain the native group. A planar Sketch placement projects the ordered identities as `PathRef::SketchCurves`; a spatial Sketch placement projects the ordered neutral entities as `PathRef::SpatialSketchCurves`. The member order is preserved.

The same ordered-curve proof applies to a Sweep path or guide-rail group. Its selected curve projects as `PathRef::SketchCurves` or `PathRef::SpatialSketchCurves` for planar or spatial placement, respectively, even when the owning Sketch contains other geometry.

The transition-evaluated boundary-count candidates also prove an unordered edge group when every recipe member carries a structured selector and the candidate union cardinality equals the complete recipe-member count. Selectors with no boundary-count candidate contribute topology context. The projected group follows increasing stable edge-slot order. A radius-group member without a matching treatment candidate contributes topology context when its changed boundary intersects the complete set of exact and radius-qualified group edges. A changed member disjoint from that set prevents radius-group resolution.

Structured recipe selectors evaluate every deleted or updated edge present in the preceding topology. Primary candidate-face boundaries restrict separate boundary proofs but do not restrict the selector candidate universe.

The structured edge-recipe program has a standard tail that starts with a positive i32 clause count followed by that many ordered side clauses. A zero-or-minus-one delimiter follows the clause count, each side's two-word header, and each of its scalar fields. A zero-or-minus-one delimiter also separates consecutive sides; a final minus-one delimiter may follow the last side. The positive header count equals the number of scalar fields plus one for the payload and determines every scalar boundary when a scalar or delimiter is zero. After the scalars, a side retains an exact field-program prefix followed by a nonnegative i32 entry count `n` and exactly `8n` topology-entry words. The compact field-program prefix is one i32 zero. An extended field program terminates with i32 minus one before the entry count. Exactly one complete division into the counted sides is valid. The header's second word and every side scalar are retained i32 values. When every nonzero value among those fields is a positive one-based prefix-reference ordinal, those ordinals are additionally retained in source-field order with repetitions; otherwise the recipe has no local-reference binding. An entry is an i32 side-local selector, a positive i32 count of edges in the referenced face loop, and two topology triplets. Each triplet retains its three words and zero-based vertex ordinal; adjacent middle-word forms additionally retain the derived incident-edge ordinal and preceding/following side. When both triplets derive the same incident-edge ordinal, the entry also retains that common ordinal. The clause count, header count, header value, every scalar word, complete field-program prefix, payload entry count, and every entry field are retained. A referenced result face whose stable slot is absent from the preceding state maps through its invariant surface carrier to the unique preceding support face. The reference edge set contains every deleted or updated boundary edge of the directly persistent reference face or that mapped support face. In a selectorless group member, fewer than two nonempty local reference-edge sets encode topology context rather than an edge assignment. Two nonempty sets assign their shared edge candidates; disjoint sets encode an unresolved assignment. For a state-bound edge-treatment group, each member's changed reference edges intersect its deleted boundary edges. A unique bipartite assignment across every assigning member resolves the complete ordered selection; context members do not add selection cardinality. No assignment or more than one assignment leaves the group unresolved. A tail outside this grammar remains an exact unstructured program.

An `Extrude` face-group member and an edge-treatment bounded-face member have the same indexed-record envelope: primary record `N`, a same-index paired header, and consecutive nested records `N+1`, `N+2`, and `N+3`. Exactly one `face_recipe_data` or `bounded_face_recipe_data` construction recipe occurs after the `N+3` header and before the immediately following indexed header, even when the enclosing construction group continues to a later indexed record. The exact recipe family is retained per face operand. The i32 at 16 bytes before the family-name bytes is the recipe's indexed-record identity. Several bounded-face recipes in one group can carry the same identity; this field is not a second binary design ID. The bytes after the `N+3` header and before the family-name length field are retained as the recipe prefix; selector-bearing prefixes use the persistent Design operand grammar defined above, and every decoded selector/reference pair carries independently bound face and edge candidates. From the end of the framed recipe-family name to the following indexed header, the payload is a nonempty, contiguous sequence of little-endian i32 values with no padding. The complete sequence and its first-byte offset are retained as the recipe program. A counted program starts with `i32 0`, an i32 zero-or-minus-one prelude, and a positive i32 header value. It may carry a counted prelude after that header before its first node opener, and it may contain no node. When nodes are present, the first opener is at word index at least three and each node starts with `i32 -1`, `i32 -1`, `i32 2`; the next node opener or the following indexed header terminates it. The terminal Split form consists only of the first two words and has no nodes. The node payload is a `-1`-delimited root scalar, two one-word prelude runs, and two topology side clauses. Each side clause has the same counted header, two or three one-word scalars, zero-tagged counted payload, and ordered eight-word entry grammar as an edge recipe side. The root, first prelude scalar, and every side scalar are retained in source-field order with repetitions. Each node retains this structure, its complete word sequence, its opener byte offset, and its exclusive terminating byte offset. The recipe's persistent Design reference joins to every active B-rep face whose persistent-subentity tag contains that reference; the resulting candidate set is ordered by B-rep face identity and deduplicated. Prefix operands carrying the recipe's own Design reference divide those faces by selector. Faces whose tag selector equals the prefix selector are topology context. Faces carrying the same token and Design reference under a different selector are the selected active face set; a nonempty selected set resolves as a whole in face-identity order. When no alternate-selector face exists, subtracting the topology-context faces produces the ordered unreferenced candidate set. An empty unreferenced set retains the broad candidates because the selected historical face has no active unreferenced candidate. Each effective active candidate also maps to its invariant surface-carrier slot when every historical occurrence of that face slot has exactly one binding to the same carrier. The operand retains every face in the scope's preceding topology that owns this carrier, each owner's ordered loop boundary context, and the subset deleted or updated across the complete state chain from the preceding state to the scope result state. Every intermediate transition must be present and uniquely state-bound. Missing, conflicting, or multiply bound active carrier identities produce no support mapping. Without an alternate-selector proof, a face operand resolves to the sole effective candidate whose stable face slot is present in the scope's preceding ASM topology. If several candidates are present, it resolves to the sole candidate whose slot is deleted or assigned a different record revision along the scope's ASM transition chain. Inserted slots are result geometry and do not select an input face. For a grouped bounded-face operand outside the Thread and Split families, the intersection of its Design references, the preceding topology's stable face slots, and the transition-changed face slots selects the face only when it contains exactly one slot. When every effective active candidate has a support mapping and every mapping names the same nonempty predecessor-face set, that complete common set resolves the operand. For a fully structured bounded-face program, the positive header equals the node count or the total loop-edge cardinality of a complete predecessor-face boundary set. Individual complete support contexts and the unique union of consistent boundaries across all contexts are candidates; exactly one mapped set with that cardinality resolves, including a multi-face set. The first recipe reference identifies the result-face support used to select a unique preceding support face when direct stable-slot resolution fails. A selected-face start with several remaining candidates resolves to the sole planar candidate coincident with the selected Sketch plane within the document linear and angular tolerances. A face slot proven by either history or unique sketch-plane coincidence is retained on the native operand. A profile face group also resolves as an unordered historical-face set when exactly one surface carrier in the preceding topology owns a changed-face set whose cardinality equals the group member count, every changed face has one carrier binding, and no member carries contradictory direct or resolved prefix-reference candidates. Prefix-reference syntax with no face candidate is not selection evidence. The projected set follows increasing stable face-slot order. A start or termination face group resolves when every ordered member resolves. Alternate-selector members contribute their complete selected set. A fully structured bounded-face member whose positive header equals its parsed node count or a complete historical predecessor-boundary cardinality contributes its complete nonempty unreferenced candidate set. Other members contribute their single proven face. The resolved selection is the member-ordered, deduplicated face list and retains the native group identity. In a direct `OffsetFaces` operand, prefix references with equal selector-field and token-field offsets form one selected-face clause. The intersection of every reference's effective candidates with the preceding topology and transition-changed faces must contain one face. Clauses contribute an ordered, deduplicated historical face list. When no clause resolves, the general unique preceding-candidate and unique transition-changed-candidate proofs apply. An `OffsetFaces` scope with a known preceding state retains proven predecessor faces and unresolved operand identities as a partial historical selection when only a proper subset of its ordered face operands resolves.

For a legacy `Extrude` face-group member, let `r` be the `record_index` of its `bounded_face` construction recipe. The result-face lane is the union of effective candidates from every prefix reference whose `design_reference` is `r`; prefix position has no semantic role. A reference's exact-selector candidates are effective when nonempty; otherwise its alternate-selector candidates are effective. The lane is admitted only for a complete counted envelope and node run, with no alternate or unreferenced aggregate candidate lane, and with equality to the aggregate candidate set when that set is nonempty. Select the unique changed preceding face slot when one exists, otherwise the unique preceding face slot. If no preceding slot exists, a unique candidate in the ASM state's source-qualified active-BREP namespace is the current active face; when the result-face lane has one candidate and no source-qualified match is available, that candidate is also the current active face. A current active face is retained as an active identity, not as a historical slot. Multiple source matches or multiple candidates leave the member unresolved.

The legacy face-group envelope stores no independent current-active-face bit. The current-active result is derived from the preceding-slot and source-qualified candidate rules above; it is an active B-rep identity, not an input-state face slot.

A face-recipe continuation header carries `N+4` in the ordinary envelope. A serialized envelope can omit `N+4` and carry `N+5` instead. When neither expected continuation occurs before the enclosing boundary, the first following valid indexed-record header terminates the envelope; its record index has no fixed delta from `N`.

A face recipe node may carry an optional postlude exactly `[-1, t, -1, 0, 0, -1]` after its two topology side clauses. The signed scalar `t` and every postlude word are retained.

An extended topology field program is complete only when it contains one or more repetitions of a nonempty nonnegative field whose first word is positive, `-1`, the exact zero field `[0, 0]`, and a final `-1`. A prefix that merely ends with `-1` is not a complete field program. The compact form is the single word `[0]`.

Face-recipe side clauses use this same compact or extended field-program grammar before their counted topology entries.

The face-node `-1`-delimited grammar uses delimiter-tagged fields: each field delimiter is independently `i32 -1` or `i32 0`. This applies to the root, both one-word prelude fields, and each topology-side field. Delimiter value does not change the retained field value or side structure.

For a `Loft` bounded-face section, the references sharing the first selector and token field offsets form the target-face clause. The counted recipe header equals both the node count and the target face's boundary-edge count. The Loft transition contains single-face construction bodies copied from its bounded-face inputs; splitting a copied boundary edge may add vertices without changing the source boundary. A unique target-clause face in the exact preceding topology whose ordered boundary vertices occur cyclically, in either sense, on a single-face construction body resolves as the historical section profile. Multi-face result bodies do not participate in this proof.

In an Extrude scope without a sketch-profile operand, profile-role construction groups whose members carry face recipes use the same member envelope. A profile-role group named by another profile-role group's member table is a child selection and is not an additional top-level profile. The member-reference relation is acyclic, every child has one parent, and a child follows its parent in scope-reference order. A root member can name a direct face operand or a child hierarchy with exactly one face-operand leaf. A grouped face operand retains both the scope-reference ordinal of its owning leaf group and its zero-based ordinal within that group's member table; a direct `OffsetFaces` operand has no group ownership. A root whose complete ordered leaf run resolves to historical face slots selects those faces in the scope's preceding input topology. When every root member is a later one-member child group whose bounded-face leaf has a valid paired selector prefix, the direct scope transition also resolves the root as an unordered historical-face set if it deletes exactly one unique preceding face per root member and each deleted face has exactly one surface-carrier binding. This paired-aggregate proof applies even when the member operands retain broader persistent candidate lanes. Increasing stable face-slot order maps that set to root-member order. Otherwise the complete root remains the native profile operand.

When an Extrude profile has one root, that root resolves directly as an active `Faces` profile when every bounded-face leaf has a counted recipe whose positive header equals its parsed node count, has one parsed node offset per node, and has a nonempty candidate-face lane, while its unreferenced, alternate-selector, preceding, changed, historical-support, resolved-slot, and resolved-active-face lanes are all empty. The result is the ordered, deduplicated candidate-face list. This direct proof does not use the profile scope's preceding history; any rejected lane returns the root to the historical proof above.

A sketch-driven `Extrude` scope has exactly one sketch-profile operand in its ordered reference table. Its primary indexed header has record index `N` and is followed by ten zero bytes, `01 + u32 N+3`, six zero bytes, `u32 1`, the LP-UTF16 asset UUID, and an LP-UTF16 decimal Sketch entity suffix. A 94-byte tail ends at the same-index paired header. The compact tail is 93 bytes and stores one nonzero ordinal twice before its references to `N+2`, `N+1`, and the owning scope. The 89-byte compact tail omits the second ordinal copy, so those three references move four bytes earlier. The suffix resolves to a Sketch-typed Design entity in the same stream and therefore to that sketch's placement and relation graph. A `Revolve` or `Loft` profile group can use the same sketch-profile operand frame as its sole member; the member's scope-reference ordinal supplies the same profile-to-group join used by `Extrude`.

A class-`338` Extrude profile member uses the persistent-selection prefix and four consecutive nested records. Its identity record is class `361` and has length 49 bytes from its indexed header to the next indexed header. Relative to that identity header, offsets `11..19` are zero, offset `20` is presence byte `1`, offsets `21..32` are zero, offset `33` is a u32 Sketch entity record index, offset `37` is a zero u32, offset `41` is a u32 curve persistent identity, and offset `45` is a zero u32. The u32 at offset `33` is the owning Sketch entity suffix and the u32 at offset `41` is that Sketch curve's primary persistent identity. A complete role-`0x0000004100000000` group using these members selects its ordered non-point Sketch entities, whether or not they form a closed profile loop.

An Extrude profile without a sketch-profile operand may select faces from the scope's preceding ASM topology. Every counted selection member identifies a historical face directly or through face ownership of a loop, coedge, edge, pcurve, curve, vertex, point, or surface. The group resolves when the intersection of every member's owner-face set contains exactly one face. The neutral profile retains that state-local face identity and the complete native selection group. Multiple groups retain first-group order and remove repeated proven faces after their first occurrence. If any group remains unresolved or resolves in a different history state, the scope identity retains the complete ordered profile construction.

Within an `Extrude` scope, `AlongDistance` is the signed first-side extent. A positive value follows the profile normal and a negative value reverses that normal. The sign remains a profile-normal direction state when the profile is retained as a native construction-operand group; it does not require resolving the Sketch placement to a model-space vector. `AgainstDistance` supplies the opposite-side extent; its magnitude is the second-side length. `TaperAngle` is the first-side draft angle and `Side2TaperAngle` is the opposite-side draft angle. Extent scalars use the parameter record's centimetre storage and draft scalars use its radian storage. Neutral Extrude promotion requires every owned parameter to belong to this extent, start-offset, or draft family; another parameter family retains the native operation.

An `Extrude` scope names zero or more counted selection groups in its ordered reference table. A group's primary indexed header has record index `N` and is followed by ten zero bytes, `01 + u32 scope_record_index`, six zero bytes, `u32 member_count`, and `member_count` entries of `01 + u32 member_record_index + six zero bytes`. The member run is followed by a nonzero u32, a finite f64, a second copy of the u32, `01 + u32 N+2 + six zero bytes`, `01 + u8 variant + u8 0`, `01 + u32 N+1 + seven zero bytes`, and `01 + u32 scope_record_index + six zero bytes`. `variant` is zero or one. The same-index paired header begins at primary-record offset `89 + 11 * member_count`.

The standard `Loft` scope stores its result operation as a u32 at primary-scope offset 29. The compact fixed-prefix form stores it at offset 25. `Sweep` stores the same operation enum at offset 25. The values are `1 = join`, `2 = cut`, `3 = intersect`, and `4 = new body`. New-body does not determine whether a Sweep is solid or surface; the dimensional kind of every bound output body determines the neutral result family. A Boolean Loft has one role-`0x0000000400000000` existing-body group. Its section groups use role `0x0000004100000000`, role `0x0000004300000000`, or both. A NewBody Loft has no existing-body group. An unguided NewBody Loft uses either role `0x0000000500000000` section groups or role `0x0000004100000000` section groups. A heterogeneous NewBody Loft has one role-`0x0000000500000000` endpoint point group, one role-`0x0000004300000000` profile group, and further multi-member role-`0x0000000500000000` profile-boundary groups. A rail-guided Loft has at least two role-`0x0000004100000000` or role-`0x0000004300000000` section groups followed by role-`0x0000000500000000` rail groups. A centerline Loft has at least two such section groups followed by one role-`0x0000000700000000` centerline group. Section and rail order follows scope-reference order. A fixed `Sweep` has one role-`0x0000004100000000` profile group followed by one role-`0x0000000500000000` path group. A guide-rail Sweep has a second role-`0x0000000500000000` group after the path group; it identifies the guide rail. The profile group's sole member can be the sketch-profile frame defined for Extrude. Its compact class-477 form has 93 bytes from the end of the Sketch entity suffix through the byte before the paired header. The u32 scalar index at tail offsets 41 and 53 is equal and nonzero, marked references at 57 and 70 name records `N+2` and `N+1`, and the marked owning-scope reference starts at 82. The 89-byte form omits the second scalar index; the three marked references instead start at offsets 53, 66, and 78. A role-`0x0000004100000000` group whose sole member is a nested entity-selection frame selects one exact planar Sketch entity: the first identity is the owning Sketch entity suffix and the second is the curve's primary persistent identity. The selected entity is an ordered profile and may be open for a surface Sweep. The path group's sole member can use the same frame, with the first identity naming the owning planar Sketch entity suffix and the second naming the primary persistent identity of that Sketch's sole curve. A Boolean Sweep can additionally carry one role-`0x0000000400000000` existing-body group; a new-body Sweep cannot. It owns six fixed scalar records with owner-local ordinals zero through five: `AlongDistance`, `AgainstDistance`, `AlongRailDistance`, `AgainstRailDistance`, `TwistAngle`, and `TaperAngle`. Each scalar primary record is 100, 104, or 105 bytes to its same-index paired header. It stores the scope reference at offset 25, the owner-local ordinal at offset 35, and the finite f64 value at offset 40. AlongDistance and AgainstDistance are fractions in the closed interval from zero through one. When a guide rail is present, AlongRailDistance and AgainstRailDistance are the corresponding directed fractions of that rail in the same interval. An unguided single-path form stores one in both rail-distance lanes. The full-path form stores one in both path-distance lanes; a one-sided partial sweep stores zero in AgainstDistance and its terminal path fraction in AlongDistance.

Every `Extrude`, `Extrusion`, `Fillet`, `Chamfer`, `Draft`, `Loft`, `Sweep`, `OffsetFaces`, `SurfaceOffset`, `DeleteFace`, `SurfaceDeleteFace`, and `Thicken` scope names one or more counted construction-operand groups in its ordered reference table. Counted groups referenced by the localized Fillet names `Congé`, `Abrundung`, and `Arredondamento` and the localized Chamfer name `Chanfrein` use the same group form as `Fillet` and `Chamfer`; these localized scope forms do not require every selection to use a counted group. A group's primary indexed header has record index `N`. Its payload is a zero leading-block byte, the property presence byte and the property block that byte gates, `u32 member_count` and that many references, two optional references, `u32 trailing_count` and that many references, `u32 0`, `u32 role`, ten zero bytes, `u32 ordinal`, `f64 scalar`, and a second copy of `ordinal` that one container generation omits. Every member is also present in the owning scope's ordered reference table. The member run and trailing-reference run may be empty, and the two optional references are both present or both absent. A trailing reference selects a grammar through its target record: persistent-selection groups name identity wrappers, placed-selection groups name the affine transform frame defined below, and SplitFace and SurfaceDeleteFace groups can name a compact flag record. The compact flag record is an indexed header followed by ten zero bytes, marker byte `01`, a boolean byte, and terminator byte `00`. Every member of a Fillet-family or Chamfer-family group has a complete edge-recipe operand frame or fixed persistent selection-identity frame owned by the same scope. Recipe-backed members can also expose their persistent identity. An identity proves one native selection operand but does not by itself prove one neutral topology edge. Within an `Extrude` or `Extrusion` scope, u64 role `0x0000000800000000` identifies existing-body operands, `0x0000004100000000` identifies the sketch-profile operand, and `0x0000001100000000` identifies face operands. Role `0x0000000500000000` identifies a target-shape group for a one-sided to-entity extent; its members are whole-body recipe operands. Within an `OffsetFaces`, `DeleteFace`, or `SurfaceDeleteFace` scope, role `0x0000001000000000` identifies selected-face operands. Within a `SurfaceOffset` scope, role `0x0000004100000000` identifies bounded-face support operands.
