Within a `Sweep` scope, role `0x0000004100000000` identifies the profile and ordered role `0x0000000500000000` groups identify the path followed by the optional guide rail. Within an `EdgeFlange` scope, each role `0x0000000800000000` group has one edge-recipe member, and the role `0x0000004300000000` group has one corresponding edge-recipe member per role-`0x0000000800000000` group in the same order. A `Hem` scope has one single-member group of each of those roles. A joined two-section `Loft` has one existing-body group with role `0x0000000400000000` and two profile groups with role `0x0000004100000000`. A heterogeneous new-body Loft has one endpoint singleton with role `0x0000000500000000`, one role-`0x0000004300000000` profile group, and further multi-member role-`0x0000000500000000` profile-boundary groups. A rail-guided new-body Loft has two profile groups with role `0x0000004300000000` followed by one or more rail groups with role `0x0000000500000000`. A centerline new-body Loft has two role-`0x0000004300000000` profile groups followed by one role-`0x0000000700000000` centerline group. The profile group's first member is the scope's sketch-profile record; subsequent members are counted profile-selection groups in source order. Face groups retain scope-reference order: a selected-face start contributes the first face group, and a one-sided to-face termination contributes the following face group. Absence of an existing-body group means the operation has no existing-body Boolean operand. The tail after the ordinal copy is a reference to record `N+2`, a flag block, a reference to record `N+1`, a zero byte, a reference to the owning scope record, the same-index paired header, and one further reference. The flag block's last byte is zero and the byte before it is `variant`, which is zero or one; one container generation prefixes the block with a further byte. Neither the repeated ordinal nor that prefix byte is announced by another member, so the tail settles both: exactly one of the four readings reaches a paired header carrying record index `N`. `ordinal` is nonzero and below 256, has one value for every group of one feature scope, and does not decrease with the record index; the second copy equals the first. `scalar` is nonnegative and finite and may be zero. `role` occupies the high word of a u64 whose low word is zero, and it is not bounded to one byte. A localized edge-treatment group is complete only when the trailing-reference run's first record starts the identity chain defined below.

A `ReplaceFace` scope in the class-`301`/`258` form has a 290-byte primary frame and four ordered references. Reference zero is a role-`0x0000000900000000` group with one whole-body recipe member at reference one; its complete input-state face selection supplies the replacement geometry. Reference two is a role-`0x0000001000000000` group with one bounded-face member at reference three; its historical face selection supplies the target faces. The group and member positions must match the ordered reference table.

In a one-sided-to-face `Extrude` or `Extrusion`, role `0x0000001200000000` is the legacy spelling of the face-termination group. In the exact class-`296`/`261` 536-byte two-sided-to-faces envelope, the same role identifies each face-termination group. The same role in a `Thicken` scope retains the bounded-face meaning defined above.

An `Extrude` or `Extrusion` existing-body group uses role `0x0000000400000000` or `0x0000000800000000`. Each member is a whole-body recipe operand.

A construction-operand affine transform is a 152-byte indexed record. Its header is followed by eleven zero bytes, sixteen row-major f64 values, and bytes `01 00`. The matrix is finite and rigid, and its last row is `[0, 0, 0, 1]`. The following indexed record has record index `N+1`.

The dual construction-operand transform is 278 bytes. Its indexed header is followed by ten zero bytes, two ordered finite rigid row-major 4×4 matrices, and one zero byte. The matrices begin at record offsets 21 and 149. Another indexed record or the end of the Design stream follows at offset 278; its record index is independent of the transform record index.

A construction-operand auxiliary selection path is an indexed record with index `N`. Its header is followed by ten zero bytes, byte `01`, and a u64 persistent entity identity. The expanded form then stores three zero bytes, a finite rigid row-major 4×4 f64 transform, one zero byte, marked references to the owning feature scope and record `N+2`, and six zero bytes. The compact form instead stores bytes `00 00 variant 00`, where `variant` is zero or one, followed by the same two marked references and six zero bytes. The indexed record immediately after either form has index `N+1`.

A construction-operand entity-tracking path starts with an indexed wrapper record `N`. Ten zero bytes follow its header. Byte `01`, the u64 value `N+1`, and three zero bytes select an adjacent carrier record `N+1`. The carrier header is followed by ten zero bytes, u32 values `1, 0, 1, 2`, a u64 primary identity, a zero u64, u32 one, a signed i32 selector, a u32 kind, and a zero u64. Two optional u64 related identities follow in order; each uses u32 zero for absence or u32 one and the u64 value for presence. The indexed record after both optionals has index `N+2`. An affine-transform record or one or more 24-byte identity wrappers may precede the tracking path. A fixed persistent identity may follow it.

A `DeleteFace` scope has at least two ordered references. Its compact primary frame places the first `DeleteFace` UTF-16LE code unit at offset `139 + 11R` and its same-index paired header at offset `236 + 11R`, where `R` is the reference count. The extended frame places them at offsets `143 + 11R` and `241 + 11R`. Reference zero is its sole role-`0x0000001000000000` construction-operand group; the remaining references are exactly that group's ordered, nonempty bounded-face members. The operation removes those selected faces and extends adjacent faces to heal the body.

Legacy `DeleteFace` scopes use one of two exact class pairs: primary class `264` with paired class `262`, or primary class `383` with paired class `263`. Both use a primary frame whose first `DeleteFace` UTF-16LE code unit is at offset `135 + 11R` and whose same-index paired header is at offset `232 + 11R`. Their ordered reference table, role-`0x0000001000000000` construction-operand group, bounded-face members, and healed-face operation are the same as the standard `DeleteFace` form. A different class pair does not admit this legacy layout.

Unhealed surface-face removal is a distinct feature family and serializes as `SurfaceDeleteFace`. Its primary frame places the first `SurfaceDeleteFace` UTF-16LE code unit at offset `140 + 11R` and its same-index paired header at offset `250 + 11R`. A second frame shifts the first kind code unit to `139 + 11R` and the paired header to `251 + 11R`. Its reference table, its sole role-`0x0000001000000000` construction-operand group, and that group's ordered bounded-face members follow the `DeleteFace` rules above. The operation removes the selected faces and leaves the adjacent faces unchanged, so the result is an open sheet rather than a healed solid.

The class-keyed `SurfaceDeleteFace` forms use these exact tuples `(primary class, paired class, base frame length, base kind offset)`, where the base values exclude the `11R` reference table: `(287, 270, 245, 135)`, `(287, 270, 256, 146)`, `(327, 257, 250, 139)`, `(414, 263, 250, 140)`, `(497, 259, 257, 146)`, `(545, 257, 246, 135)`, `(545, 257, 250, 139)`, and `(545, 257, 257, 146)`. A class-keyed tuple is admitted only with its listed paired class. Its group and member rules are the same as the common forms.

A distance-form single-edge `EdgeFlange` scope has `8 + W` ordered references: the selection wrapper, the role-`0x08` group and its edge-recipe operand, the height and angle parameter owners, the role-`0x43` aggregate group and its aggregate edge-recipe operand, the settings record, and `W` width-distance parameter owners. The ordered reference table is in record-index order, so no role has a fixed table position. Each role is named by a marked slot in the fixed operation section instead, the recipe-backed operand of a group is the record three after that group, and the `W` entries no role claims are the width-distance owners. `W` carries the edge-width mode: zero spans the complete edge, one centres the flange on it, and two measures the flange from each end. `W` is at most two.

A single-edge to-object `EdgeFlange` scope has eleven ordered references: the eight distance-form roles with no width-distance owners, a role-`0x21` target group and its entity-selection operand, and one `ToObjectOffset` parameter owner. The target group has one member, and the member is the record three after the target-group record. The target entity-selection operand's primary identity names the indexed record immediately before the selected `WorkPlane` or `WorkPoint` scope. The target group and its member are named by the ordered table; the two further marked references in the fixed section are not ordered-table members.

A four-byte scope-header member is present in some `EdgeFlange` and `Hem` frames and absent in others. The member is not announced, so it shifts the fixed operation section by a header shift `S` of zero or four. `S` is settled by reference agreement: exactly one candidate makes every marked slot name a record the ordered reference table lists, and a frame that reads under both candidates is refused.

The `EdgeFlange` fixed operation section starts at offset `85 + S` with a u32 bend-position discriminator, the u32 edge count `1`, the marked wrapper reference, the marked settings reference, a u32 height-datum discriminator, and the marked angle-owner and height-owner references. Bend-position value `1` puts the bend outside the selected edge, `2` inside it, `3` at it, and `4` tangent to the side reference plane. Height-datum value `1` measures the height from the inner faces of the sheet and `2` from the outer faces. A u32 whose role is not settled follows the height-owner reference, and the positive f64 inside bend radius in centimetres starts 15 bytes after that u32. Fourteen bytes after the radius starts a u32 result-record count `R`. The marked aggregate-group reference starts 22 bytes after the radius plus `15R`, and the marked role-`0x08` group reference follows it 27 bytes later. The primary frame length is `493 + 15R + 11W + S`.
A class-`325` or class-`334` single-edge distance-form `EdgeFlange` scope uses a separate fixed layout. The class-`325` form has paired class `258`; the class-`334` form has paired class `257`. Both forms have eight ordered references, no width-distance owners, and a 494-byte primary frame. Relative to the primary scope header, the fixed fields are bend position at offset `76`, edge count `1` at `80`, the marked wrapper reference at `84`, the marked settings reference at `95`, height datum at `106`, the marked angle owner at `110`, the marked height owner at `121`, reference-side discriminator at `132`, and the positive finite f64 inside bend radius in centimetres at `138`. The result count at `146` is `2`; the two 15-byte result records start at `150` and `165`, carry marked references, and have u32 trailers `1` at `161` and `0` at `176`. A u32 value `1` at `180` precedes the marked aggregate-group reference at `184`; the marked role-`0x08` group reference starts at `207`. Each group's recipe-backed operand is the record three after the group. The bend-position and height-datum values have the meanings defined for the standard form.
A class-`325`/`258`, class-`334`/`257`, or class-`364`/`261` full-edge multi-edge `EdgeFlange` scope uses a 591-byte fixed layout with twelve ordered references and no width-distance owners. Relative to the primary scope header, the bend-position discriminator is at offset `92`, the edge count at `96`, the two marked wrapper references start at offsets `100` and `111`, the marked settings reference at `122`, height datum at `133`, the marked angle owner at `137`, the marked height owner at `148`, reference-side discriminator at `159`, and the positive finite f64 inside bend radius in centimetres at `165`. The edge count is `2`. The result count at `173` is `3`; the three 15-byte result records start at `177`, `192`, and `207`, and have u32 trailers `1` at `188`, `1` at `203`, and `0` at `218`. A u32 value `1` at `222` precedes the marked aggregate-group reference at `226`. The two marked role-`0x08` group references start at `249` and `260`, and each group's recipe-backed operand is the record three after the group. After the fixed slots claim the wrappers, settings, owners, aggregate group, and edge groups, the remaining ordered references are the two edge operands followed by the two aggregate operands in source order. The bend-position and height-datum values have the meanings defined for the standard form.
A class-`364`/`261` per-edge-width `EdgeFlange` scope uses a 643-byte primary frame with fourteen ordered references. Its fixed operation starts at primary-relative offset `92`. The bend-position discriminator is at `92`, the edge count `2` at `96`, the marked wrapper references at `100` and `111`, the marked settings reference at `122`, height datum at `133`, the marked angle owner at `137`, the marked height owner at `148`, reference-side discriminator at `159`, and the positive finite f64 inside bend radius in centimetres at `165`. The result count at `173` is `5`; the five 15-byte result records start at `177`, `192`, `207`, `222`, and `237`, and have u32 trailers `1` at `188`, `203`, `218`, `233`, and `0` at `248`. A u32 value `1` at `252` precedes the marked aggregate-group reference at `256`. The two marked role-`0x08` group references start at `279` and `290`, and each group's recipe-backed operand is the record three after the group. After the fixed slots claim the wrappers, settings, owners, aggregate group, and edge groups, the remaining ordered references are the two edge operands, two aggregate operands, and two `EdgeWidth` owners in source order. The bend-position and height-datum values have the meanings defined for the standard form.
A class-`286`/`258` single-edge distance-form `EdgeFlange` scope uses a 483-byte primary frame with eight ordered references and no width-distance owners. Relative to the primary scope header, the fixed fields are bend position at offset `80`, edge count `1` at `84`, the marked wrapper reference at `88`, the marked settings reference at `99`, height datum at `110`, the marked angle owner at `114`, the marked height owner at `125`, reference-side discriminator at `136`, and the positive finite f64 inside bend radius in centimetres at `142`. The result count at `150` is `1`; its 15-byte result record starts at `154`, carries a marked reference, and has a u32 trailer `0` at `165`. A u32 value `1` at `169` precedes the marked aggregate-group reference at `173`; the marked role-`0x08` group reference starts at `196`. Each group's recipe-backed operand is the record three after the group. The bend-position and height-datum values have the meanings defined for the standard form.

The extended class-`286`/`258` two-edge distance form uses an 801-byte primary frame with 28 ordered references. Its fixed operation has the class-`325`/`334` two-sided-per-edge offsets: bend position at `96`, edge count `2` at `100`, wrapper references at `104` and `115`, settings at `126`, height datum at `137`, angle owner at `141`, height owner at `152`, reference side at `163`, and positive finite f64 inside bend radius in centimetres at `169`. Result count `5` is at `177`; five 15-byte result records start at `181`, `196`, `211`, `226`, and `241`, with trailers `1` at `192`, `207`, `222`, `237`, and `0` at `252`. A u32 value `1` at `256` precedes the aggregate-group reference at `260`; the two role-`0x08` group references start at `283` and `294`. Each edge-group operand is the record three after its group, and the aggregate operands are the two members of the role-`0x43` group. After those fixed roles and group operands are claimed, four signed `EdgeOffset_1`/`EdgeOffset_2` owners occur in source order, followed by twelve auxiliary references. The neutral two-sided-per-edge widths use the magnitudes of those signed offsets. The auxiliary references remain ordered operation members.
The to-object fixed operation section uses the same common fields through the result-record count and has one result record. Five zero bytes follow the count, then the marked target-group reference starts at `94 + S`. The u32 at `105 + S` is `2`. The marked first inserted reference starts at `109 + S`; the u32 at `120 + S` is `1`; and the marked second inserted reference starts at `124 + S`. Four zero bytes follow the second inserted reference, the u32 at `139 + S` is `1`, and the marked aggregate-group reference starts at `143 + S`. Twelve zero bytes follow the aggregate-group reference, the u32 at `166 + S` is `1`, and the marked role-`0x08` group reference starts at `170 + S`. The primary frame length is `576 + S`. The two inserted marked references remain native operation references.

A multi-edge frame outside the settled class-`286`/`258`, class-`325`/`258`, class-`334`/`257`, or class-`364`/`261` forms is refused. Its further group slots and the order of its edge operands against the aggregate operands are not established.

The class-`325`/`258` and class-`334`/`257` two-sided-per-edge forms carry four `EdgeWidth_1`/`EdgeWidth_2` owners in source order. The first pair belongs to the first role-`0x08` edge group and the second pair belongs to the second role-`0x08` edge group. `EdgeWidth_1` and `EdgeWidth_2` are the first-end and second-end distances for that selected edge; they are independent between edge groups and are not one operation-wide pair. The neutral `TwoSidesPerEdge` width law preserves one pair per selected edge in edge-group order. It does not infer a cross-edge orientation from the fixed operation.

An `EdgeFlange` role-`0x0000000800000000` group can use its transition as an edge proof when every member has no recipe-reference entries, selector contexts, or local topology references. A member then contributes its updated predecessor boundary edge when exactly one updated edge is present and that edge occurs in its preceding, changed, and result boundary sets. For a group with multiple members, the contributed singleton sets must have one unique assignment of distinct stable edge slots. An extra updated edge, a missing endpoint occurrence, or a duplicate assignment leaves the group native.

Their 669-byte primary frame has sixteen ordered references. The fixed operation starts at primary-relative offset `96`: bend position at `96`, edge count `2` at `100`, wrapper references at `104` and `115`, settings at `126`, height datum at `137`, angle owner at `141`, height owner at `152`, reference side at `163`, and positive finite f64 inside bend radius in centimetres at `169`. Result count `5` is at `177`; five 15-byte result records start at `181`, `196`, `211`, `226`, and `241`, with trailers `1` at `192`, `207`, `222`, `237`, and `0` at `252`. A u32 value `1` at `256` precedes the aggregate-group reference at `260`; the two role-`0x08` group references are at `283` and `294`. Each group's recipe-backed operand is the record three after the group. After those fixed roles and group operands are identified, the aggregate operands are the aggregate-group members and the four remaining parameter owners are the width owners.

Parameter records owned by an `EdgeFlange` scope name their role in their source kind. The height owner names `FlangeHeight` and the angle owner names `FlangeAngle`. In the ordinary distance form, a symmetric width owner names `EdgeWidth`, and the two-sided owners name `EdgeWidth_1` and `EdgeWidth_2` in reference-table order. The class-`364`/`261` per-edge-width form has two `EdgeWidth` owners after the edge and aggregate operands in reference-table order; its classed fixed layout identifies that owner lane separately from the ordinary width-owner count. The extended class-`286`/`258` form uses signed `EdgeOffset_1` and `EdgeOffset_2` owners for its two-sided-per-edge distances.

A `Hem` scope names one parameter owner per input its form takes, the edge wrapper, a role-`0x08` group and its edge operand, a role-`0x43` aggregate group and its edge operand, and the settings record. The ordered reference table is in record-index order, so no role holds a fixed position in it. The header shift `S` defined for `EdgeFlange` applies here too.

The form is carried by the parameter set and the executed transition, not by a fixed-section discriminator. Each owner's parameter names its role in its source kind: a flat hem owns `HemGap` and `HemLength`, an open hem owns the same two, a rolled hem owns `HemRadius` and `HemAngle`, and a teardrop hem owns `HemGap`, `HemLength`, and `HemRadius`. A flat hem stores a `HemGap` its form does not use.

The gap-and-length frame is `494 + S` bytes. It stores the u32 edge count `1` at offset `89 + S`, the marked edge-wrapper reference at `93 + S`, the marked settings reference at `104 + S`, the marked gap-owner and length-owner references at `127 + S` and `138 + S`, and a positive f64 rule-derived inside bend radius in centimetres at `156 + S`. The marked aggregate-group reference is at `193 + S` and the marked role-`0x08` group reference at `220 + S`; each group's operand is the record three after the group.

A rolled frame has the same `494 + S` length and group-reference offsets. Its owner references start at offsets `126 + S` and `139 + S`, and its rule-derived inside bend radius remains at `156 + S`; the first owner is the angle input and the second is the radius input. A teardrop frame is `515 + S` bytes. Its marked gap-owner, length-owner, and radius-owner references start at offsets `127 + S`, `138 + S`, and `149 + S`, its rule-derived inside bend radius starts at `166 + S`, and its marked aggregate-group and role-`0x08` group references start at `203 + S` and `230 + S`. A valid frame has one of these three owner layouts, with matching marked slots, group operands, and paired-header length.

An executed gap-and-length `Hem` inserts exactly two coaxial cylinder carriers parallel to the selected source edge. Let their radii be `r0 < r1`, and let `t = r1 - r0`. The form is `Open` when `2r0 = t`, and `Flat` when `2r0 < t`. A transition that does not satisfy either invariant retains the shared gap-and-length form. The preceding state supplies the source edge. Its persistent incident planar face is the source face when exactly one such face has all inserted cylinder-axis origins at finite, nonzero signed offsets from its plane and those offsets have one sign; positive is `Forward` and negative is `Reverse`. Ambiguous or zero offsets leave the direction unresolved.

Four further fields are retained without a meaning: u32 at `85 + S`, u32 at `115 + S`, one byte at `119 + S`, and u32 at `121 + S`. Each holds one value across every readable hem form and direction state, so none of them selects the form, the direction, or the bend position.

Within a scope of the Fillet family, construction-operand groups in scope-reference order pair one-to-one with `Radius` parameters in increasing owner-local order. `TangencyWeight` parameters, when present, pair with the same groups in increasing owner-local order. The group, owner, and parameter records store no radius or tangency-weight assignment cross-reference. The two orderings define both pairings. Each radius therefore applies to the ordered edge operands carried by its paired group.

A variable-radius `Fillet` has one construction-operand group, one `StartRadius`, one `EndRadius`, zero or more paired `MidRadius` and dimensionless `MidParams` inputs, and zero or one dimensionless `TangencyWeight`. When present, `TangencyWeight` is retained as the explicit tangency weight; its omission means that no explicit weight is stored. Start and end radii occupy normalized edge-chain parameters zero and one. Each `MidParams` value is the normalized parameter of the corresponding `MidRadius`; repeated midpoint inputs pair in owner-local order. Radii are nonnegative, at least one radius is positive, and midpoint parameters are strictly increasing inside `(0, 1)`.

A chordal `Fillet` has one construction-operand group, one `ChordLen`, and one dimensionless `TangencyWeight`. The chord length applies to every edge operand in the group.

A fixed `Fillet` has no indexed parameter owners and exactly one construction-operand group. Its fixed scalar lanes follow owner-local ordinal order. A single lane is a nonnegative constant radius with no explicit tangency weight. A multi-lane sequence starts with a positive dimensionless tangency weight. It then carries either one nonnegative constant radius or two endpoint radii followed by zero or more alternating intermediate-radius and intermediate-parameter pairs. Intermediate parameters are strictly increasing and lie in `(0, 1)`. The first and second radii apply at parameters zero and one; each following radius applies at its paired intermediate parameter. Radii use centimetre storage, and at least one radius is positive.

A full-round `Fillet` has no indexed parameter owner or radius law. It has exactly one role-`0x0000000400000000` construction-operand group with one member. The member is the compact persistent-identity frame also retained in the edge-identity arena, but its group-linked bounded-face operand classifies it as a face selection. Its resolved preceding-state face is the full-round center face. The group has one trailing compact flag record, its boolean is `true`, and its `variant` byte is zero; this flag selects automatic inference of both side-face sets. A neutral full-round group therefore retains the center face and represents both side sets as automatic selections.

Within a scope of the Chamfer family, construction-operand groups in scope-reference order pair one-to-one with dimensional specifications in increasing owner-local order. The group, owner, and parameter records store no cross-reference for this assignment; the two orderings define the pairing. An equal-distance chamfer has one `Distance` or `leftDistance` parameter per group. A two-distance chamfer has either one `Distance 1` and one `Distance 2`, or one `leftDistance` and one `rightDistance`, per group. A distance-angle chamfer has one `Distance` parameter and one `Angle` or `Rotate Angle` parameter per group. Each independently dimensioned specification applies to the ordered edge operands carried by its paired group.

A fixed equal-distance `Chamfer` has no indexed parameter owner. It owns exactly one fixed scalar record with owner-local ordinal zero. That scalar is the positive `Distance` in centimetres and applies to the scope's single construction-operand group.

A blind `Hole` has exactly one positive `HoleDepth`, one positive `HoleDiameter`, and one `TipAngle` parameter. Length values use centimetre storage, and the angle uses radian storage. A `TipAngle` strictly between zero and pi defines a conical drill point. A `TipAngle` equal to pi defines a flat bottom. A counterbored hole additionally has one positive `CBDepth` and one positive `CBDiameter`. `CBDepth` does not exceed `HoleDepth`, and `CBDiameter` is greater than `HoleDiameter`. Other parameter families or multiplicities do not define these hole forms.

A `Hole` scope's point-and-direction carrier is the indexed record whose type-table entry is `F2A7590D-6654-4674-B393-A2AEF4FEC48A` at version 4. Its base level is:

```
u32      boundingboxIndex
3 × f64  position
3 × f64  direction
2 × f64  pointParameters
u32      refType
u8       tangentPointDataPrefix
3 × f64  tangentPointData
u32 count, count × ref inputs
```

The input count frames the final reference run. It is positive, every input reference is marked, every f64 is finite, and `direction` is a unit vector. `position` and `tangentPointData` use centimetres. The two point parameters, `refType`, and the prefix byte are retained as encoded. `Hole` construction-operand groups use role `0x0000000400000000` or `0x0000000500000000` and carry no Extrude role discriminator. They are native metadata; a Hole's typed body and face operands are direct scope references. The body operand uses a whole-body recipe, and each face operand uses a bounded-face recipe. When more than one face operand is present, their resolved stable input-face slots must be identical; otherwise the face selection is unresolved.

The first identity-wrapper record in a construction-operand group's trailing-reference run starts a nested identity chain. Every wrapper is an indexed header followed by ten zero bytes and `01 01 00`; the next indexed header begins exactly 24 bytes after the wrapper header. Wrappers repeat while physically adjacent records carry the same wrapper grammar, and chains may share wrapper suffixes. Physical adjacency to the first non-wrapper record does not by itself assign that record to the operand. When the following record is a fixed persistent identity, it is exactly 190 bytes from its indexed header to the next indexed header. Its header is followed by ten zero bytes, a u64 local identity, an LP-UTF16 asset UUID, an LP-UTF16 context UUID, `u32 2`, and an optional-slot marker followed by u32 zero. The marker is zero when the slot is absent and one when it is present. For an `Extrude`, the asset UUID equals the owning scope's sketch-profile asset UUID. A `Fillet` or `Chamfer` replaces every selected input edge. When one group contains `n` compact persistent members and its exact operation transition deletes exactly `n` edges, the group selects the complete unordered deleted-edge set; neutral transfer does not require a per-member assignment. The value of `n` is the compact-member count of that group. The cardinality proof is evaluated independently for each group and does not apply to another feature family.

Each selection member is a 190-byte indexed record ending immediately before the next indexed header. Its eleven-byte header is followed by ten zero bytes, a local persistent identity, the LP-UTF16 asset UUID, an LP-UTF16 context UUID, `u32 2`, and the same optional-slot marker plus u32 zero. Every member's asset UUID equals the scope's sketch-profile asset UUID, and all members in one group carry the same context UUID. Selection-group member order is the encoded run order. A construction-operand identity chain belongs to the member when its following indexed header is the member header and its complete local, asset, and context identity equals the member identity; multiple construction groups may share one member through separate chains. A Fillet or Chamfer persistent-edge identity has an eleven-byte indexed header followed by twelve zero bytes, or eleven or ten zero bytes in the compact forms, then a marker byte `1`, a u32 local identity, six zero bytes, u32 `1`, and the two LP-UTF16 UUIDs. The local identity names either the equal stable ASM entity slot or a record revision assigned to one stable slot by the complete history archive. In a bulletin update pair `(old, new)`, `old` is the archived record revision and `new` is its stable entity slot. In a deletion pair `(old, null)`, the archived revision and stable slot are both `old`. Each archived revision has one such mapping. A value valid in both namespaces resolves only when both name the same stable slot. State identities occurring in more than one retained ASM history do not contribute to identity or position resolution. The normalized stable slot, its invariant topology or carrier family across uniquely identified states, and the ordered containing state identities form the historical selection identity. A class-297 identity whose local identity equals `N+3` and whose record contains the bounded-face envelope above is a bounded-face edge rule. Its exact preceding support face supplies ordered boundary edges; intersection with the owning feature transition's deleted treatment edges is the rule's selected edge run. Every support owner must be changed by that transition, and repeated edges contributed by later group members are removed after their first occurrence. Historical topology retains each vertex-to-point binding and each point carrier's model-space position. A loop supplies its coedges, a pcurve supplies every coedge using that carrier, a coedge supplies its edge, an edge supplies its two vertices, a curve supplies every edge using that carrier, a vertex supplies itself, and a point supplies its stored position. When the local identity uniquely equals a persistent point id or a primary or nonzero secondary persistent curve id owned by the selected Sketch, it selects that point or curve.

A fixed persistent identity can terminate the Design stream after its 190-byte frame. Both UUID strings use 36 UTF-16 code units. It then has no following indexed record; its native following-record index is zero and its following byte offset is the stream-end boundary. A selection member has the same terminal form.

An Extrude selection resolves to neutral sketch-loop indices when every ordered member resolves to a curve and each curve occurs in exactly one neutral profile loop. A role-`0x0000004100000000` construction group can contain nested entity-selection members directly. All members have the same primary identity, asset UUID, and context UUID. The primary identity names one Sketch placement, and each member's secondary identity names one unique curve owned by that Sketch. The ordered, deduplicated profile-loop indices containing those curves are the selection. A curve in no loop or more than one loop leaves the group unresolved. Repeated members of one loop produce one loop index in first-member order. A Sketch containing exactly one profile loop has one possible selection and resolves to that loop independently of the member identity family. Otherwise a member in the historical body, region, shell, surface, face, loop, coedge, edge, vertex, point, curve, or pcurve family supplies its historical boundary-vertex positions. Body, region, and shell members traverse the complete body-region-shell-face ownership hierarchy. A historical state contributes positions only when the complete ownership and entity-to-edge-to-vertex-to-point chains required by every member resolve in that state. Positions from different history states are never combined. Each complete state-local member set is orthogonally projected into the selected Sketch frame, and every resolving state must select the same neutral result. When no history state contributes a complete member set, each persistent Sketch point supplies its projected position and each persistent Sketch curve supplies every neutral profile containing that exact curve. The collective positions select one atomic supported planar region when they lie strictly inside the same smallest containing loop or uniquely span that region's exterior and immediate-child boundary loops. If collective positions do not select one region, exact member selections form their ordered deduplicated union. One unique selected region also governs when every differing member resolves to one of that region's exterior or hole boundary loops. Each region records its exterior-loop index and immediate child-loop indices. Model-space displacement normal to the Sketch plane does not affect the result. When member identities do not resolve, the Extrude scope's current and previous history-state identities select their unique linked transition. Each inserted face with a complete face-loop-coedge-edge-vertex-point boundary is projected into the Sketch frame. A face whose points select an atomic region is a cap candidate; a side face selects no region. The transition resolves when at least one cap candidate exists and every cap candidate selects the same region. When that inserted-face proof does not resolve, deleted faces in the unique preceding state supply a second transition proof. Every deleted face must have exactly one surface-carrier binding, exactly one carrier must own more than one deleted face, every other deleted carrier family must be a singleton, and deleted face identities must be unique. Every face in the multi-face family must have a complete historical boundary whose projected points select a profile. The family resolves to the ordered deduplicated union when all faces select loops or all faces select regions. Multiple groups resolve as their group-ordered, deduplicated union when every group resolves to loops or every group resolves to regions. An incomplete member run, conflicting cap candidates, absence of a cap candidate, a boundary point that does not uniquely identify a region closure, an unsupported profile geometry family, intersecting candidate loops, an unresolved group, or a mixture of loop and region selections retains every group identity in scope-reference order as a native selection within the resolved Sketch. A scope without a selection group consumes the whole resolved Sketch.

An Extrude selection against a spatial Sketch resolves each complete selection group from the member local persistent identities. Every member must uniquely name a curve owned by the selected spatial Sketch, each named curve must occur in exactly one spatial profile, and all members of one group must name the same profile. Members that uniquely name different profiles contradict the group and do not admit a topology fallback. A direct role-`0x0000004100000000` entity-selection group instead uses the common primary Sketch identity and each member's secondary curve identity; it contributes the ordered, deduplicated union of the uniquely containing spatial profiles. Multiple groups contribute profile indices in scope-reference order and remove repeated indices after their first occurrence. A spatial Sketch containing exactly one profile resolves to index zero when exact member identities are unavailable. Otherwise the scope's current and previous history-state identities select their unique linked transition. For each inserted face with a complete face-loop-coedge-edge-vertex-point boundary, the boundary points are tested against every spatial profile. A profile is a candidate when all point offsets along its normal are equal, its complete boundary consists of spatial lines, and every point projected into its profile frame lies in the polygon closure. A profile with missing or nonlinear boundary geometry prevents that face from contributing a candidate when all point offsets along the profile normal are equal. At least one inserted face must contribute a candidate, and all contributing faces must name the same profile. If the inserted faces do not resolve, the deleted faces in the unique preceding state use the same rule. An incomplete member run, an ambiguous curve-to-profile relation, conflicting member identities, conflicting face candidates, or unsupported candidate geometry retains every group identity in scope-reference order as a native selection within the resolved spatial Sketch. A scope without a selection group consumes the whole resolved spatial Sketch.

Inserted Extrude side faces that all project to the same nonempty sketch-loop selection prove that selection; inserted regions are Boolean result context. When side faces project to differing loops, one inserted cap selecting a loop-defined region remains the proof only when every loop selection names exterior or hole loops of that region. A loop outside the cap region or multiple distinct cap regions contradicts the proof.

Each parameter-owning Sketch scope references exactly one paired placement record. The compact placement frame is 201 bytes from its primary indexed header to its same-index paired header and denotes the identity local-to-model transform. The explicit placement frame is 329 bytes and inserts a row-major 4×4 f64 local-to-model matrix at primary-record offset 55. The 305- and 325-byte placement frames store the same matrix at primary-record offset 48. Its bottom row is `(0, 0, 0, 1)` and its three basis columns are orthonormal. Matrix column 0 is the sketch u-axis, column 1 is the sketch v-axis, column 2 is the sketch normal, and column 3 is the model-space origin.

A `Sketch` scope joined to `EntityGenesis`-form entity headers references its placement in a wider frame. The placement class holds one class level whose members are, in order:

```
u8       LinearOffsetOnPeriodicFace      v >= 14
3 × f64  alignUpDirection                v >= 4
f64      arcLengthForPeriodicFace        v >= 11
u8       axisFlipped                     v >= 8
u8       bUseUDirection                  v >= 10
ref      definerCreator                  v >= 6
ref      extraOffset                     v >= 13
ref      globalParamU                    v >= 9
ref      globalParamV                    v >= 9
ref      globalReorientParam             v >= 9
ref      globalReorientParam1            v >= 9
u8       isInferredXAxis
u8       isInferredZAxis
u8       isZFlip
u8       identity, 16 × f64 matrix3d     matrix stored only when identity is 0
f64      oldRotation                     v >= 7
f64      pickPointParam                  v == 2
2 × f64  pickpoint                       v >= 1
u32 count, count × ref  rEdgeReferences  v >= 5
ref      rOrigin
u32 count, count × ref  rPlaneReferences
ref      rXAxis
ref      rZAxis
u32      refType
u8       resizeType                      v >= 12
u32 count, count × ref  values
```

The base level's two counted reference lists close the record. `matrix3d` is the row-major 4×4 local-to-model transform with the translation in column 3 and the bottom row `(0, 0, 0, 1)`; the origin is in centimetres. The leading identity byte is the member's presence marker read the other way round: `1` states that the transform is the identity and stores no matrix, so the two forms of one class version differ by exactly the matrix's 128 bytes. `alignUpDirection` is a unit vector or zero. `resizeType` is one byte wide; reading it as a u32 desynchronizes the `values` list that follows it. WorkPlane records of the same class are not sketch placements.

A `WorkPlane` scope references one placement frame of the same indexed record class. The class-290/262 placement frame is 325 bytes: it stores 34 zero bytes after the indexed header, bytes `0x01, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00` at offsets 45 through 48, and a row-major 4×4 f64 local-to-model matrix at offset 49. The class-308/257, class-320/258, class-364/263, class-380/262, and class-431/257 placement frames use a second 325-byte layout: they store 38 zero bytes after the indexed header and the same matrix at offset 49. One 321-byte compact form stores the same zero prefix and matrix offset. Class 364 is also paired with class 264 and stores the matrix at offset 49 in two 321-byte forms: 35 zero bytes followed by `0x01, 0x00, 0x00`, or 34 zero bytes followed by `0xcc, 0xcd, 0x00, 0x00`. The 326-byte compact form stores 39 zero bytes after the indexed header and the matrix at offset 50. Its primary and paired class pair is 279 and 266, 409 and 258, or 450 and 259. The 337-byte class-350/258 and class-409/258 placement frames use a third 337-byte layout: they store 39 zero bytes after the indexed header and a row-major 4×4 f64 local-to-model matrix at offset 50. The 352-, 363-, and 374-byte direct forms store `0x01`, ten zero bytes, and the matrix at offset 66. The 362- and 373-byte referenced forms store `0x01, 0x00, 0x01` at offsets 55 through 57, a u32 construction-record reference at offset 58, fourteen zero bytes, and the matrix at offset 76. The matrix basis, origin, rigidity, and centimetre units equal a sketch placement matrix. It defines the constructed plane directly: column 0 is the plane u-axis, column 2 is its normal, and column 3 is its origin.
A legacy three-reference `WorkPlane` scope is a 279-byte class-359 frame paired with class 262. Its first member is a 345-byte class-400 placement frame paired with class 262; the placement frame stores 38 zero bytes after its indexed header and the row-major 4×4 f64 local-to-model matrix at offset 49. The scope's two remaining members are vertex-recipe records, and this form has no five-member `ExtraOffset` construction table. The solved placement matrix is the complete plane carrier for this form.
The class-350/262 three-reference `WorkPlane` scope is 273 bytes, or 269 bytes in its omitted-lane variant. In the 273-byte form, its reference count is at offset 133, the three marked member references are at offsets 138, 149, and 160, the current history-state identity is at offset 170, the UTF-16LE kind starts at offset 178, the feature ordinal is at offset 196, and the preceding history-state identity is at offset 227. The 269-byte form shifts these fields to 129, 134, 145, 156, 166, 174, 192, and 223. The paired header is class 262. The ordered members are not positional; one member is a class-256/262 placement frame. The class-256/262 WorkPlane placement frame is 325 bytes. That 325-byte frame has zero bytes 11 through 44; bytes 45 through 46 are an opaque little-endian u16; bytes 47 through 48 are zero; the class-256/262 placement frame stores its row-major 4×4 f64 local-to-model matrix at offset 49. The two remaining members are construction metadata, and the solved placement matrix is the complete plane carrier for this form.

The class-337/266 WorkPlane placement frame is 325 bytes and uses the same offsets: bytes 11 through 44 are zero, bytes 45 through 46 are an opaque little-endian u16, bytes 47 through 48 are zero, and the row-major 4×4 f64 local-to-model matrix starts at offset 49. The class-341/261 and class-346/262 placement frames use a 321-byte form with the same prefix and matrix offsets. These class-pair rules are separate from the zero-prefix 321-byte form.

The class-322/261 WorkPlane placement frame is 332 bytes. Bytes 11 through 48 are zero. The frame stores a row-major 4×4 f64 local-to-model matrix at offset 49; the remaining 155 bytes are the opaque placement-carrier tail.

A three-point `WorkPlane` scope has five ordered references: the solved placement frame, three `vertex_recipe_data` operands in construction-point order, and one `ExtraOffset` parameter owner. The placement frame's construction reference names the `ExtraOffset` owner. Its finite evaluated value is zero. Each vertex operand uses the five-record terminal envelope defined for a `WorkPoint` vertex input. Its persistent face references select one common vertex in the preceding history state. The three vertices are distinct and non-collinear. Their positions lie on the solved placement plane, and their cross-product normal is parallel or antiparallel to the placement normal.

A two-point `WorkAxis` scope has five ordered references. Reference zero is a 232-byte solved-axis carrier. After its indexed header it stores ten zero bytes, u32 value count 8, and eight f64 values at offset 25: a three-coordinate origin in centimetres, a three-component displacement to the second point in centimetres, and two zeros. A u32 reference count of two at offset 118 is followed by marked references to scope references two and four. Those records are 197-byte point carriers; each stores 31 zero bytes after its indexed header and a three-coordinate point at offset 42. The first point equals the solved-axis origin and the second equals the origin plus the displacement. The displacement is nonzero and defines the axis direction.

Two two-reference `WorkAxis` scope forms use direct axis carriers. Their scope frames are:

| Primary class | Paired class | Frame length | Reference-count offset | Member-reference offsets | Current-state offset | Kind offset | Feature-ordinal offset |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 302 | 262 | 268 | 131 | 136, 147 | 157 | 165 | 181 |
| 361 | 258 | 254 | 127 | 132, 143 | 153 | 161 | 177 |

The first ordered member is the direct axis carrier and the second is its construction-support record. The class-297/262 carrier is 215 bytes; the class-335/258 carrier is 195 bytes. Both carriers store ten zero bytes at offsets 11 through 20, u32 value count 8 at offset 21, and eight f64 values at offset 25. Values 0 through 2 are the origin in centimetres, values 3 through 5 are the displacement in centimetres, and values 6 and 7 are zero. The displacement is finite and nonzero. The carrier stores u32 reference count 6 at offset 89 and u32 value 1 at offset 93. Its remaining construction/reference tail starts at offset 97 and ends at the paired header; the tail layout is generation-specific. The support record is class 306/262 or class 349/258 and is 293 bytes. The direct carrier's origin and displacement define the axis direction.

A `JointOrigin` scope owns one coordinate frame. The 336- and 347-byte forms store 34 zero bytes after the primary indexed header, a marked construction-record reference at offset 45 with ten trailing zero bytes, and a row-major rigid 4×4 local-to-model matrix at offset 60. The 385-byte class-364 form is paired with class 264 and stores 34 zero bytes, `0x01, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00`, and the matrix at offset 49. The 300-, 322-, and 344-byte parameter-scope envelopes store construction graphs; their solved frame is the unique matching operand frame in the consuming `Assemble` scope. An operand frame whose construction reference equals the `JointOrigin` scope record index defines that origin. The 604-byte class-276 `Assemble` form paired with class 258 stores a marked construction reference at offset 24, seven zero bytes, the origin matrix at offset 36, a marked `JointOrigin` scope reference at offset 164, six zero bytes, and u32 value 1. It has exactly eight parameter owners. Four placement owners at local ordinals zero through three precede the angle, local X offset, local Y offset, and local Z offset alignment owners at ordinals four through seven. Matrix columns 0, 1, and 2 are the coordinate-system x, y, and z axes; column 3 is its origin in centimetres.

A direct `WorkPoint` scope references a point-data construction record. The primary record is paired by its record index. Its class level is:

```
i32      boundingboxIndex     v >= 2
2 × f64  param
ref      pickPtParam          v >= 1
3 × f64  point3d
u32      refType
3 × f64  tangentPointData     v >= 3
```

A vertex-rule input can instead use the direct sketch-point selection envelope. It keeps the same persistent-selection prefix and four nested indexed records, with the identity record at `N+3` ending at `N+4`. From the identity record header, offsets 11 through 19 are zero, offset 20 is the presence byte `01`, offsets 21 through 24 are a zero `u32`, offsets 25 through 28 are the owning Sketch entity record index, offsets 29 through 32 are a zero `u32`, offsets 33 through 36 are the sketch-point persistent id, and offsets 37 through 40 are a zero `u32`. The next indexed record is `N+4` and carries the point-data record. The owning Sketch record index and sketch-point persistent id resolve to one sketch point; the point belongs to a planar or model-space sketch according to its projected sketch arena.

For `refType` 10, this direct sketch-point envelope is an alternate to the B-rep vertex recipe; the input envelope determines which semantic operand is selected.

The base level's first counted reference list holds the point's geometry inputs. `point3d` is the work point in centimetres. `refType` names the construction rule. Value `5` selects the center of one circular edge. Value `7` selects the intersection of two edges. Value `8` selects the intersection of three planes. Value `10` selects one B-rep vertex. Value `14` selects the intersection of one edge and one plane. Value `20` selects a normalized position along one edge. These forms carry respectively one, two, three, one, two, and one input references. The u32 immediately after the optional `tangentPointData` is the input count, and that many marked references follow it; the count, rather than a fixed rule table, frames the input run. `refType` is not bounded above by the rules a given file uses. Each input names a record of class `5662F619-2281-4AD9-93DC-1FCFDA1062E7` or `5A1BF548-241F-46FD-9FB5-E4B05126EB9D`; both classes derive from `6144EA59-3ACA-4172-9F14-A6531E94D69B`. An edge-valued input uses the four-record `edge_recipe_data` envelope that starts at indexed record `N`, but the following indexed header is `N+5`; this consumer does not use the ordinary edge-feature envelope's `N+4` closing index. A vertex-valued input has the same `N` through `N+3` envelope and `N+5` closing index with family name `vertex_recipe_data`. Its persistent face references select one common vertex in the input history state, and that vertex's point position equals `point3d`. A plane-valued input uses the compact persistent entity-selection envelope. Its primary identity names the indexed record immediately before the selected `WorkPlane` scope record. Value `20` owns one `PathDistance` parameter. Its finite evaluated scalar is the path fraction in the closed interval from zero through one; it is not a centimetre length. A `refType` of `18` may include a second input naming a child point-data record and repeats that child's `point3d` unchanged. `boundingboxIndex` holds uninitialized memory where the record does not use it, so a reader takes it only from a record whose `refType` gives it a meaning. `tangentPointData` is `(-1, -1, -1)` where unused.

A member-run sketch's `EntityGenesis`-form entity header is followed by a paired same-index record whose prologue — zero bytes from record offset 11 to 19, `0x01` at offset 19, and a u64 head-record reference at offset 20 — names the head record of the sketch's member run. The head record stores its u32 record index at offset 7, eleven zero bytes, the row-major 4×4 f64 local-to-model placement at offset 22, and a `0x00 0x01`-marked tail; the record is 162 bytes. The matrix row and column semantics equal the explicit placement frame, and the origin is in centimetres. The entity header's reference tail carries a marked back-reference to the owning feature's entity. A localized `Esquisse` scope does not repeat the sketch entity suffix in its generic reference table. It owns the preceding sketch entity when it follows that entity before the next sketch entity in the same Design stream. A member-run sketch without such a scope retains no parameter-scope owner.

A text-frame boundary curve has type GUID `16DEFC4D-1816-4FB0-8E39-9BDA23954248`, version `0`, and add-in `MSketch`. It uses the ordinary `EntityGenesis` curve-identity prefix followed by two point references. The first reference has seven zero bytes after its u32 record index and the second has six. An inline indexed analytic-curve record follows: its numeric LP-ASCII class tag is followed by the enclosing curve record index, eight zero bytes, and the ordinary twelve-f64 bounded-line payload. The line coordinates and displacement use centimetres.

A sketch relation is the leading-block presence byte `01`, a u32 count and that many `(reference, u32 relation ordinal)` pairs, the property-block presence byte and its block, the `ParentNode` reference naming the owning Sketch, a u64 constraint mask, a u32 count and that many bare references, and one zero byte. The leading-block presence byte is also the constraint-mask width discriminator. Value `01` selects the paired member run and the u64 mask. Value `00` selects the relation base class's version-0 form, omits the pair list, and selects a u32 mask. Another value is invalid and does not select a mask width. The mask is little-endian, so every mask above `0xff` begins with a zero byte and a reader that locates it by skipping zeros reads it shifted. The property block carries `EntityGenesis` where the relation has one. The record ends at the trailing zero byte, at the next indexed-record header. Relation kind is a property of the record class: the shared relation class, the text-path class, and the spline class are distinct classes, and no member of any of them encodes a kind.

A sketch-relation class writes its own members between the property block and `ParentNode`. The class is named by the type GUID its segment's type table carries for the record's entity; a class tag cannot name it, because a tag is `256` plus an index into that segment's own type table and one tag names different relation classes in different segments. `60403D47-0C49-49B0-BDE8-1679608164A2` is the shared relation class, `D3BD153B-EB8A-405E-9D29-69EE0C3D227C` the offset class, and `73762C3B-82DC-4632-93B0-B8FE1CC5282F` the spline-group class; none of the three writes a member of its own, so `ParentNode` follows the property block directly. `24DB790E-3DCD-4336-AFA3-6F119EF2239B` is the tangency class and writes three `u8` flags, each `0` or `1`. `8B369926-123F-4F9D-878E-6D4C076128D3` is the text-frame class and writes two references, the second of which is the sketch-text entity and repeats the relation's first member. `9D30FCDC-EA07-4141-93E2-918B1A59E962` is the text-path class and writes the text-entity reference, a u32 character count, and that many glyph blocks; from class version 1 a `u8 1` precedes the reference. `8269E861-0BB7-47E0-9911-5AE3EC475058` is the circular-pattern class and writes the angle-parameter reference, the count-parameter reference, the evaluated f64 total angle in radians, the evaluated u32 instance count, the pattern tables, and one zero byte. `40800FB9-C2BE-494E-A047-7D76E82B9F6C` is the rectangular-pattern class and writes three `u8` flags, a u32-counted run of references, the pattern tables, and its two direction clauses. The pattern tables are a u32-counted map whose entry is a u64 key, a u32 count, and that many u64 values, followed by a u32-counted run of u32; either table may be empty. A reader that instead walks this run reference to reference and stops at the first reference naming a sketch desynchronizes on the flags and counts that precede a pattern class's references.

The two reference runs hold the same members. The integer interleaved into the first run is a relation ordinal, not a member role: it counts the relations already recorded on the member it follows, so the integers on one member are pairwise distinct across that member's relations and say nothing about the member's part in this relation. Only the second run is in semantic order, and member position below means position in the second run. A native neutral relation retains both runs and the auxiliary references in field order; repeated references remain repeated. A zero mask denotes the default coincident relation. Constraint bits are `0x1` coincident, `0x2` colinear, `0x4` concentric or operand-discriminated symmetry, `0x8` equal length, `0x10` parallel or operand-discriminated midpoint, `0x20` perpendicular or operand-discriminated offset, `0x40` horizontal, `0x80` vertical, `0x100` tangent, `0x200` curvature, `0x400` symmetry, `0x800` equal, `0x1000` midpoint, `0x2000` polygon, `0x10000000` circular pattern, `0x20000000` rectangular pattern, `0x80000000` spline group, `0x2000000000` offset, `0x10000000000` text frame, and `0x20000000000` text path. Binary neutral geometric constraints require two distinct entity identities. A single-bit `0x40` or `0x80` relation with one line constrains the line horizontally or vertically. With two point entities, `0x40` constrains equal vertical sketch coordinates and `0x80` constrains equal horizontal sketch coordinates. A single-bit polygon relation with at least three completely resolved member references transfers those members in source order as one polygon constraint. A single-bit `0x100` or `0x200` relation transfers only when both resolved members are curve-valued; `0x100` is tangency and `0x200` is equal tangent-direction and curvature continuity at contact. A single-bit `0x800` relation transfers equal size for two lines, two circular entities, or two ellipses; a circle and circular arc are compatible circular entities. Other member families retain the native relation and ordered operands. A `0x4` relation between two circular or elliptical entities is concentric. A three-member `0x4` or `0x400` relation is symmetry when all three entity identities are distinct and exactly one line member is a reflection axis that maps the other two point, line, equal-radius full-circle, or equal-radius circular-arc entities onto each other; reflected arcs also have equal sweep magnitude and reflected endpoint sets. The unique geometric relation assigns the axis independently of member order. A `0x10` relation with two line members is parallel. A `0x10` or `0x1000` relation with one bounded line and one point whose coordinates equal the arithmetic mean of the line's stored endpoints constrains that point to the line midpoint; member order does not change the roles. A `0x10000000` relation's input members are the pattern center point and the seed entities; its generated members are the instance entities. Its class members carry the angle parameter, the count parameter, the evaluated total angle, and the evaluated instance count. Its return run lists the instances in instance order and ends with the center point. A `0x20000000` relation stores two direction clauses after the class's counted reference run and its pattern tables. The first clause starts at the byte after the second pattern table, and its count-parameter reference is the first auxiliary reference after the counted run. Each clause is the evaluated u32 count, the count-parameter reference, a three-component f64 unit direction, a source distance scalar, and the distance-parameter reference, in that order, so each count directly precedes its count-parameter reference. All four clause parameter references must be present before either direction transfers; an absent reference does not change the byte framing and leaves the pattern definition unresolved. A non-empty counted reference run stores adjacent spacing in the source distance scalar. An empty counted reference run stores the total seed-to-final span; its adjacent spacing is the scalar divided by count minus one. Count includes the seed instance. The member and return runs list instances in instance-major order with one fixed per-instance entity order; the first instance is the seed. A `0x80000000` relation is the spline-group relation: it binds a spline's curve-identity records and its primary curve record to the owning sketch and carries no geometric constraint; a relation whose sole bit is `0x80000000` and whose complete member run resolves to distinct typed entities transfers those members in source order as one spline-group constraint. In a model-space spline group the second run ends with the NURBS and the preceding members are the consecutive control-polygon segments: member `i` joins control points `i` and `i+1`. The first run holds the same members in an unrelated order, so its last entry does not name the spline. A preceding member without an independent geometry payload inherits that exact solved segment; an independently stored segment must have the same ordered endpoints. A `0x10000000000` relation's members are the sketch-text entity followed by its frame curves; the text entity repeats as the second of the class's two references, and the return run names the frame curves. A `0x20000000000` relation occupies a record class distinct from the shared sketch-relation class. Its members are the path curve and the sketch-text entity, and its return run names the path curve. Its class members are the text-entity reference, a u32 character count, and that many blocks of `u32 16` and a row-major 4×4 f64 transform placing one character in the owning sketch frame in centimetres, in character order; `ParentNode` follows the last block directly. Pattern angle- and distance-parameter references resolve to scalar parameter owner frames and pattern count-parameter references resolve to counted parameter owner frames of the pattern's input parameters.

Sketch text occupies two record classes. `E0618268-3A06-450E-9E94-7CF4C2E66802` is the `textex_tag` class and `f0b1afa3-3baf-42d0-b2f3-94b95662f2a9` is the `txt_tag` class; both derive from `8E9D588C-28EF-43C4-B9A6-AA5430A68D4B` and both type-table entries carry the add-in name `Geometry`. A legacy record of either class carries its identity as a property-block key, not as a member: `textex_tag` in the first class and `txt_tag` in the second, with `txt_tag_base` and `EntityGenesis` optional in both. A `txt_tag` record below class version 4 writes no identity key at all and stores no persistent identity; its property block can hold `txt_tag_base` alone. Such a record uses its indexed-record index as a source-local text identity within the owning Sketch. The source-local identity is unique within the Design stream but does not claim persistence across a rewritten source document. The legacy classes share the record prefix, the leading block, and the property block, and diverge after them. The leading block takes the form a sketch relation carries: a presence byte, and, when that byte is `01`, a u32 count and that many `(reference, u32)` pairs.

The indexed Design form of `textex_tag` has the indexed header: the three-digit class tag is followed by a u32 record index at byte 7. Bytes 11 through 19 are zero, and the ordinary property block starts at byte 20. This form has no record-name string or leading relation block. Its property block contains `textex_tag` and may contain `EntityGenesis`. The byte after the property block is zero, followed by the f64 width factor, four f32 RGBA colour components, the counted UTF-16LE font-family name, a zero byte, and the f64 nominal text height in centimetres. After the common text body, its class suffix is a u32 text-type discriminator (`0` for frame text or `1` for path text), followed by u32 values `1, 256, 0, 0`, two zero bytes, two positive f32 scale values, and five zero bytes. The owning-sketch reference follows the suffix. The indexed form carries no neutral anchor or rotation.

In a `textex_tag` record the property block is followed by `u8 1`, an f64 horizontal width factor, four f32 RGBA colour components each in `[0, 1]`, a u32-count UTF-16LE font-family name, `u8 0`, and an f64 nominal text height in centimetres. The width factor precedes the font family and the height follows it; a reader that swaps them assigns every text the same height. The width factor takes `0` and `1`, so a reader that requires it positive refuses the records storing `0`; the height is always positive. A `0` does not scale glyph advance to zero, so it does not denote a zero horizontal scale, and a neutral model takes no width factor from a record storing zero.

In a `txt_tag` record the twenty-nine bytes from the end of the property block to the u32 font-family count are the f64 rotation, five further bytes, and the four f32 RGBA colour components each in `[0, 1]`. The class stores no width factor. Its font-family name is directly followed by the height, with no zero byte between them, and it inserts members between the height and the text string: two bytes, the f64 x and y coordinates of the text anchor point in the sketch frame in centimetres, and eleven further bytes ahead of the u32-count UTF-16LE text string. Below class version 4 that run is ten bytes; the eleventh is added at version 4.

A `textex_tag` record writes two optional parameter-reference members: one after the height naming the parameter driving it, and one after the text string naming the parameter driving the content. The record either writes a member, whose own presence byte then says whether it targets a parameter, or omits it; nothing ahead of a slot distinguishes the two, so a reader frames the record by requiring the member sequence to end exactly on the owning-sketch reference. Between the two slots the record stores a u32 horizontal-alignment enum, three flag bytes, and a u32-count UTF-16LE text string; after the second slot it stores a u32 vertical-alignment enum, one flag byte, and an i32 font weight. Horizontal value `1` is left, `2` is right, and `3` is center. Vertical value `1` is top, `2` is bottom, and `3` is middle. Each alignment varies independently of the other. The font weight takes `400`, `500`, and `750`.

A `textex_tag` record's class tail opens with a u32 text-type enum: `0` is frame text and stores a row-major 4×4 f64 placement transform, `1` is path text and stores none. One flag byte follows the enum, ahead of the transform slot, and repeats the enum's value. The transform is a planar rigid placement. Its bottom row is `(0, 0, 0, 1)`, its third row and third column are the identity's, and its 2×2 basis has unit determinant, so it carries no scale and no shear. Column 3 is the text anchor point in the sketch frame in centimetres, and the angle of basis column 0 is the text rotation counterclockwise about that anchor. A record whose text type is path text stores no anchor and no rotation. A `txt_tag` record writes neither the parameter-reference slots nor the text-type enum and its transform: its f64 text rotation in radians directly follows the property block and is followed by five bytes before the colour components. Its text string is followed by a u32-counted reference run, three bytes, an i32 font weight, and eight unclassified bytes. Zero is an explicit unrotated value. Thirty bytes and the padded owning-sketch reference close a record of either class. Text-frame and text-path relations resolve the text record as a typed sketch entity. Text-path character transforms scale their translation column from centimetres to neutral millimetres.

A two-line `0x20` relation is perpendicular. An aggregate `0x20` offset relation has an even return-member run of at least four curve records. Consecutive return members form ordered `(source, result)` pairs. Each source curve has a null secondary persistent identity and each result curve has a nonzero secondary identity. Every resolved entity participates in exactly one pair. Every pair resolves to parallel bounded lines at one common positive separation magnitude. A result on the source curve's right side reverses that source traversal before applying the common distance along its left normal. The relation constrains the complete ordered pair set at that common offset distance. The `0x2000000000` offset relation stores an even member run. Its second run supplies the source/result bijection in order, as consecutive `(source, result)` pairs; source secondary identities need not be null, so one relation's results can be the next relation's sources. A single-curve linear annotation frame drives an otherwise unparameterized offset relation when its curve is one of that relation's sources and its parameter magnitude uniquely equals the relation's common distance.

Each member and return-member index resolves through the indexed Design record graph. A sketch-point record contributes its persistent point identity, and a sketch-curve record contributes its primary and nullable secondary persistent curve identities. Other indices retain their generic indexed-record identity. Resolution preserves the encoded run order and cardinality. Persistent point identities and persistent curve-identity pairs are unique within one owning sketch in one Design `BulkStream`. Neutral sketch-entity identities combine the byte-length-prefixed owning sketch identity, a point-or-curve discriminator, and the corresponding persistent identity values; indexed-record position does not participate.

A relation whose sole constraint bit is coincident and whose complete member run resolves to distinct typed sketch points or curves constrains those entity loci to coincide. Repeated references to one entity retain the native relation because they do not distinguish multiple loci on that entity. A point and curve pair is a point-on-curve constraint. The encoded member run supplies the neutral coincident-locus order without an additional endpoint selector. When the solved geometry has exactly one shared point across all members and exactly one point, start, end, or center locus per member at that position, those exact loci define the neutral constraint. Multiple shared positions or multiple loci from one member at the shared position retain whole-entity coincidence.

Relations owned by a spatial Sketch use the same member and return runs. Coincident, midpoint, tangent, horizontal, vertical, and spline-group bits retain their ordinary geometric meanings in model space. A coincident relation resolves only between two distinct points at the same transformed model-space position. A midpoint relation resolves only when its point equals the transformed midpoint of its bounded line. A tangent relation resolves only between two distinct spatial curve entities. A single-line horizontal relation constrains the model-space line parallel to placement-matrix column 0; a single-line vertical relation constrains it parallel to column 1. Spatial point coordinates come from the point record's complete `(u,v,w)` payload rather than from a planar projection.

A spatial Sketch linear-dimension annotation frame can carry one null annotation locus followed by two line loci. The two line records define the measured entities; the null locus carries no geometric operand. When the transformed lines are parallel, their model-space minimum separation equals the governing length parameter.

A spatial Sketch `Linear Dimension*` parameter without a typed annotation frame first governs every bounded line in its owner whose model-space endpoint distance equals the evaluated length within the source kernel's absolute linear resolution. One matching line establishes a line-length relation. Multiple matching lines establish a repeated line-length relation in spatial-sketch entity order. With no matching line, the parameter governs a parallel-line distance only when exactly one pair of transformed lines has the evaluated model-space minimum separation. When that entity-pair rule does not establish a relation, collinear transformed lines form carriers within the same resolution. The unique pair of parallel carriers with the evaluated minimum separation and overlapping bounded member spans establishes a parallel-line-set distance when at least one carrier contains multiple entities. Carrier members follow spatial-sketch entity order. Zero or multiple matching carrier pairs do not establish a neutral relation.

The owning sketch reference is the numeric suffix of the owning sketch container's full Design entity id. It resolves through the sketch entity-header table. The reference applies to every typed sketch-point and sketch-curve record named by the relation's member and return-member runs. All relations using one typed geometry record carry the same owning sketch reference. Conflicting owner references are invalid.

A sketch-point record contains one typed property named `pt_tag`: `u32 property_count=1`, LP-ASCII `pt_tag`, LP-ASCII `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64`, and the persistent u64 point id. The record then stores `0x01`, a u32 paired record reference, a 14-byte flag area whose bytes are each `0x00` or `0x01`, and two f64 sketch coordinates at record offsets 89 and 97. The alternate form sets `property_count=2` and prefixes `pt_tag` with an `EntityGenesis` `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64` property; all subsequent fields shift by 52 bytes.

A sketch-curve record contains two typed properties in order: `crv_primary_id` and `crv_secondary_id`, both `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64`. The primary id is the curve's persistent identity; zero in the secondary slot is null. The alternate form sets `property_count=3` and prefixes these properties with an `EntityGenesis` `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64` property, shifting the curve identity and geometry fields by 52 bytes. The `EntityGenesis` value on a sketch point or curve is an origin bitfield: bit `0x2` marks a user-authored entity, bit `0x4` marks SVG-imported sketch geometry, bit `0x8` marks source geometry imported from DXF, bits `0x80` and `0x100` mark rectangular- and circular-pattern membership, and bit `0x200` marks a result curve generated by a sketch offset. A generated center point of an imported circular arc and native text-generated entities carry zero. The segment type table selects the geometry family before the geometry payload is read. Type GUID `DCA267ED-D615-4934-B64F-AD805E8003E2`, version `1` or `2`, add-in `Geometry`, is the bounded-line class. Its derived bounded-line classes are `EA3B930A-3383-4AD3-BE25-4B2814EA3985`, version `0`; `AE42BAB6-643F-4169-A33C-529C8E0A4D84`, version `0`; `F279874A-17AB-43DA-BF8E-80259802D06E`, version `0`; and `58751243-FEA8-41E6-BBC9-37960EB8164B`, version `0`, all with add-in `Geometry`. Type GUID `F0130424-8B7E-4092-93C9-1CA807482534`, version `0`, add-in `Geometry`, is the full-circle and circular-arc class. Type GUID `FF23079A-D99C-47AB-940E-2F4E18F022AB`, version `2`, add-in `Geometry`, is a second circular-arc class. A full analytic payload following the identity properties is twelve f64 values. A line stores `(start point xyz, displacement xyz, auxiliary unit direction xyz, unit sketch normal xyz)`. Start plus displacement defines its bounded geometry; the neutral unit tangent is the normalized displacement. A circular arc stores `(center xyz, unit normal xyz, in-plane unit reference direction xyz, radius, start angle, end angle)`. Angles are radians. A full circle stores an angular interval spanning 2π. Point coordinates, displacements, and radii in fixed-form records are in centimetres; in `EntityGenesis`-form records they are ten times the centimetre value, and the placement frame maps `(x/10, y/10, 0)` into model space. A planar neutral projection accepts local sketch normal `(0,0,+1)` or `(0,0,-1)` and multiplies arc angles by its z sign to use the neutral positive-z angular convention. A referenced analytic wrapper prefixes this payload with `0x01 + u32 record_ref + six zero bytes`.

A spatial-sketch surface record has `property_count=2`: `EntityGenesis` followed by `surface_tag`, both `IntrinsicMetaTypeuint64`. The surface tag is its persistent identity. A u32 control-point count follows the surface tag, then that many xyz f64 control points in centimetres. The tensor-product definition follows as u32 first degree, u32 second degree, u32 first knot count and its f64 knots, u32 second knot count and its f64 knots, then u32 first control count and u32 second control count. Control points are first-parameter-major. Their count equals the product of the two control counts, and each knot count equals its control count plus its degree plus one. Both knot vectors are nondecreasing. The owning spatial Sketch placement transforms every control point into model space.

A spatial-sketch `Coincident` relation between a point record and a surface record constrains the point to lie on that surface.

A sketch NURBS payload begins with either an eight-byte all-`0xff` null sentinel or a non-null u64 carrier reference, then a nested dynamic-class record header, the degree marker, f64 fit tolerance, and three arrays. Each array header is `(u32 count, u32 duplicate_count, u32 scalar_width=8)`. The arrays are the nondecreasing f64 knot vector, positive f64 weights, and xyz f64 control points. A non-rational curve stores a zero-length weight array; otherwise weight and control-point counts are equal. In both forms, `knot_count = control_point_count + degree + 1`. Fit tolerance and control points are in centimetres. Type GUID `D82E012F-6DDD-4AED-BDE1-C0F7F9100B9B`, version `3`, add-in `MSketch`, is the standalone spline-curve class.
