It carries this payload after its curve-identity properties, and its counted control-point array holds the curve's control points in centimetres.

The ACT `BulkStream` is framed by its sibling `MetaStream` primary index. Each primary `(u64 record index, u64 BulkStream offset)` pair starts one record. Its extent ends at the next primary offset or at the end of the stream. Offsets increase strictly, and record indexes are unique u32 values. Each record starts with an LP-ASCII class tag of exactly three decimal digits and the same u32 record index. Class tag `n` names type-table entry `n - 256`, and that entry's entity-ID list contains the record index. An `ACTTable` byte window in another indexed record's payload does not identify the table.

**The ACT segment.** An ACT segment tracks change versions per design entity. Its records key on an **entity key**, the LP-UTF16 string `<segment id>_<entity id>` naming a design entity of the same document. Where an entity key sits beside a reference to the entity it names, the segment id in the key is that reference's target segment id.

An **ACT component link** payload is ten zero bytes, `0x01 + u32 instance-root record + six zero bytes`, an entity key, `0x01 + u32 tracked-entity record + five zero bytes`, `0x01 + u32 registry flag`, an LP-UTF16 display name, one through eight zero bytes, and `0x01 + u32 components-root record`. Zero bytes can fill the remainder of the indexed record extent. The registry flag is zero or one. There is one link per component. The link whose tracked-entity record is `3` is the document-root link; its entity key does not select the root and need not end in `_3`. An ACT stream has exactly one document-root link.

An **ACT change group** payload is ten zero bytes, a u32 count, that many `(LP-ASCII change kind, LP-UTF16 change version GUID)` pairs, and the entity key of the entity the group tracks. Zero bytes can fill the remainder of the indexed record extent. The change kinds are `Appearance`, `Geometry`, `PhysicalMaterial`, `Properties`, `StructureChange`, and `Transform`. A group holds a subset of them: `Appearance`, `Geometry`, and `Properties` are in every group, and `PhysicalMaterial`, `Transform`, and `StructureChange` are not. The all-zero GUID is the unchanged state. The indexed record index is the change group's identity. One index carries one entity key and one group. A group can have no inline ACT-table row.

The ACT segment has exactly one indexed **ACT table** record. After its dynamic record header, it stores a zero u32, LP-ASCII `ACTTable`, a zero u16, a u32 row count, and that many rows. A row is `0x01 + u32 change-group record index + six zero bytes + LP-UTF16 entity key`. Its record index names one change group, and its key equals that group's key. Duplicate row indexes, duplicate groups, a missing group, or a key disagreement are invalid.

An uncounted ordered GUID pool follows the rows. Each pool item is an LP-UTF16 canonical 36-character hyphenated GUID. The pool carries stream-wide change-version and occurrence-role identities. It ends at the first field that is not such a GUID; pool position does not assign a GUID to one row or channel. A u32 reference count follows, then that many `0x01 + u32 target record + six zero bytes` references. Each target is a record in the same primary index. A u32 channel-registry count follows, then that many `(LP-ASCII channel name, LP-UTF16 canonical 36-character GUID)` entries. Registry names are unique. The registry ends at the indexed table record's extent.

An **ACT instance root** opens with the prologue, a further u8, a u32 count, and that many LP-ASCII change kinds, which are the same six kinds in the same order. Two references, the entity key, and a further reference follow them.

On a body, `generic_tag_attrib_def` supplies a one-through-eight-byte ASCII design/construction ID distinct from the material-assignment suffix. This ID keys the design BulkStream body construction-recipe records and is retained without truncation. A body can have no body-keyed recipe.

An executed `Fillet` or `Chamfer` group with exactly one selection operand resolves a connected source-edge chain from its exact ASM transition. A result face is a treatment face when its sole surface carrier is absent from the preceding topology. Its support faces are result faces adjacent across its boundary whose sole surface carrier maps to exactly one preceding face. Every preceding edge shared by a pair of those support faces and deleted by the transition belongs to the selected chain. The chain is the nonempty increasing stable-slot-order union across all treatment faces. Recipe evidence is the union of each operand's changed and deleted boundary edges and the changed-reference edge sets selected by its local topology references; when local topology references are absent, all reference contexts contribute. When any recipe evidence exists, every chain edge must occur in that union. A directly resolved deleted boundary edge must occur in the chain, and every operand with deleted boundary edges must share one chain edge. A transition-chain vector is scoped to the complete state transition and repeats on persistent identities that share that transition. The repeated vectors do not assign edges to individual identities. A group with multiple full-layout persistent members does not take the transition-chain vector as its selection.

An executed `Hem` group has one selectorless empty reference context for the selected edge and one or more nonempty reference contexts for its support faces. The union of those nonempty contexts contains the support-face boundary edges changed by the transition. The selected source edge is the unique changed boundary edge absent from that union. A sole candidate is a historical edge selection in the group's preceding state; no sole candidate leaves the native selection unresolved.

The deleted-triplet proof is independent of contextual reference-edge intersections. A unique deleted-triplet slot resolves when selected reference sets are empty or disjoint. Any other exact deleted-edge proof must name the same slot.

A class-413 Mirror scope has primary class `413` and paired class `262`. Its ordered reference table is variable and precedes the kind string. The fixed tail after the UTF-16LE kind has 77 bytes: offset 0 stores the feature ordinal, offset 31 stores the preceding-history state, offset 35 stores u32 marker `89`, offset 39 stores the positive stitch tolerance as an f64 in source centimetres, and offset 47 repeats marker `89`. Offset 51 stores a marked reference to scope index plus two followed by two zero bytes; offset 64 stores a marked reference to scope index plus one followed by two zero bytes. The paired indexed header begins at offset 77 of this tail. The class-413 frame has no owner-local ordinal-one tolerance record.

A class-440 Mirror scope has primary class `440` and paired class `258`. Its ordered reference table is variable and precedes the kind string. The fixed tail after the UTF-16LE kind has 77 bytes with the same field offsets and reference order as the class-413 tail, but both scalar markers are u32 `100`. The f64 at tail offset 39 is the positive stitch tolerance in source centimetres; the references at offsets 51 and 64 name scope index plus two and plus one, each followed by two zero bytes. The paired indexed header begins at tail offset 77. The class-440 frame has no owner-local ordinal-one tolerance record.

A class-441 Mirror scope has primary class `441` and paired class `267`. Its ordered reference table is variable and precedes the kind string. The fixed tail after the UTF-16LE kind has 72 bytes: offset 0 stores the feature ordinal, offset 30 stores the preceding-history state, offset 34 stores u32 marker `61`, and offset 38 stores the positive stitch tolerance as an f64 in source centimetres. Offset 46 stores a marked reference to scope index plus two followed by two zero bytes, and offset 59 stores a marked reference to scope index plus one followed by two zero bytes. The paired indexed header begins at tail offset 72. This form has no repeated scalar marker and no owner-local ordinal-one tolerance record. The scope reference at ordinal 3 names a class-426 primary frame paired with class `267`; its 99-byte frame stores the exact count `2` at offset 40, a parameter reference to count-record index plus two at offset 45, owned ordinal `1` at offset 55, scope-record index at offset 64, companion-record index plus one at offset 77, and scope-record index at offset 89. The paired class-267 header begins at offset 99.

A class-391 Mirror scope has primary class `391` and paired class `261`. Its ordered reference table is variable and precedes the kind string. The fixed tail after the UTF-16LE kind has 76 bytes: offset 0 stores the feature ordinal, offset 30 stores the preceding-history state, offset 34 stores u32 marker `94`, and offset 38 stores the positive stitch tolerance as an f64 in source centimetres. Offset 46 stores u32 marker `94`; offset 50 stores a marked reference to scope index plus two followed by two zero bytes, and offset 63 stores a marked reference to scope index plus one followed by two zero bytes. The paired indexed header begins at tail offset 76. This form has no owner-local ordinal-one tolerance record.

A class-369 Mirror scope has primary class `369` and paired class `261`. Its ordered reference table is variable and precedes the kind string. The fixed tail after the UTF-16LE kind has 77 bytes: offset 0 stores the feature ordinal, offset 31 stores the preceding-history state, offset 35 stores u32 marker `89`, offset 39 stores the positive stitch tolerance as an f64 in source centimetres, and offset 47 repeats marker `89`. Offset 51 stores a marked reference to scope index plus two followed by two zero bytes; offset 64 stores a marked reference to scope index plus one followed by two zero bytes. The paired indexed header begins at offset 77 of this tail. The class-369 frame has no owner-local ordinal-one tolerance record.

#### Constant-radius edge chains

A recipe-backed constant-radius group resolves by a chain proof. Every member contributes all of its radius-qualified deleted predecessor edges, including candidates outside that member's changed boundary; an individually proven member also contributes its proven edge. A member with neither contribution is topology context and is permitted only when its changed-boundary-edge set is empty. The group resolves to the increasing stable-slot-order union of these contributions. One operand can therefore denote more than one edge in a connected treatment chain. A transition-derived radius-candidate vector on a persistent identity describes the complete operation transition and can prove only a one-member group. Except for the compact exact-deletion cardinality proof, a multi-member persistent-identity group requires an exact edge contribution from every member.

#### Edge-recipe incidence matching

For each selector, an edge is an incidence match when, for every present clause entry, its incident loops contain both encoded boundary-count and coedge-ordinal incidences. Each selector retains all matching changed historical edge slots in stable slot order and separately retains the edge slot when that set is a singleton. Primary operand resolution uses the complete selector/reference intersection. An explicit reference run whose every candidate set is empty suppresses selector incidence and boundary-count proofs. It does not suppress a topology-triplet or deleted-topology proof, because those proofs derive the edge identity independently; every nonempty reference candidate set, when present, still intersects that identity. When the primary intersection is empty, exactly one singleton persistent-reference edge resolves the operand when the edge also belongs to the deleted boundary set and appears in a selector's incidence, boundary-count, or topology-triplet candidates. A construction group with a known preceding history state retains that state and every ordered operand identity even when none of its edge identities resolves.

A suppressed edge-treatment scope resolves its recipes against the unique terminal topology of its ASM history. The terminal state is the feature's replay input state. An operand's terminal face context is the ordered, deduplicated union of its primary persistent-reference faces and each prefix reference's exact-selector faces, falling back to that reference's alternate-selector faces only when its exact set is empty. Incidence, boundary-count, and common-triplet proofs operate on the resulting terminal boundaries without transition-only changed-edge filters and must resolve to one common slot. More than one terminal state in a history, or matching terminal states in more than one history, does not select a topology. Deleted-edge, treatment-radius, changed-context, and scope-partition proofs require an executed transition and do not apply. When the terminal recipe graph has no complete group proof, every proven edge and every unresolved operand identity form a partial historical selection.

#### Assembly operands

The four 421-byte `As-built` generations use one exact class and offset map:

| Scope primary / paired class | Scalar owner primary / paired class | Operand-carrier classes in reference order | Solved frame primary / paired class | Frame span; marker, matrix, and paired-header offsets | Limit source order and domain |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `364 / 272` | `293 / 272` | `351 / 307`, `350 / 307` | `376 / 272` | 389 bytes; marker `+45..48 = 01 01 00 00`, matrix `+49`, pair `+389` | `AngleMinimum`, `AngleMaximum`; angular |
| `420 / 262` | `378 / 262` | `426 / 273`, `400 / 273` | `327 / 262` | 390 bytes; marker `+46..49 = 01 01 00 00`, matrix `+50`, pair `+390` | `DistanceMaximum`, `DistanceMinimum`; linear, reverse source order |
| `417 / 263` | `318 / 263` | `432 / 263`, `332 / 263`, `312 / 263`, `332 / 263` | `448 / 263` | 390 bytes; marker `+46..49 = 01 01 00 00`, matrix `+50`, pair `+390` | `DistanceMaximum`, `DistanceMinimum`; linear, reverse source order |
| `457 / 258` | `418 / 258` | `296 / 258`, `264 / 258`, `363 / 258`, `264 / 258` | `297 / 258` | 385 bytes; marker `+45..48 = 01 01 00 00`, matrix `+49`, pair `+385` | `DistanceMinimum`, `DistanceMaximum`; linear |

The class-430/262 `Assemble` generation uses class-313 parameter-owner
records with 103-byte owner frames. Its 744-byte scope uses the compact
operand-frame form: operand references at offsets 24 and 164, transforms at
offsets 36 and 176, and the two operand-path locators at offsets 362 and 373
after the count at offset 358. Its 748-byte scope uses the standard form:
operand references at offsets 28 and 168, transforms at offsets 40 and 180,
and the two operand-path locators at offsets 366 and 377 after the count at
offset 362. Both forms have eight owners. Owner ordinals 0 through 3 are
placement lanes; ordinals 4 through 7 are angle, X, Y, and Z alignment lanes.
The class pair is part of the admission key because the same frame lengths
occur in other scope families.

An `Assemble` or `As-built` scope stores finite alignment parameter owners.
The serialized frame length and total owner count select the lane layout; the
class pair is an additional admission key for the 744- and 748-byte forms.
The 399-byte `As-built` form and the 627-, 633-, 637-, and 692-byte `Assemble`
forms have exactly four owners. All four are alignment lanes in owner-local
order: signed angle, signed local X offset, signed local Y offset, and signed
local Z offset. The 604-byte datum-envelope form, the 732-byte joint form, and
the class-430/262 744- and 748-byte forms have exactly eight owners. Their
four placement owners are at local ordinals zero through three, and their four
alignment owners are at ordinals four through seven in the same angle, X, Y,
and Z order. The 705-byte form has exactly six owners: one group of `OffsetX`,
`OffsetY`, `OffsetZ`, and `AngleZ` placement lanes followed by `alignAngle`
and `alignOffset`. The 772-byte form has exactly ten owners: two such
placement groups followed by `alignAngle` and `alignOffset`. The final scalar
of each axial form is an alignment offset and maps to local Z. No other
frame-length, owner-count, and class-pair combination carries an assembly
alignment. Joint forms store two operand frames. The 627-, 637-, and 692-byte
forms store marked operand construction references at offsets 28 and 168 and
row-major rigid 4×4 operand-local-to-model transforms at offsets 40 and 180.
The 633- and 732-byte forms store the references at offsets 24 and 164 and the
transforms at offsets 36 and 176. The 705- and 772-byte forms store operand
references at offsets 28 and 167 and transforms at offsets 39 and 178. In the
399-byte `As-built` form, the locator records store the operand construction
references and transforms. Matrix translations are in centimetres. Alignment
angles are in radians and alignment offsets are in centimetres. The alignment
owner records are the final members of the scope reference table. Each value
is the evaluated scalar duplicated from its parameter record by its parameter
owner, independent of the owner-frame class and width.


In a 421-byte `As-built` scope, the eleven-entry reference table starts with its marked entry at offset 189. Offset 185 stores u32 value 11. Entries 0 and 1 form the first operand-carrier pair, entries 2 and 3 form the second pair, entries 4 through 7 are the placement owners in `OffsetX`, `OffsetY`, `OffsetZ`, and `AngleZ` order, entry 8 is the solved connector-frame carrier, and entries 9 and 10 are the two degree-of-freedom limit owners. Offset 310 stores four `ff` bytes, offset 314 stores u32 value 8, offset 318 stores the UTF-16LE string `As-built`, and offset 334 stores the feature ordinal. The canonical alignment values are the `AngleZ` value followed by the three offsets. Each row in the generation map supplies the carrier pair, solved frame, paired header, and limit interpretation. The frame carrier stores one solved connector-frame matrix; it does not store either operand-local frame. The two operand-carrier pairs are direct construction/selection pairs and do not use the operand-path locator run of the 399-byte form.

The first direct pair is a point construction followed by a face-recipe selection. The second is a point-and-direction Hole construction followed by a face-recipe selection. The primary construction classes and selection classes are the four class pairs listed in the generation table, in reference order. The construction record header is the `byte_offset` named by scope reference 0 or 2; the paired selection header is the `byte_offset` named by scope reference 1 or 3. The point position and Hole position are model-space centimetres. The Hole direction equals the third column of the solved frame's rotation matrix. No construction carrier stores an independent connector orientation.

The two face-recipe selections store their asset UUID, context UUID, recipe record, recipe family, and ordered recipe references. These UUIDs qualify a selection namespace in the current document; the 421-byte form contains no occurrence path, external-object envelope, or external-document identity. The selection record and recipe record indices remain part of the native identity because two selections can share a namespace and recipe family. The first neutral joint operand is the point/face pair and the second is the Hole/face pair, in that order. Both operands have no occurrence and no external-document qualifier. Their object identities are keyed by the selection asset UUID, context UUID, recipe identity, selection record index, and recipe record index.

The neutral connector frame for each pair copies the solved frame's complete rotation basis and replaces its translation with the paired construction position. Its reference record is the point or Hole construction record, its reference offset is the corresponding scope reference entry, and its matrix offset is the solved-frame matrix offset. The solved frame remains a separate native carrier. Alignment offsets and connector-frame translations convert from centimetres to millimetres; rotation bases and the alignment angle remain unchanged.

In a 705- or 772-byte scope, a component operand's frame reference names a paired construction carrier after the scope's paired header. The fixed span from the primary header through the paired header is 391 bytes. The carrier's row-major rigid transform starts at offset 48 and equals the operand-frame transform. Marked same-segment axis-record references start at offsets 192 and 208. The carrier's paired indexed header starts at offset 380 and has the same record index as its primary header. The two axis-record indices are distinct. Each axis record has one primary and one paired indexed header before the construction carrier. For each axis-record index `A`, the scope reference table contains exactly one consecutive `(A, A+3)` pair; `A+3` is the selector-record index.

An axial selector primary record has a 37-byte fixed prefix: its eleven-byte indexed header, eleven zero bytes, a marked same-segment reference to the selector-record index plus three, and u32 value 1. Two 36-code-unit LP-UTF16 GUIDs follow in selector-asset and selector-context order. They are followed by u32 values 2, 0, and 1, a nonzero marked same-segment occurrence reference, and u32 value 1. The external-object reference then stores byte 1, a nonzero u64 object reference, byte 1, a u32 segment, an LP-UTF16 asset GUID, one zero byte, a nonempty LP-UTF16 link name, and an optional-tail byte. Optional-tail value 0 ends the reference. Value 1 adds an LP-UTF16 property GUID and a nonempty LP-UTF16 version identity. The external asset GUID equals the selector asset GUID. The selector's paired indexed header has the same record index and follows the external-object reference. The occurrence-role record at selector-record index plus five starts with its eleven-byte indexed header, ten zero bytes, u32 value 1, and the u32 code-unit count of a 36-code-unit LP-UTF16 role GUID; the role GUID payload starts at offset 29.

The two selectors of one construction carrier have the same selector GUIDs, external-object reference, segment, external asset GUID, link name, optional property GUID, version identity, and role GUID. Their axis-specific occurrence references can differ. The common role GUID uniquely identifies the `Component Insert` scope whose component construction carries that role. The insertion's projected occurrence qualifies the operand, and the selectors' common external-object identity names its connector. A frame reference can instead equal one `JointOrigin` scope record index while its transform equals that origin's solved frame. This form selects the current document root: the neutral operand has no occurrence or external-document qualifier, and its object is the `JointOrigin` feature.

The 705- and 772-byte forms store no occurrence paths. Every other form stores a u32 count of two followed by two marked same-segment operand-path-locator references. The 399-byte `As-built` form stores the count at scope offset 47 and the references at offsets 51 and 62. The 627-, 637-, and 692-byte forms store the count at scope offset 362 and the references at offsets 366 and 377. The 633- and 732-byte forms store the count at offset 358 and the references at offsets 362 and 373. The class-430/262 744-byte form stores the count at offset 358 and the references at offsets 362 and 373; its 748-byte form uses offset 362 and offsets 366 and 377. Each reference resolves to exactly one locator envelope after the scope's paired indexed header. The locator record indices are distinct, and the two envelopes do not overlap. The first locator reference qualifies the first operand frame and the second locator reference qualifies the second operand frame. For `As-built`, each locator's nonzero reference at locator offset 21 is the operand construction reference and its transform at locator offset 33 is the operand frame.

An operand-path locator is a fixed 190-byte indexed record with record index `N`. Its eleven-byte header is followed by ten zero bytes, a nonzero marked same-segment reference at offset 21, one zero byte at offset 32, a row-major rigid 4×4 transform at offset 33, one zero byte at offset 161, a marked backlink to the assembly-operation scope at offset 162, a marked reference to record index `N+2` at offset 173, u32 value 2 at offset 184, and two zero bytes at offset 188. The occurrence-path record starts at locator offset 190 and has record index `N+1`. The next indexed record is its wrapper at record index `N+2`. The wrapper is exactly 37 bytes: its eleven-byte indexed header, ten zero bytes, byte value 1 at offset 21, u32 value 1 at offset 22, and a marked reference to path record `N+1` at offset 26. The next indexed record starts at wrapper offset 37.

A class-329 path begins with its indexed header, six zero bytes, a positive u32 path count, and exactly that many LP-UTF16 occurrence GUIDs in outermost-to-innermost order. It either ends at the wrapper after the occurrence run or continues with the identity suffix. A class-294 or class-299 path begins with its indexed header, six zero bytes, u32 value 1, three zero bytes, one LP-UTF16 occurrence GUID, two LP-UTF16 identity GUIDs, u64 value 2, two more LP-UTF16 identity GUIDs, u32 value 2, and a zero tail ending at the wrapper. The class-329 identity-qualified form and class-386 and class-390 paths append two LP-UTF16 identity GUIDs, u64 value 2, two more identity GUIDs, u32 value 2, and a zero tail ending at the wrapper after the occurrence run. The first occurrence GUID names the component occurrence containing the selected connector. Remaining occurrence GUIDs are the ordered descent path within that component. The four identity GUIDs are retained in source order. A class-294, class-299, or class-386 path whose root occurrence is not local uses its first identity GUID as the unresolved external-document identity. A scope whose two frames and two path or axial qualifiers are complete projects a fixed assembly joint. Occurrence and connector-frame translations and the three alignment offsets convert from centimetres to millimetres, while rotation bases and the alignment angle remain unchanged.

A Design circular-pattern scope stores one seed operand group with role `0x0000000400000000` or `0x0000000800000000`, one count owner at owner-local ordinal zero, one total-angle owner at owner-local ordinal one, and one axis construction. Role `0x0000000400000000` selects faces through entity-selection operands. Role `0x0000000800000000` selects bodies through whole-body recipe operands. The count is a positive integer including the seed. The total angle is a positive scalar in radians. Both owner values are duplicated from their parameter records and are independent of the owner-frame class and width. The 195-byte axis record stores a three-component origin in centimetres at offset 25 followed by a three-component displacement in centimetres at offset 49; the neutral unit axis direction is the normalized displacement. The displacement is nonzero and finite. Its marked selection reference identifies the persisted axis operand. A scope can contain this record and an earlier axis record. Exactly one 195-byte record supplies the solved axis when it is present. An earlier record supplies the axis only when the scope contains no 195-byte record and contains exactly one earlier record. Multiple 195-byte records or multiple earlier records without a 195-byte record leave the axis unresolved. The earlier axis record has a 129-byte two-wrapper form and a 118-byte compact form. Both begin after the indexed header and ten zero bytes. The two-wrapper form stores u32 one, a marked wrapper reference, u32 one, the marked seed-member reference, u32 one, a second marked wrapper reference, and the marked scope reference. The compact form stores u32 zero, u32 one, the marked seed-member reference, u32 one, one marked wrapper reference, and the marked scope reference. Both then store a repeated nonzero u32 around a finite f64, marked references to axis-record indexes plus two and one, and a second marked scope reference. Each wrapper stores a nonzero u64 lane, the asset and context GUIDs, a nested two-record chain, and a persistent u64 identity. Both wrappers in the expanded form carry the same persistent identity. A historical edge, coedge, curve, pcurve, vertex, or point identity supplies the unique analytic carrier axis in the feature's preceding history topology. If the scope has no preceding history state, the identity must yield the same model-space axis line in every state containing it. A face identity supplies the axis of its unique analytic surface carrier: a planar carrier uses its normal, and a cylindrical, conical, or toroidal carrier uses its axis; a surface identity supplies the axis of its unique analytic carrier, using a planar normal or a cylindrical, conical, or toroidal axis. An identity that does not resolve directly is a snapshot-local record ordinal only when exactly one archived record occupies that ordinal and the record is an edge; the record's positive revision identity selects the historical edge. A selected historical edge uses the axis of its line, circle, or ellipse carrier. An edge without such a carrier uses the common model-space axis line of its incident cylinder, cone, and torus surface carriers. Missing carrier bindings, multiple bindings for one selected face, or conflicting incident surface axes leave the axis unresolved. A whole-body recipe member selects the unique body in the preceding history topology identified by its persistent recipe. When no preceding history state exists, the recipe's Design id and selector join to one current persistent body link; if candidate faces are present, that body must occur in their body incidence. Without that link, a direct model-body selection resolves only when every reference maps to one common body, displayed bodies take precedence over cache bodies, and group members map to distinct bodies. Pattern face operands name result-face equivalence classes. A selected source face is the unique changed face in the preceding history topology whose procedural surface radius equals the one radius shared by every result face in that equivalence class. Missing radii, unequal result radii, or more than one matching changed face leave the source face unresolved.

A Design rectangular-pattern scope stores four parameter owners in owner-local order: U instance count, V instance count, U extent, and V extent. Each count is a positive integer including the seed. At least one count exceeds one. A direction with count one has zero extent; a direction with count greater than one has a nonzero signed seed-to-final-instance extent. Extents are stored in centimetres. Adjacent spacing is the extent divided by count minus one. Each value is the evaluated scalar duplicated by its parameter owner, independent of the owner-frame class and width.

When exactly one rectangular-pattern direction is active, the scope references the seed record, the four parameter owners, a metadata record, one generated-instance record per non-seed instance, and an aggregate record. The seed and generated-instance records contain a unique common-basis transform run whose translations divide the signed seed-to-final extent into equal intervals. Transform translations are stored in centimetres.

A Design Mirror scope stores one seed group with role `0x0000000400000000` or `0x0000000800000000` and one mirror-plane group with role `0x0000000500000000`. Ordinary forms store a count owner at owner-local ordinal zero; class-441/267 stores the count in the class-426 frame at scope-reference ordinal 3. Ordinary non-class-413 forms store a stitch-tolerance owner at owner-local ordinal one; class-391/261, class-413/262, class-440/258, and class-441/267 store the tolerance in their fixed scope tails. The count is exactly two, including the seed. The stitch tolerance is a positive length in centimetres. The plane group has one compact feature-reference, entity-selection, or face-recipe member. A compact feature-reference primary record refers to its record index plus three and is followed by paired, nested, and identity records at consecutive indexes; the identity record stores the referenced index after ten zero bytes. A plane reference names the record immediately before a WorkPlane scope. The WorkPlane frame's translation column is the mirror-plane origin in centimetres and its third basis column is the unit plane normal. A plane entity selection resolves its primary and auxiliary persistent identities against historical faces in the same ASM history namespace. One auxiliary-identity candidate selects the face. When the auxiliary identity has no historical candidate, one primary-identity candidate selects it. Multiple candidates do not select a plane. When the selected identity exists in the scope's preceding history state, that state supplies the plane. Otherwise every state containing the selected identity must supply the same plane. In each contributing state a loop identity defines the plane through its complete coedge-edge-curve chain: every edge has one axis-bearing analytic curve, and all curve axes are normal to the same plane. Other identity kinds use the selected face, which has exactly one surface carrier with exactly one planar definition. A face-recipe member uses the face-recipe envelope and resolves its preceding candidate faces in the scope's preceding history state. Every resolved candidate must have exactly one planar surface carrier, and all candidate planes must be coincident; the lowest stable face slot supplies the origin and normal. A missing carrier, a nonplanar candidate, or noncoincident candidate planes leaves the plane unresolved. The origin and unit normal are in model-space millimetres. A one-member role-`0x0000000800000000` seed group can use the same compact form to name a complete preceding feature scope. Role-`0x0000000400000000` seed members select faces through entity-selection or historical face-selection operands. Role-`0x0000000800000000` seed members select bodies through whole-body recipe operands.

A sketch rectangular-pattern direction's evaluated distance is the source distance scalar. A relation with a non-empty counted reference run stores adjacent spacing; a relation with an empty run stores the total seed-to-final-instance span. Count includes the seed. The neutral adjacent spacing is the scalar for the first form and `evaluated_distance / (count - 1)` for the second. A count-one direction has zero generated displacement.

A guide-surface `Sweep` has two role-`0x0000004100000000` groups: one carries the persistent profile selection and the other carries the scope's sketch-profile record. Its sole role-`0x0000000500000000` group identifies the path, and one role-`0x0000001100000000` group identifies the guide faces that constrain the section frame.

A `SurfaceRuled` scope stores nine zero bytes after its indexed header, followed by a u32 direction law at offset 20: `0 = tangent`, `1 = normal`, and `2 = explicit direction`. Three zero bytes follow. The byte at offset 27 selects the opposite incident face when it is one. Marked references at offsets 28 and 39 name the ruled-angle and ruled-distance parameter owners. A u32 corner law at offset 50 is `0 = rounded` or `1 = mitered`. Offset 54 starts a counted marked-reference list of boundary-edge groups, followed by a zero u32, a counted auxiliary-reference list, another zero u32, and a second counted boundary-edge-group list. The concatenated boundary lists preserve edge order. Each boundary record is a role-`0x0000000800000000` construction-operand group with one edge-recipe member. A 36-code-unit LP-UTF16 direction entity identity follows the last list; the all-zero UUID means absent. Three zero bytes separate it from the generic scope reference table. That table starts with the distance owner and angle owner and contains each boundary group immediately followed by its edge-recipe member. Tangent and normal forms have no direction entity; an explicit-direction form has one.

An absent auxiliary identity uses the same primary-candidate fallback as an auxiliary identity that has no historical candidate; the fallback remains subject to unique-candidate and exact-planar-carrier checks.

### 3.2 Materials

Visual and physical materials are distinct serialized channels.

Color attribute records include `rgb_color-st-attrib`, `truecolor-adesk-attrib`, `entatt_color-bt-attrib`, `color-adesk-attrib`, and `material-adesk-attrib`. The exact direct-color forms and their forward-chain precedence are specified in [`asm.md` §5.6](asm.md#56-attributes-on-the-topology-graph). `color-adesk-attrib` is an AutoCAD Color Index or inheritance selector. Its palette and display context are not in the F3D B-rep stream. `material-adesk-attrib` is a material-library lookup pair and carries no color value. Neither record independently defines a neutral color. Both remain source attributes. `Timestamp_attrib_def` carries an f64 Unix-epoch timestamp in microseconds for the original feature or body creation time. The ASM header `save_date` stores the file save-time string.

`.protein` assets are **nested ZIP archives** carrying per-asset `AssetData/*.bin` value streams plus XML schemas (`CommonSchema`, `GenericSchema`, `PhysMatSchema`, `PrismOpaqueSchema`, …). The package ZIP, `InstanceProperties.bin` / `DefinitionIteratorProperties.bin` page framing, logical-record header, schema inheritance, and property carriers are specified in [`protein.md`](protein.md).

The decoded Protein records form a document-local appearance catalog. Catalog entries do not imply a topology assignment; only Design body assignments, body-override records, and per-face assignment records bind an appearance to topology.

An `InstanceProperties` record opens with four u32-length-prefixed ASCII strings: the schema UID, the asset GUID, the asset name, and `AssetLibID`, the first member of the inheritance closure in serialization order. `AssetLibID` identifies the library the asset name resolves against: a GUID for a library Fusion ships, and a path for a user library. The asset name and `AssetLibID` together form the library-qualified preset reference. The value block that follows concatenates the remaining members of the closure in ASCII-lexicographic property-id order, and ends exactly at the end of the record. Properties marked `readonly` or `definitionIteratorData` do not serialize; `public="false"` and `metadata` do not suppress serialization. A `PropertyAlias` does not rename or reorder an inherited serialized property id. One further member does not serialize: a value block whose closure includes `TextureMap2dSchema` is one four-byte slot shorter than that closure, and its two serialized map-channel integers hold `1` and `0`. Of `texture_MapChannel`, `texture_MapChannel_ID_Advanced`, `texture_MapChannel_UVWSource_Advanced`, and `swatch`, only omitting `texture_MapChannel_ID_Advanced` or `texture_MapChannel` leaves every surviving member at its schema default. `interior_model` serializes in every Prism schema and carries the subtype ordinal: `0` PrismOpaque, `1` PrismMetal, `2` PrismLayered, `3` PrismTransparent, and `4` PrismWood.

Boolean stores one u8. Integer and Choice store one little-endian u32. Float stores one little-endian f64; a Float with a `unit` schema attribute prefixes that value with a u32 unit tag. Distance stores a u32 unit tag followed by one f64. A unit tag is `(quantity class << 12) | unit index`, with the unit index one-based, so the tag is 32 bits wide: length is class `0x2`, and the length tags are `0x200d` centimetre, `0x200e` millimetre, and `0x2016` inch. The tag names the unit of the stored f64 and is a property of the asset, not of the document: the document display length unit does not select it and does not rescale the value. A schema `unit` attribute states the unit the property is authored in and does not constrain the serialized tag: a property declared in inches serializes with the millimetre tag, and one record can mix tags across its own members. The schema attribute does, however, name the unit that its declared default value is expressed in, and a default that reaches the stream unconverted carries the tag of its declared unit. String and Uuid store u32-length-prefixed ASCII. Color stores four finite little-endian f64 values in normalized RGBA order and no marker byte, for connectable and non-connectable properties alike; every channel lies in the closed interval `[0, 1]`. Reference has no value bytes. TextureURI stores a u8 kind: kind `0` is followed by a little-endian u32 path count and that many u32-length-prefixed ASCII paths, which name library resources; kind `1` is followed by one u32-length-prefixed ASCII path, a plain file name. A property declared `allowmultiplevalues="true"` on any other carrier stores a little-endian u32 value count followed by that many carrier values.

A connectable property follows its value carrier with a connection carrier. Byte zero denotes no connection. A set connection is `u8 1`, `u8 1`, and a little-endian u32 count, followed by `count` u32-length-prefixed ASCII asset GUIDs. A Reference consists only of this connection carrier. Each GUID names a texture asset record in the same Protein stream.

`UnifiedBitmapSchema` and `BumpMapSchema` records are texture assets. Their TextureURI values name library resource paths; their bitmap-URN String values name library resources rather than embedded pixels. `TextureMap2dSchema` contributes inherited `texture_*` properties inline to each derived texture record and has no separate instance. The mapping properties carry map channel, UVW source, U/V offset, U/V scale, rotation in degrees, U/V repeat flags, and real-world offsets and scales. Neutral mapping rotation is radians and neutral real-world distances are millimetres. `bumpmap_Type` value zero denotes a height map and value one denotes a normal map; BumpMap also carries depth and normal scale.

Connected texture slots retain the source property id. Generic appearances connect color through `generic_diffuse` and bump through `generic_bump_map`. Prism appearances connect color or reflectance through `opaque_albedo` or `surface_albedo`, normal data through `surface_normal`, and cutout data through `surface_cutout`.

The neutral base-colour member is selected by shader role, not by property serialization order. A Boolean `common_Tint_toggle` value of true selects `common_Tint_color`; false or absence of the toggle excludes that member. Without an enabled tint, `GenericSchema` selects `generic_diffuse`; `MetalSchema` selects `metal_color`; `MetallicPaintSchema` selects `metallicpaint_base_color`; `PlasticVinylSchema` selects `plasticvinyl_color`; `PrismOpaqueSchema` selects `opaque_albedo`; `PrismMetalSchema` selects `metal_f0`; `PrismTransparentSchema` selects `transparent_color`; and `PrismLayeredSchema` selects `layered_diffuse`. Other `PrismCommonSchema` descendants select the inherited `surface_albedo`. A record with an enabled tint and no valid `common_Tint_color` has no neutral base colour.

A design BulkStream material assignment targets the nearest preceding component-prefixed entity ID. Its physical-material token joins to the `.protein` `PhysMatSchema` asset. Its visual appearance token is the complete token immediately before the appearance library id. A physical-material default-appearance clause stores associated GenericSchema and Prism appearance asset references.

Legacy per-face appearance assignments live in a Design record of class `D3937028-C20C-4E65-B010-94AD418A5C20`, whose type-table entry carries the add-in name `Body`. The record stores a u32 assignment count followed by that many variable-width entries. An entry stores, in order, an LP-UTF16 lower-case face GUID; four normalized f32 RGBA components whose alpha is `1`; bytes `01 01`; nine zero bytes; a u8 selector-name form; an LP-UTF16 complete visual token; LP-UTF16 appearance library id `BA5EE55E-9982-449B-9D66-9F036540E140`; an optional display-name member; one LP-UTF16 selector name; f32 `0`; and f32 `1`. The optional display-name member is either u32 `0` for absence or a nonempty LP-UTF16 string. Selector-name form `0` requires a `Prism-` token with a decimal suffix. Form `1` requires an alphanumeric `Prism` shader-family name. The face GUID also appears as the sole lower-case GUID string payload of the owning face's `NEUTRON_Material_attrib_def` `ATTRIB_CUSTOM` attribute in the B-rep stream. **Face appearance join:** face `NEUTRON_Material_attrib_def` GUID → Design BulkStream face assignment → complete visual token → appearance asset. A face assignment overrides the owning body's appearance for that face.

The first exact direct-color attribute selected by the B-rep chain supplies a body or face neutral color. Palette, inheritance, and material-library attributes do not enter this precedence. Without an exact direct color, a legacy face-assignment entry supplies its face's stored RGBA color. Without either, exactly one appearance binding whose asset has a base color supplies the target's neutral color. Conflicting assignment-entry colors are invalid. Multiple appearance bindings on the same target do not select a neutral color.

A paired-library face-scoped appearance envelope occurs within one primary-index record frame. Its first lower-case GUID is followed by an eight-byte field, eight zero bytes, u32 `1`, bytes `01 01`, and three zero bytes. Its second lower-case GUID is followed by twelve zero bytes, f32 `1`, bytes `01 01`, ten zero bytes, the complete visual token, library identifier `08861000-1D69-CF2A-C082-CBD98E7E5D7F`, u32 `0`, and library identifier `005E1000-55CE-AFB6-81A1-36E3EF077C5F`. The second lower-case GUID equals the string payload of the face's `NEUTRON_Material_attrib_def` attribute. The first lower-case GUID is not a B-rep operand. A paired-library tail with only one lower-case GUID is not face-scoped.

A `PhysMatSchema` value block follows the same schema-driven layout as every other instance record. Its constituent aspect assets are the connected-asset GUIDs of its `physmat_aspects` Reference. The physical-material join is `BulkStream` `PrismMaterial` token → `PhysMatSchema` asset → referenced Structural, Thermal, and Prism aspect assets.

**Design-entity join backbone:** body identity resolves across five tables via the numeric design-entity namespace:

```
BREP-qualified body selector (exact asm_body_key, otherwise body ordinal)
  ↔ design BulkStream BREP body map (body_selector → entity_suffix)
  ↔ material-assignment record entity-id suffix ("0_985" → 985)
  ↔ MetaStream entity ID listed by a type whose add-in name is `Body`
  ↔ ACT fusion_entity_id
```

The material-bearing bodies are the ACT PhysicalMaterial-channel entities minus the document/component roots.

The ID count in a MetaStream type-table entry is a count, not a flag with fixed `id1`/`id2` slots. BulkStream design body IDs use the numeric design-entity namespace and do not index the ACIS RecordTable.

Material records store a visual preset (`Prism-###`), visual token, protein phrase, physical-material token and category, and shader parameters. A complete Protein visual token identifies one appearance record within one document. Preset phrases of the forms `PrismMaterial-N`, `Prism-N`, and aspect-suffixed names identify library entries across documents; equal phrases denote equal library payloads. A material assignment first selects exactly one appearance by complete visual token. If no record has that token, a present visual-preset token selects exactly one appearance with the same preset name. Multiple token or preset matches are invalid. An assignment with no visual-preset token has no preset-name identity.

A preset reference is library-qualified: a library identifier paired with the name of an entry in that library. `BA5EE55E-9982-449B-9D66-9F036540E140` identifies the appearance library and `C1EEA57C-3F56-45FC-B8CB-A9EC46A9994C` the physical-material library; a user library is identified by its content path instead of a GUID. In a Design `BulkStream` presentation envelope and in a per-face assignment entry the library identifier immediately precedes the name it qualifies. In an `InstanceProperties` record the pair is the asset name and `AssetLibID`. The qualified name is either a preset token or the library entry's display name in the authoring session's locale. A material record carries its own schema, every parameter with its unit tag, and its full texture graph, so the appearance it denotes is determined without reading the library. The library resources that a `TextureURI` kind `0` path names are outside the container.

---
